# Hush — Technical Overview

## Architecture

Hush is a [Tauri v2](https://v2.tauri.app/) desktop app with a vanilla JavaScript frontend and Rust backend. The editor is built on [CodeMirror 6](https://codemirror.net/) — no framework.

```
Frontend (src/)                        Backend (src-tauri/src/)
───────────                            ────────────────────
main.js                  ←──IPC──→     lib.rs (app setup + run)
├── font-imports.js                    ├── atomic.rs            (tmp+rename writer)
├── style-application.js               ├── commands/
├── window-shortcuts.js                │   ├── files.rs
├── tooltips.js                        │   ├── images.rs
├── command-palette.js                 │   ├── settings.rs
├── command-palette-commands.js        │       └── defaults.rs

│   ├── index.js                       (themeList, getThemeById, getActiveTheme)
│   ├── _create-theme.js               (EditorView.theme + HighlightStyle wrapper)
```

Communication: `invoke` IPC for commands (settings, file CRUD) and `emit`/`listen` for events (settings updates, fullscreen toggle).

## Development Rules

**No code file may exceed 700 lines.** If a module grows past this limit, split it. The rule is enforced by `scripts/check-line-limits.sh` (run automatically as part of `npm run build`). A small allowlist lives in `.line-limit-exceptions` for files where splitting would do more harm than good (currently: `notebook/state.ts` and `notebook/drawing/engine/stroke.js`); every entry there must carry a one-line justification.

## Frontend

### Entry Points

- **`index.html`** — Main editor window. Loads `src/main.js`.
- **`settings.html`** — Settings window (separate Tauri WebviewWindow). Loads `src/settings/settings-window.js`.

Both are built by Vite as separate Rollup inputs.

`main.js` is pure orchestration — `init()` wires up the editor, sidebar, panes, command palette, and Tauri integration. Three companion modules carry pieces that used to bloat `main.js` past the line limit:

- **`font-imports.js`** — every `@fontsource` CSS import (~33 lines), pulled in for side effects only
- **`style-application.js`** — `applyActiveStyle(state)` (apply a named style's theme/font/colour overrides to the document), `applyFocusModeOpacity(state)` (publish the `--focus-mode-opacity` CSS var), and `handleOAuthCode(state, invoke, code)` (Google OAuth loopback callback)
- **`window-shortcuts.js`** — `installWindowShortcuts(state, windowCommands)`, the window-level keydown fallback that fires when CodeMirror hasn't already consumed the event (Cmd+P, Cmd+O, the always-allowed sidebar/outline/fullscreen toggles, plus the dispatch into `dispatchDomShortcut` for everything else)
- **`tooltips.js`** — global tooltip gate. `setTooltipsEnabled(bool)` reflects the `showTooltips` setting onto the DOM; `applyTooltip(el, label)` stashes the label on `data-tooltip` and only writes a native `title` attribute when tooltips are enabled. The notebook `h()` helper routes its `title:` option through `applyTooltip` automatically
- **`cmd-button.js`** — iOS Touch-mode floating pills. The **☰ palette pill is unconditional on iOS** (it's the only keyboard-free path to the command palette and the only fixed bottom-left affordance), so `applyButtonVisibility` mounts it any time `isIOSDevice()` is true regardless of the Touch mode toggle. The other three pills (⌘ hold-for-Cmd, 📋 paste, ↶ undo) still gate behind `settings.touchMode` (legacy `showCmdButton` alias preserved); they mount via `syncTouchPills(true)` and unmount via `syncTouchPills(false)` without disturbing the palette pill. On phone (`html.phone`) the ⌘ and ↶ pills are skipped — Cmd-modified flows and notebook undo aren't useful in that form factor — and CSS in `sidebar.css` re-anchors the remaining palette + paste pair to the **lower-right corner** (palette flush against `env(safe-area-inset-right)`, paste one slot to its left) instead of stacking them on the left. Holding the ⌘ pill sets `window.__hushCmdHeld = true`, adds `body.cmd-held`, and dispatches a synthetic `Meta` keydown so listeners that already gate on `e.metaKey` pick up the virtual modifier without per-call-site changes. Exports `isCmdHeld(event)` — call sites that previously checked `e.metaKey || e.ctrlKey` on pointer / drag events (text-drag, image-decorator, sortable-list drag-out, notebook drop / merge / link-click) consult this helper so the on-screen ⌘ counts as Cmd-held even though the touch event itself has `metaKey: false`. iPad detection lowered the touchpoints threshold to `> 0` (some WKWebView versions only expose 1 touch even though the device handles multi-touch)
- **`backup.js`** — *Backup App Data* command palette modal. Calls the Rust `backup_app_data` command with a user-chosen destination, listens for `backup-progress` events to fill a progress bar, then reports the final path + size. Versions / snapshots are excluded server-side
- **`multi-window.js`** — multi-window plumbing (desktop **and** iPad — see the Multiple Windows section for the iPad/Tauri-2.11 native path). Exports `setupMultiWindow(state)` (called from `main.js` after Tauri integration) plus `openInNewWindow(fileId, fileType)` for the command palette. `openInNewWindow` spawns a `WebviewWindow` seeded with `index.html#file=…&type=…`; on iOS it passes **only** `{ url }` (iPad scenes are sized/decorated by the system, so the desktop chrome options — size, decorations, overlay title bar, transparency — are skipped via `isIOSTauri()`). Handles four jobs: (1) reads the URL hash on load so a new window seeded with `index.html#file=…&type=…` opens that file instead of restoring `lastFileId`; (2) calls `register_window` to claim a sequential number from the Rust `WindowRegistry` and pushes the current file via `set_window_file` whenever `file-opened` / `notebook-open` fire; (3) listens for `windows-updated` so `state.windowList` (and the sidebar's per-window numeral badges) tracks reality; (4) listens for `cross-window-state-changed` and re-pulls settings or the file tree from disk when a sibling mutates them — re-pinning per-window keys (`lastFileId`, `lastNotebookId`, `lastProjectId`, `scrollPosition`, `typewriterMode`, `dryMode`) on the local copy so a sibling write can't blow away this window's session state in memory

### Fonts

Google Fonts are bundled locally via `@fontsource` npm packages. Loaded as a side-effect import (`import "./font-imports.js"`) from `main.js` so Vite resolves npm paths correctly without polluting `main.js` itself.

**Built-in fonts:** Source Sans Pro (default), Source Serif Pro, Libre Franklin, Libre Baskerville, Karla, Lora, EB Garamond, Inter, Fira Code, iA Writer Duo, iA Writer Mono, iA Writer Quattro, Helvetica (system). The iA Writer families are bundled from `src/assets/fonts/ia-writer-*` and registered via `src/styles/ia-writer-fonts.css`.

### State Management (`state/state.js`)

`AppState` is the single source of truth. It holds settings, file list, mode flags, and the editor reference. Uses a simple event emitter (`on`/`off`/`emit`) to notify UI of changes. The default `settings` shape is built by `createDefaultSettings()` in `state/state-defaults.js` (~170 fields mirroring the Rust `AppSettings` struct).

**Sibling state modules.** `state.js` stays a thin coordinator; the chunks of behaviour that don't need to live on `AppState` itself were lifted into siblings so the file stays under the line limit:

- **`state-modes.js`** — every `toggle*` / `start|stopRatchet` mode flip. Each export takes `state` as the first arg; the matching `AppState.toggleX()` methods are one-line delegations so external callers (command palette, sidebar, editor commands) keep working unchanged. These global toggles only reach the main editor — floating panes and stack columns have per-editor mode contexts (see `mode-context.js`) that the shortcut handlers in `commands.js` route to instead.
- **`state-snapshots.js`** — `trackKeystroke(state)` (the every-30-keystrokes auto-snapshot) and `createManualSnapshot(state)`. Doc-only; notebook snapshots ride the autosave path in `notebook-bridge.js`.
- **`state-naming.js`** — `deriveName`, `cursorOnFirstLine`, `updateTreeNodeNameByFileId`, `maybeRenameFromFirstLine`, `maybeRenameFileFromContent`. The "name follows first line" rule has four triggers in `editor.js` — cursor leaves line 1, editor blur, autosave when cursor isn't on line 1, and a 1.5 s-debounced timer fired from `docChanged` (so a user who titles a doc and never moves the cursor still sees the rename land). The pane-driven counterpart fires on `savePaneContent` so docs created via "New Document as Pane" get the same treatment. `renameTreeNode` in `state-tree.js` early-returns when the requested name resolves to the existing one (after `uniqueChildName` suffixing) so the debounced trigger doesn't churn. **Rename hot-path costs:** because the 1.5 s idle debounce fires the *full* rename pipeline after every typing pause while a first line is composed, that pipeline must stay cheap. Two spots used to violate this after the Local Desks refactor made `list_files` read every file's full content: `renameTreeNode` / `renameFile` re-fetched the entire library over IPC after each `rename_file` (now they patch the one cached entry's `name` in place, falling back to a full `list_files` only when the file isn't cached — the same in-place-patch pattern `saveCurrentFile` adopted for the autosave path), and `propagateWikilinkRename` `JSON.parse`d every notebook envelope in the library per rename (it now pretests the wikilink regex against the raw JSON string and only parses on a hit; names containing `"` or `\` — which would be JSON-escaped in the raw text — skip the shortcut and parse unconditionally). The remaining per-rename work (the on-disk `rename_file`, the `save_file_tree` reconcile, and one `files-changed` sidebar rebuild) is bounded and doesn't scale with library content size. Both `maybeRenameFromFirstLine` and `maybeRenameFileFromContent` skip auto-rename for tabbed documents (`---Tab---` markers present) that already carry an explicit name (not "Untitled") — the name was set intentionally (e.g. by a Project → Doc conversion) and shouldn't be overwritten by the first content line under the first tab marker.
- **`state-convert.js`** — `convertProjectToDoc(state, nodeId)` and `convertDocToProject(state, nodeId)`. Project → Doc loads each child doc's content, merges them with `---Tab name---` markers via `joinTabs()`, creates a single document replacing the project in the tree; non-doc children (notebooks, stacks, PDFs, useAsNote docs) are moved to a `"[Name] - Files"` folder inserted beside the new doc. Doc → Project parses tab markers via `parseTabs()`, creates a child document for each section, and replaces the original doc with a new project node. Both functions handle save-before-convert (project content / dirty doc) and old-file cleanup (delete + snapshot purge).
- **`state-split-combine.js`** — `splitDocAtHeadings(state, nodeId, { level, convertH1sToTabs, deleteOriginal })` and `combineDocsIntoDoc(state, fileIds, { name, deleteOriginals })`, exposed on `AppState` as `state.splitDocAtHeadings` / `state.combineDocsIntoDoc`. Split walks the doc's lines with a `^(#{1,6})\s+…$` heading matcher: each heading at the chosen `level` opens a new section (whose body keeps the heading line), and any content before the first matching heading becomes a leading section named after the original doc. Each section becomes a child document inside a new **project** dropped into the *same container* as the original (right after it). `convertH1sToTabs` rewrites every `# H1` line in the output to a `---H1---` Hush tab marker; `deleteOriginal` routes the source through `state.deleteTreeNode` (move-to-Trash, recoverable) after the project lands — `currentFileId` is nulled first so deleteTreeNode's "current file removed" fallback doesn't fire and drop the editor to the no-file empty pane. The heading helpers `listHeadingLevels(content)` (distinct levels present, ascending — drives the modal's level dropdown) and `splitSections(content, level, opts)` (the parse, reused by the modal for its live filename preview) are exported so the modal and the mutation share one source of truth. Combine resolves each fileId to a doc node + content (honouring the live editor buffer for the open doc), builds `joinTabs()` sections — a root section carrying `# <name>` followed by one `---Filename---` tab section per source in the user's chosen order — and creates the merged doc via `state.newFile(null, { initialName, initialContent })` (lands in the active desk's Inbox); `deleteOriginals` trashes each source afterwards. Child-doc names are de-duped via `uniqueChildName` and slashes stripped so they map cleanly to on-disk paths.

**`state.runtime` substructure.** Cross-module side-channel data that doesn't belong on `settings` (not persisted) and isn't first-class state (no event emissions) lives on `state.runtime`. Replaces the prior convention where any module could stamp a fresh `state._foo` field on AppState. Current fields:

- `columnResizeHandler` — set by `editor/modes.js`; called by sidebar / panel-resizer / pane-manager / main.js whenever sidebar or pane geometry changes so the editor column re-centers.
- `hasVisibleDocPane` — written by `pane-manager.js` when pane visibility changes; read by `editor/modes.js` to decide whether to leave the right gutter free.
- `visiblePaneCount` / `visiblePaneCentroid` — written by `pane-manager.js` (`_collectPaneMetrics`); drive the now-automatic make-space-for-panes: `editor/modes.js` shifts the column away from a single floating pane (centroid vs. viewport mid) and stays centred for 0 or 2+. Docked panes are excluded from this count.
- `dockedLeftWidth` / `dockedRightWidth` / `dockedTopHeight` / `dockedBottomHeight` — footprints of edge-docked panes on all four edges; `editor/modes.js` subtracts the horizontal pair from the column's usable width and applies the vertical pair as scroller `paddingTop` / extra `paddingBottom` so the editor shrinks beside / above / below a dock instead of sliding under it.
- `pendingScrollPosition` — set during `init()` from the persisted `scrollPosition` setting; consumed once by `main.js` after the editor mounts.
- `localSyncWriteFlag` — short-lived timestamp set when Hush writes to a Local Sync file; the watcher uses it to suppress its own echo within ~500 ms.
- `syncPulling` — true while a sync layer (Local Sync watcher, multi-window broadcast) is pushing remote content into the editor; suppresses `markDirty` so the pull doesn't re-trigger an upload.

Key events: `mode-changed`, `fullscreen-changed`, `files-changed`, `file-opened`, `settings-changed`, `theme-changed`, `style-changed`, `style-preview`, `style-preview-end`, `show-files-panel`, `hide-panel`, `show-styles-panel`, `show-ratchet-dropdown`, `show-versions-panel`, `export-current-file`, `notebook-open`, `notebook-unmount`, `notebook-autosave`, `doc-content-changed`, `notebook-shapes-changed`, `panes-changed`, `panes-hidden-changed`, `zen-focus-changed`.

**Pane Docking (`pane/pane-dock.js`).** Dragging a floating pane's title bar within ~50 px of the left, right, top, or bottom edge surfaces a blue drop zone (overlay built in `pane-drag.js` — four trapezoidal zones tile the container so neighbouring zones meet on a shared 45° diagonal at each corner with no overlap or gap; each zone div fills the overlay and uses an inline `clip-path: polygon(...)` whose outer edge sits flush with the container edge and inner edge insets by 50 px along the docked axis). Zones default to `opacity: 0` and only fade in when the cursor is within 200 px of their matching edge — `updateDockZoneProximity` toggles `.near` per-zone on every pointermove, measuring `distLeft` from `containerEl.left + getLeftInset()` (sidebar's right edge) rather than the raw window edge so a wide sidebar doesn't trip the left zone halfway across the screen. Releasing inside a lit zone calls `dockPane(pane, edge)`. The hit-test (`dropZoneAt`) is "distance to nearest container edge — pick the closest, return that edge if it's under 50 px," which naturally tracks the trapezoid boundaries (ties on the diagonal fall to `left` / `right` / `top` / `bottom` in that order). A docked pane is positioned *beside* the host surface rather than over it: `applyDockGeometry` pins it to the absolute container edge and defaults the user-controlled axis to half of the visible container dimension. Side docks (`left` / `right`) take the full container height minus any top/bottom-dock footprints (`vertDock.top` + `vertDock.bottom`, collected from `panes` via `collectDockFootprints`); top/bottom docks take the full container width minus any side-dock footprints — so the four edges carve mutually-exclusive territory and corners are owned by side docks. The opposite edge stays resizable (`pane-drag.js` allows only the one perpendicular handle while docked — `e` for left, `w` for right, `s` for top, `n` for bottom); resizing rewrites `dockUserSize` (width-axis or height-axis depending on the edge) and re-flexes every dock so perpendicular siblings shrink/grow in lockstep. `publishDockCssVars()` writes eight custom properties on `documentElement` — `--pane-dock-{left,right}-width` / `--pane-dock-{top,bottom}-height` (footprints) and `--pane-dock-{left,right,top,bottom}-edge` (absolute viewport-x/y of the dock's inner edge, sidebar already included for `left-edge`) — and dispatches a `pane-dock-changed` document event carrying `{leftWidth, rightWidth, topHeight, bottomHeight, leftEdge, rightEdge, topEdge, bottomEdge}`. Containers consume the vars purely in CSS: `#stack-container` and `#pdf-container` set `top` / `left: var(--pane-dock-left-edge)` / `right` / `bottom` so the whole surface (scrollbars included) shrinks on every docked edge; `#right-panel-overlay` reads all four vars (height becomes `auto` so top + bottom can both clamp at once); the notebook shelf adds the top/bottom heights into its `top`/`bottom` `calc()` so it slides away from any top/bottom dock the same way it slides away from a right dock. `editor/modes.js` subtracts `state.runtime.dockedLeft/RightWidth` from the column's usable width and applies `dockedTopHeight` as scroller `paddingTop` plus `dockedBottomHeight` as scroller `paddingBottom` (the 50vh last-line runway lives separately as `padding-bottom` on `.cm-content` in editor.css), and offsets the column resizers' `top`/`bottom` so their rails stop at the dock's inboard edge. `updateColumnResizers` also subscribes synchronously to `pane-dock-changed` — copies the event's `{leftWidth, rightWidth, topHeight, bottomHeight}` straight onto `state.runtime.docked*` and re-runs `applyColumnLayout` in the same frame. `dockPane`'s own call into `refreshPaneLayoutMetrics` goes through a lazy import to dodge the pane-dock ↔ pane-manager cycle, and the promise could resolve after the next paint — without the direct listener the editor would paint under a freshly-docked right pane until that lazy chain settled. `setupPaneResize`'s docked-pane branch coalesces `applyDockGeometry` + `reflowAllDockedPanes` + `notifyPaneDragMove` into a single rAF tick so a fast drag-resize doesn't stack 60+ synchronous layouts per second. The notebook listens for `pane-dock-changed` to mirror all four footprints into `DrawingState.docked{Left,Right}Width` / `docked{Top,Bottom}Height` (toolbar centring, visible-screen-centre, `focusShape` pan-target, `pocketRightInset`). `installDockReflowListeners()` (a window `resize` listener plus `ResizeObserver`s on the sidebar / outline / shelf / annotation-shelf — the sidebar width rides a CSS var, so a plain MutationObserver wouldn't fire) re-flexes every docked pane as chrome opens / closes / resizes. `publishDockCssVars` skips `display:none` panes so a dock owned by a different context doesn't leak its footprint into the active one, and `onContextChange` / `closePane` re-publish *and* re-flex remaining docks so switching desks or closing a dock fully resets the vars and lets perpendicular siblings reclaim freed cross-axis space. Dock state (`docked`, `dockEdge`, `dockUserSize`, `dockPrev`) round-trips through `pane-persistence.js`.

**Notebook state:** When a notebook is open, `currentNotebookFileId` is set and `currentFileId` / `currentProjectId` are null. The notebook canvas has its own `DrawingState` (in `src/notebook/state.ts`), managed by `notebook-bridge.js`. See [README-NOTEBOOK.md](README-NOTEBOOK.md) for details and [README-DRAWING.md](README-DRAWING.md) for the freehand drawing layer and its engine/shim architecture.

On Tauri, state loads from the Rust backend via `invoke("get_settings")`, `invoke("list_files")`, and `invoke("get_file_tree")`. In the browser (dev without Tauri), it falls back to localStorage.

**File tree:** `AppState.fileTree` holds a nested tree of documents, folders, projects, and images. Each node: `{ id, type, name, fileId?, children[], flagged?, useAsNote?, lockedStyleId?, zoteroAttKey?, bgColor? }` — all the optional fields are mirrored in the Rust `TreeNode` struct (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`) so serde round-trips preserve them. Persisted via `file_tree.json` (backend) or `localStorage` (web). Four special nodes are auto-created if missing and pinned in place — the tail order is **Images | PDFs | Archive | Trash**: **Inbox** (root position 0, accepts new docs/notebooks), **Images** (holds every `image` node dropped into a doc), **Archive** (`__archive__:<deskId>`, a plain `folder` special directly above Trash for inactive docs/projects — drag items in or out; its row exposes no menu actions, and it defaults collapsed via `seedNewArchivesCollapsed`, which seeds the freshly-created archive id into `settings.collapsedFolderIds` so an existing install's persisted collapse set picks it up exactly once), and **Trash** (last). New files created into the Inbox insert at the **top** (newest-first): docs pass a `prepend` flag through `insertNode`, while notebooks and stacks — inserted tree-side by the Rust `create_*` commands, which append — are hoisted to the front by `hoistInboxChildToTop` after the JS tree is re-read. Inserts elsewhere (folders, projects) keep the tail position so their ordering is untouched. A fifth special — **PDFs** (`__pdfs__:<deskId>`) — is created lazily when the first PDF is added via Zotero and hidden from the sidebar when empty. Pinned between Images and Archive (`pinSpecialsInList` re-establishes the whole tail order after every drag / create via a `pinBefore` helper). PDF items cannot be dragged out of the PDFs folder. PDF metadata (title, authors, year, citekey) lives in a local registry; the actual PDF binary data is a per-device cache re-downloaded from Zotero on demand.

**Desk identity.** The desk list is derived from the file tree — every top-level node is a `type: "desk"` node with a stable `id` + `createdAt`, mirrored into the `settings.desks` registry. Under the Local Desks plan each desk becomes a self-contained folder carrying a `.hushdesk` identity file (see LOCAL-DESKS-PLANNING.md); the Dropbox-era wire modules that used to publish/apply `.hushdesk` files are removed.

**Desks** (`state/state-desks.js`) sit one level above everything else and are part of the structural model — there's no on/off switch. The top-level tree always holds `type: "desk"` nodes; each desk carries its own per-desk Inbox / Images / PDFs / Archive / Trash addressed as `__inbox__:<deskId>`, `__images__:<deskId>`, `__pdfs__:<deskId>`, `__archive__:<deskId>`, `__trash__:<deskId>` (the canonical kind list is `SPECIAL_KINDS` in `state-desks.js`; `ensureDeskSpecials` creates any missing special and returns the ids it created so callers can react to a first-time appearance). Active-desk routing goes through `state.getInboxId()` / `getImagesId()` / `getArchiveId()` / `getTrashId()` (delegated to `state-desks.js#activeSpecialId`); `state.isSpecialNodeId` and `state.isInTrash` match across every desk's namespaced ids by prefix. `migrateLegacyTreeIfNeeded(state)` runs in `init()` after the file tree loads — installs predating the always-on rollout persist a flat tree with bare `__inbox__` / `__images__` / `__trash__` ids, so the helper calls `enableDesks(state, "Personal")` to wrap them under a single default desk; the same helper also runs `reconcileDesksWithStrayFolders` so an already-broken tree self-heals on next launch, plus `syncDesksRegistryWithTree` — a two-way reconcile between the tree's desk nodes and the `settings.desks` registry that prunes registry entries pointing at no tree desk, backfills entries for tree desks the registry doesn't know about (in the desk-folder world that's a first-class seam, not just drift: `load_forest` adopts any desk folder found on disk, which lands a desk node in the tree with no registry write), and re-points a dangling `activeDeskId` at the first desk. Without the backfill the registry stayed short forever: the all-desks view (tree-driven) showed every desk while the desk switcher (gated on `settings.desks.length >= 2`) never appeared in single-desk mode. Backfilled desks get a `desksMeta` stub that the portable-meta pull (see "Portable desk meta") overwrites from `.hushdesk` right after boot. The boot migrate/reconcile cluster lives in `state/state-desks-boot.js`, re-exported through `state-desks.js` so callers keep one import surface. `ensureDesksTreeSpecials` (called immediately after) folds top-level "stragglers" (non-desk, non-special nodes) into the active desk so a flat-shaped tree picks up after the wrap, but it explicitly skips stragglers whose name matches a known desk in `settings.desks` or any desk in the tree — those are unfinished desk absorptions and folding them under the wrong desk would nest content under the wrong path. `createDesk` / `renameDesk` / `deleteDesk` mutate the desk list in place. The very last desk can't be deleted. Per-desk metadata (active style, last-opened *tree* file) lives in `settings.desksMeta[deskId]` at runtime and is mirrored into the desk's own `.hushdesk` `meta` object (`sync/desk-meta.js` — write-through on change, disk-wins pull at boot/adopt/reconcile) so it travels with the desk; the active desk id (`settings.activeDeskId`) is intentionally local-only — each device picks its own. **Files-panel desk view.** `settings.deskDisplayMode` (`"single"` default, or `"all"`) chooses whether the panel shows only the active desk's contents behind the dropdown switcher, or every desk as a top-level folder row (`visibleTopLevel` / `isAllDesksMode` in `files-panel-rows.js`). In the all-desks view each desk renders with its own desk icon and the active desk wears an inverted name pill (current style's text colour as background, background colour as text); its row menu carries **Set as Active** / Rename / Delete. Content across every desk stays visible and editable (you can drag files between desks), but the *active* desk alone owns theme, where Cmd+N lands, and which Local Folders show. `reorderDesks(state, orderedIds)` (`state-desks-ops.js`) reorders both the tree's desk nodes and the `settings.desks` registry in lockstep — driven by drag handles in the switcher popover and by dragging the desk rows in the all-desks view. **Per-desk last Local Folder file.** Local Folder files live on disk rather than in the tree, so their last-opened slot can't ride `desksMeta`; a local-only `settings.deskLastLocalSync[deskId]` (plus the per-window `settings.lastLocalSync`) holds it instead. The local slot and the synced tree slot are kept mutually exclusive — each open path clears the other — so the most-recent open always wins on restore (boot via `init()`, desk switch via `openLastFileForDesk`). All three of these (`deskDisplayMode`, `deskLastLocalSync`, `lastLocalSync`) are declared on the Rust `AppSettings` struct (`desk_display_mode` / `desk_last_local_sync` / `last_local_sync`) — the typed struct silently drops any field it doesn't declare on the `save_settings` round-trip, so an undeclared setting resets to its default every launch. Tree traversal helpers in `state/tree-helpers.js` know about the desk node type via prefix-matching the special-id checks (`pinSpecialsInList`, `enforceSpecialPositions`, `sanitizeDescendants`, `normalizeProjectChildren`).

**Per-desk active style.** The styles list (`settings.styles`) is shared across every desk, but the user's *selected* style is stored per-desk on `settings.desksMeta[<deskId>].globalStyleId`. `state.getDeskGlobalStyleId()` / `state.setDeskGlobalStyleId(id)` (thin wrappers over `state-desks.js`) own reads and writes; the legacy top-level `settings.globalStyleId` is preserved as a per-device fallback so installs that haven't been touched yet keep working. `enableDesks` seeds the default desk's meta with the user's existing top-level value on the first migration, while `createDesk` seeds new desks with `globalStyleId: null` so a fresh desk starts on Default rather than inheriting the previous selection. The style picker (`sidebar/styles-panel.js`), the `Mod+1..5` shortcut path (`editor/commands.js`), and the style-delete cascade all write through `setDeskGlobalStyleId`. `main.js` repaints on `active-desk-changed` (locked-style files still win via the `file-opened` branch).

**Per-desk last-opened file.** Each desk remembers the doc/notebook last viewed inside it, so switching desks lands the editor on that file instead of leaving it parked on the previous desk's content. `settings.desksMeta[<deskId>].lastFileId` + `lastFileType` carry the slot; `state.recordActiveDeskLastFile(fileId, type)` writes through (`state-files.js`'s `openFile`, `openNotebook`, and the `openImmediately` branch of `newFile` all call it on every "this is the active file" event). On `active-desk-changed`, `main.js` invokes `openLastFileForDesk(state, deskId)` from `state-desks-ops.js`, which resolves with priority: (1) the saved per-desk last file if it still lives in that desk's subtree; (2) the first doc/notebook in the desk's Inbox; (3) a fresh empty doc in the Inbox so the editor never shows a stale view.

**Desk lifecycle** (`state/state-desks-ops.js`). The command palette exposes **New desk** (creates a fresh "Untitled desk" and switches to it), **Convert folder to desk** (promotes a folder/project that's a direct child of a desk into its own top-level desk; inner Inbox/Trash/Images subfolders matched by name become per-desk specials), and **Collapse desk into folder** (demotes a desk back to a regular folder placed inside another desk). The collapse path refuses while the source desk's Inbox or Trash carry any items — the operation drops those specials on the way through, so silently merging non-empty contents would be a data-loss surprise. The blocked path throws an error tagged `code: "desk-collapse-blocked"`; the picker surfaces the message via `window.alert` so the user knows what to clear before retrying. Images survive the collapse as a plain folder when non-empty so existing image refs still resolve. The same module also exports `openLastFileForDesk(state, deskId)` — `main.js` calls it on `active-desk-changed` so switching desks drives the editor to that desk's most-recent file. Resolution order: the desk's saved Local Folder file (`deskLastLocalSync`, reopened via `openLocalEntry`), then its saved tree last-file, then its Inbox first-child, then — when the desk is empty — the **no-file empty state** (`state.clearActiveFile()`, surfaced by `editor/empty-state.js` through the `empty-mode` surface toggle, with New Doc / Notebook / Stack buttons) rather than spawning a throwaway doc. The same empty state is what every delete path drops to now (the open file is deleted, or there's nothing to restore) instead of jumping to an arbitrary file in another desk. `reorderDesks(state, orderedIds)` also lives in this module.

**Desk switcher** (`sidebar/desk-switcher.js`) is a header rendered above the create-buttons row in the files panel. It auto-hides while there's only one desk so single-desk sessions don't carry the chrome — and also in the all-desks panel view (`deskDisplayMode === "all"`), where the desk rows themselves replace it. Clicking the header opens a popover with one row per desk (active marker, click to switch), inline rename, delete, a per-row **drag handle** that reorders desks via `state.reorderDesks` on pointer-up, and an "Add desk" entry that creates a new desk and switches to it. (In the all-desks view, where the switcher is hidden, desk creation moves to the footer **Add** popup's **New Desk** entry.) `render()` updates the header in place rather than rewriting `_container.innerHTML`, so a sync apply firing `desks-changed` while the user is mid-rename doesn't blow away the input; the popover body refresh is also gated on "no rename input present". The popover click handler short-circuits while a rename input is open — the input lives inside the row's `<button data-action="pick">` (it replaces the name span, which is a child of that button), and browsers synthesize a click on a `<button>` when Space is pressed on a child input; without the short-circuit that synthesized click bubbles up and gets routed to the "pick" branch, closing the popover mid-typing. The legacy `useDesks` boolean still ships in `state-defaults.js` and `AppSettings`, but only as a deprecated marker — the JS layer ignores its value and treats desks as structural; the field is retained so settings.json files written by older builds parse cleanly.

**Switch Desks command** (command id `desk-switch`, shortcut `shortcutSwitchDesks` defaulting to `Ctrl+Shift+D`). A keyboard-first counterpart to the sidebar popover: runs *inside* the command palette as a desk picker (`enterDeskPicker` in `command-palette-pickers.js`), so it carries the palette's look and arrow-key / return navigation. The command entry is `keepOpen` and swaps the palette's list for one row per desk (the active one flagged `(current)`); the shortcut routes through `openDeskPalette` in `command-palette.js`, which opens the palette straight into the desk picker. Picking a desk switches via `state.setActiveDesk(id)`. The command palette entry and the shortcut both hide / no-op when fewer than two desks exist. The shortcut sits in the `alwaysAllowedKeys` list in `window-shortcuts.js` so it fires regardless of focus (sidebar input, notebook canvas, etc.). The default chord uses literal `Ctrl` rather than `Mod` so it doesn't collide with `Mod+Shift+D` (Select previous instance) on macOS.

**Project state:** When a project is selected (`currentProjectId`), the editor shows all child *documents* joined by separator markers. Notebook and stack children of a project are intentionally excluded from the joined buffer — they ride along as supplementary material in the sidebar instead (sorted below docs at 50 % opacity). `openProject()` loads and concatenates document content; `saveProjectContent()` splits on separators and saves each part back. `state.projectDocIds` only carries doc fileIds, so the split-and-save pipeline never sees a notebook or stack envelope. Projects can also be added to stacks as columns — they mount their joined buffer with separator widgets; converting a project back to a folder auto-removes it from any active stack.

Tree traversal utilities live in `state/tree-helpers.js` (`findNode`, `findNodeByFileId`, `findParentOfNode`, `removeNode`, `collectDocumentIds`, `insertAfter`, etc.).

**Same-name guard.** `tree-helpers.js#uniqueChildName(parent, baseName, type, excludeId)` returns a name that doesn't collide with any same-type sibling — `Foo`, then `Foo (2)`, `Foo (3)`, etc. on collision. It's wired into every create + rename path: `state.newFile`, `state.createNotebook`, `state-tree.js#createTreeNode` (folder + project), and `state-tree.js#renameTreeNode`. Two same-type children of a parent can't share a name (names map to on-disk paths); rename also uses `excludeId` so the renaming node doesn't see itself as a conflict. New folders / projects default to the active desk as parent so they don't drop at the top level next to the desks (where `visibleTopLevel` would never render them); `createTreeNode` runs `enforceSpecialPositions` after the Tauri create so the new entry sits above the desk's Inbox/Images/Trash tail rather than below it.

### Theme Colors (`theme-colors.js`)

Extracted from `main.js`. Contains `fontFallbacks` map, `themeBackgrounds` and `themeForegrounds` color tables, `hexLuminance()`, `updatePrivateBoxColor(state, overrideBg?, overrideFg?)`, and `applyFontFamily()`. `updatePrivateBoxColor` derives `--private-box`, `--theme-bg`, and panel colours from the active background, and sets `--fg` to the resolved *text* colour (the style's `fg` override, else the theme's foreground from `themeForegrounds`, else the legacy luminance grey) so the UI chrome matches the editor text rather than a generic light/dark grey. **iOS force-repaint:** production WKWebView builds leave `background: var(--theme-bg)` chrome stale after a focus / visibility transition (it falls back to the neutral `--bg` grey), so on `html.ios` the function also writes an inline `backgroundColor` onto every surface in the `THEME_BG_SURFACES` selector (`#panel-overlay`, `.sidebar-grip`, `#right-panel-overlay`, `#comments-panel`, `.pdf-shelf-view`) plus `html`/`body`; `main.js`'s `visibilitychange → visible` handler calls it once synchronously and once on the next `requestAnimationFrame` so the nudge lands after WKWebView finishes restoring its layers.

### Editor (`editor/editor.js`, `editor/modes.js`)

The CodeMirror 6 instance is configured with:

- **Markdown language** with inline syntax highlighting (headings get scaled font sizes, generic syntax characters dimmed to 40% opacity; `%%` comment markers and `==` highlight markers carry their own tags pinned to 20% opacity so the delimiters fade further than the content they wrap)
- **Lezer markdown extensions** — the `markdown()` call (in both `editor.js` and `createBaseExtensions`) passes `extensions: [Strikethrough, Table, CommentExtension, HighlightExtension]`: `Strikethrough` and `Table` (GFM pipe-table parsing) from `@lezer/markdown`, plus **custom inline parsers** for `%%comments%%` and `==highlighted text==`
- **Heading indent plugin** — hides `#` markers entirely by default via replace decorations; when the cursor enters the heading line the markers are revealed inline so the user can edit them. This replaces the previous behavior of pulling the markers into the left margin, which was being cropped when the editor column was narrow or inside a floating pane
- **Heading normalization** — optional setting to remove scaled heading sizes
- **Theme/highlight compartments** for live reconfiguration
- **Ratchet keymap** (`Prec.highest`) intercepting all deletion/navigation/selection keys when ratchet mode is active
- **Transaction filter** blocking deletions and non-end insertions in ratchet mode
- **Mouse filter** blocking mousedown in ratchet mode

**Plugins loaded:** private mode, D.R.Y. highlighting, footnotes, focus mode, callouts, project view (separators), flag highlighting, link decorator, heading indent, sticky headers, encourage typing, properties (metadata frontmatter), table renderer (`plugins/table-renderer.js` — renders a GFM pipe table as a real `<table>` block widget while the cursor is outside it, raw source when a cursor/selection overlaps; the decoration set is a `StateField` provided via `EditorView.decorations.from` because CodeMirror rejects block decorations sourced from view plugins; cells render basic inline formatting — code, links, bold/italic, `~~strike~~`, `==highlight==`, `&#124;` → literal pipe — through an escape-first formatter; CSS under `.cm-md-table*` in `styles/editor.css`).

**Sibling modules.** Two CodeMirror plugins were extracted to keep `editor.js` under the line limit:

- **`editor/heading-indent.js`** — `headingIndentPlugin` (collapses `#` markers when the cursor isn't on the heading line) plus the hang-indent helpers for wrapped list lines (`measureListMarkerPx`, `listIndentLineDeco`, `blockquoteLineDeco`).
- **`editor/comment-plugins.js`** — `createMultiLineCommentPlugin` (multi-line `%%…%%` blocks) and `createCommentAfterPlugin` (the `---%` end-of-document dim marker, scoped per file in project view — the dim closes at the next `---hush-separator---` line so a `---%` in one project doc no longer bleeds across the boundary into the next).

Both are re-exported from `editor.js` so external imports keep working.

**Folding (`editor/folding.js`, `editor/fold-arrow.js`).** Built on `@codemirror/language`'s `codeFolding` state field + `foldEffect` / `unfoldEffect`. `folding.js` exposes `buildFoldingExtension()` (the field plus a `preparePlaceholder` / `placeholderDOM` pair that renders the unwrap pill — header folds report a line count, selection folds a `countWords` word count; `placeholderDOM`'s `onclick` arg already dispatches the unfold), heading-range helpers (`foldRangeForHeading` collapses to the next heading of the same-or-higher level; `foldBodyForHeading` collapses only to the next heading of *any* level so "fold all sections" keeps the outline visible — both fold from the heading line's `to` so the heading itself survives), and the command helpers (`foldCurrentSection`, `unfoldCurrentSection`, `foldSelection`, `foldAllSections`, `unfoldAllSections`, `foldAllAtLevel`). `applyFolds(view, candidates)` is the shared dispatcher: it drops candidates that would overlap an existing or earlier fold (overlapping replace decorations crash the view layer) and, because CodeMirror auto-clears a fold whose interior holds the caret, parks a cursor at the fold's start when the selection would otherwise be stranded inside a freshly folded range. `fold-arrow.js` is a `ViewPlugin` mounted via `createBaseExtensions`, so every surface (main, panes, stack columns, Zen) gets its own arrow element inside `scrollDOM`; it tracks the hovered line via `posAtCoords` and positions the arrow in the left-margin gutter the same way `line-indicator.js` does (content-left + `contentDOM` padding, pulled left with a CSS `translateX`). It shows beside a non-empty selection's first line, or beside a hovered heading whose section isn't already folded. The command-palette entries (doc context) resolve the active editor through `getActiveModeContext(state)?.view || state.editor.view`. Folds are session-only — not persisted across reloads.

**`createBaseExtensions(state, onChange)`** builds the shared extension set (theme, syntax highlighting, shortcuts, all plugins) used by both the main editor and floating pane editors. Returns compartment handles for theme, highlight, shortcut, and editable reconfiguration.

**`editor/modes.js`** contains mode application (`applyModes`, `applyFullscreen`), column width/resizer management (`updateColumnResizers`), and ratchet timer display (`updateRatchetTimer` — the countdown wakes once per second via a second-aligned `setTimeout` rather than a 60 fps `requestAnimationFrame`, since the `mm:ss` label only changes per second). `applyModes` toggles CSS classes on `#app`: `ratchet-active`, `private-mode`, `typewriter-mode`, and `dummy-mode` (when private mode + dummy text is active).

Column width is managed by dynamically setting `paddingLeft`/`paddingRight` on `.cm-scroller`. Draggable resizer elements sit 10px outside the column edges. When the sidebar panel is open in inset mode, the column re-centers within remaining space.

### Command Palette (`command-palette.js`, `command-palette-commands.js`, `command-palette-desk-commands.js`, `command-palette-helpers.js`, `command-palette-pickers.js`)

Centered overlay activated by `Cmd+P` (hardcoded in the fixed keymap). Lists all major commands with icons, labels, and keyboard shortcut keycaps. Supports arrow-key navigation, Enter to execute, Escape to dismiss, and text filtering.

Commands are context-sensitive: **shared** commands (New document, New notebook, **New document as pane**, **New notebook as pane**, Files, **Edit Styles**, **Use Style: <name>** (one row per saved style plus Default), Versions, Export, Toggle fullscreen, Settings, etc.) always appear; **doc-only** commands (Ratchet, Private mode, Typewriter, Show repeats, Highlight sentence, Outline view, **Add notebook as gutter**, **New notebook as gutter**, **Create Google Doc from current**, **Split Headings to Files**, the **Fold** family — **Fold current section** / **Unfold current section** / **Fold selection** / **Fold all sections** / **Unfold all sections** / **Fold all H1** / **Fold all H2** / **Fold all H3** — and the **Properties** trio — **View properties** / **Hide properties** (mutually exclusive on `settings.propertiesVisible`) / **Add property**) are hidden when a notebook is open; **notebook-only** commands (Open shelf, Start brainstorm) appear only in notebook mode. The retired Styles, Versions, and Export sidebar buttons all route through the palette now — Versions opens `sidebar/versions-modal.js`, Edit Styles opens `sidebar/style-editor-modal.js`, and Export dispatches to `stack-export.js`, `notebook-export-modal.js`, or `doc-export-modal.js` (in that priority order) via the `export-current-file` event. The per-style **Use Style** rows go through `buildUseStyleCommands(state)`, which mirrors the click-a-row behaviour the old sidebar styles list had: if the current doc carries a locked style, the switch also updates the lock; otherwise the choice flows through the per-desk style.

The "as pane" variants call `state.newFile(null, { openImmediately: false })` / `state.createNotebook(name, null, { openImmediately: false })` — both methods accept an option to skip the main-view switch and return `{ fileId, name }` so the palette can hand them to `createPane()`. Every "new notebook" entry (sidebar, palette, row-menu, as-gutter) routes through a shared `promptNewNotebookName(onConfirm)` helper that surfaces a small input modal before the create call — there's no auto-named "New Notebook" placeholder dropped into the tree anymore. The new file lands in Inbox like any other; it just opens as a floating reference instead of taking over the main editor.

**Gutter-as-one-shot commands.** `Add notebook as gutter` opens a fuzzy picker over notebooks in the active desk; `New notebook as gutter` prompts for a filename, creates the notebook, then in both cases `openNotebookAsGutter(state, fileId, name)` (in `command-palette-pickers.js`) calls `createPane(...)` → awaits a microtask so `focusPane()`'s effects settle → calls `useActivePaneAsGutter()`. Both entries hide via `docContextHasGutterAlready(state)` (walks the live `panes` Map for any pane with `gutter: true` matching `"doc:" + currentFileId`) so a doc can't carry two gutters.

**Command definitions extracted.** `command-palette-commands.js` holds the icon imports, the `icons` object, `buildCommands(state)` (the full command definitions array), and the mode-turnoff builders (`buildActiveModeTurnoffs`, `docModeTurnoffs`). `command-palette-pickers.js` holds every `enter*Picker` plus the gutter / prompt helpers; `command-palette-helpers.js` keeps the pure utility functions (`collectFileLeaves`, `activeDeskSubtree`, `formatShortcutKeys`, `canUseAsNote`, `isDesktopTauri`). The main `command-palette.js` retains only the UI: overlay lifecycle, keyboard/mouse handling, and list rendering. The split keeps every file under the 700-line repo cap.

Doc, notebook, project, and trash rows reuse the inline `typeIcons` glyphs exported from `sidebar/files-panel-shared.js` (filled rectangle, ruled rectangle, triangle, lid + bin) so the palette and the file tree show the exact same visual language. Mode-toggle and action rows still use the hand-drawn 24-unit SVGs from `src/sidebar/sidebar_icons/`. Each entry in the `icons` map is a fully-formed `<svg>…</svg>` string (24-unit for the sidebar imports, 16-unit for the `typeIcons` glyphs); `renderList` just drops it into the icon slot rather than re-wrapping with a per-row viewBox. All glyphs use `currentColor` for stroke/fill so they pick up the palette's `--fg`.

**File picker (`enterFilePicker`).** `collectFileLeaves` walks the *active desk's subtree* (via `activeDeskSubtree`) and surfaces document, notebook, and user-created project nodes — sibling desks are intentionally invisible so `Cmd+O` and **Send selected** don't pull a target from a different desk. It skips `__images__` / `__trash__` outright and skips the `__inbox__` *project entry* while still recursing into its children (Inbox is internally typed as a project but functions as a folder, so it shouldn't appear as a clickable target — only the docs inside it should). `enterFilePicker` accepts an `{ includeProjects }` opt-in flag because only the main editor can host a project's joined view — `createPane` doesn't know how to render the multi-doc separator buffer. The Cmd+P "Open document, notebook, or project" entry and the `Cmd+O` shortcut both pass `includeProjects: true` and route projects to `state.openProject(node.id)`; the "Open as pane…" entry and `Cmd+Shift+O` filter projects out so the resulting list only carries pane-compatible types.

**Keyboard nav vs. hover.** Arrow keys set a module-level `keyboardNav` flag and re-render the list. The per-row `pointerenter` handler early-returns while `keyboardNav` is true, so the `pointerenter` that fires on the row that re-renders under a stationary cursor doesn't yank the highlight back to the mouse position. An overlay-level `mousemove` listener clears the flag the moment the user actually moves the mouse again — `mousemove` is a mouse-only event (touch never dispatches it) so iPad touch scrolling doesn't trip the reset.

When toggle modes are active (ratchet, private, typewriter, D.R.Y., focus), "Turn off X" entries are prepended at the top of the list (doc mode only). Mouse hover selection is suppressed while keyboard-navigating to prevent conflicts.

**Touch-friendly hover handling.** Row hover activation keys off `pointerenter` filtered to `pointerType === "mouse"` — synthetic mouse events fire on iOS for every row your finger crosses during a touch scroll, and toggling an `.active` class across every row in response was causing visible scroll stutter. The keyboard-nav-clearing reset moved from an overlay-wide `pointermove` listener (which fired every touch frame) onto the per-row `pointerenter` so there is no per-frame work during a scroll. `.cmd-palette-list` carries `overscroll-behavior: contain` (so flicks at the end don't yank the underlying editor) and `will-change: scroll-position` (to nudge WKWebView onto a fast scrolling path).

**Backup App Data.** A shared command (`backup`) opens a modal from `src/backup.js` that calls the `backup_app_data` Tauri command with a destination chosen via `tauri-plugin-dialog::save`, listens for `backup-progress` events to drive a progress bar, and reports the final path + size on completion. Versions / snapshots are excluded server-side.

**Show Shortcuts.** A shared command (`show-shortcuts`, `keyboard` icon) opens `src/ui/shortcuts-modal.js` → `openShortcutsModal(state)`. The modal reads the active main-view filetype (`currentNotebookFileId` → notebook, `currentStackFileId` → stack, `currentPdfFileId` → pdf, else project/document) and builds an ordered section list: filetype-specific, Editor, Panes & Panels, App-wide. The **Editor** section is split into **Formatting / Selection / Navigation / Editing** subsections (the latter three partition the "Editing" settings category by key). Every section after the first carries a light top border. Shortcut keys + labels come from `shortcutCategories` (the same source `settings-tabs-shortcuts.js` exposes to Settings > Shortcuts) so the modal never drifts from the settings UI — `General` is split into pane toggles (`PANE_KEYS`), doc-surface modes (`DOC_MODE_KEYS`), and the app-wide remainder, and a few hard-wired bindings with no settings field (`⌘P`, `⌘,`, `⌘O`, `⌘⇧O`, PDF zoom) ride along as non-editable rows. Keys render via the shared `renderShortcutKeys` helper as graphic keycaps (CSS in `styles/shortcuts-modal.css`, a fixed two-column grid so full descriptions stay readable). The filetype grouping (`PANE_SHORTCUT_KEYS`, `DOC_MODE_SHORTCUT_KEYS`, `filetypeShortcutKeys(filetype)`, `SHORTCUT_FILETYPES`) lives in `settings-tabs-shortcuts.js` so it's shared with the **Shortcuts settings tab**, which carries a matching **filetype filter dropdown** beside its search box — picking a filetype narrows the editable list to the shortcuts that surface on that surface (`renderShortcutsTab(settings, query, filetype)`; conflicts stay pinned regardless of filter). Hovering an editable row fades the keys and rises an **Edit** button; clicking it deep-links to Settings via `openSettingsWindow(state, { tab: "shortcuts", shortcutSearch: label })`. That new `shortcutSearch` option threads through `tabHash` (encoded as `#shortcuts?q=…` for a fresh window), the `settings-goto-tab` cross-window event (already-open window), and the iOS-modal `setActiveTab(tab, subTab, shortcutSearch)` path — all of which seed `shortcutSearchQuery` in `settings-window.js` so the Shortcuts tab opens filtered to that one row.

### Sidebar (`sidebar/sidebar.js`)

The legacy 50 px icon column is gone — `#panel-overlay` is the sidebar itself, anchored at `left: 0`. It hosts a single Files panel, a header carrying the desk switcher, and a footer with two icon-only buttons (Add and Settings). Styles, Versions, and Export reach users through the command palette / modals instead. A full-height **grip** on the panel's right edge owns open / close and resize; the floating circular toggle that earlier iterations carried is gone. Cmd+\\ toggles the panel too. The panel is responsive: above 700 px viewport it sits in inset mode (pushing the editor column over); at 700 px or narrower it overlays as a modal.

**Grip (`.sidebar-grip`).** A 24 px (`--sidebar-grip-width`) full-height strip living *inside* `#panel-overlay` on its right edge. Two children with disjoint roles:

- **`.sidebar-grip-toggle`** — the wide left portion. A real `<button>` that emits `toggle-left-panel` on click. The chevron glyph (`›` when closed, `‹` when open) sits at 0.6 opacity by default and bumps to 1.0 on hover; `body.typing-fade` fades it to 0 with a 1 s ramp while the user is typing in a doc, and cursor / pointer activity clears the class so the arrow rises back (same behaviour the legacy floating toggle had). Hover paints a faint background-tint pill via a `::before` pseudo-element capped at `height: 100px; border-radius: 5px` and vertically centred — the tint never fills the whole toggle height. The pill works in both open and closed states. No `title` / `aria-label` attributes — the chevron-on-hover affordance is the whole UI.
- **`.sidebar-grip-resize`** — a narrow 6 px strip flush against the panel's outer edge. `cursor: col-resize`. Drag-to-resize is wired by `attachGripResize(state, resizeEl, panelOverlay)` in `sidebar/panel-resizer.js`. A 1 px `::after` rail at `right: 0` aligns flush with the panel's `border-right`, so hover deepens that existing hairline rather than painting a second one a few pixels inside. The strip is `display: none` when the panel is collapsed — there is no body width to drag.

The pointer-driven cursor changes (`cursor: pointer` on the toggle, `cursor: col-resize` on the resize strip) use `#panel-overlay`-prefixed selectors so they win the cascade over the panel-wide `#panel-overlay * { cursor: crosshair }` rule.

**Closed state.** `#panel-overlay.hidden` collapses the width to `--sidebar-grip-width` and hides the body-stack via `display: none`. The hidden rule asserts `display: flex !important` to win against the global `utility.css` `.hidden { display: none !important; }`. Border-right is set to `none` when collapsed so the leftover surface reads as empty until the chevron rises.

**Footer (`.panel-overlay-footer`).** No top border. Two icon-only buttons:

- **Add (+)** — opens a small popover (`sidebar/add-popup.js`) anchored above the button with the four create actions: New Doc, New Notebook, New Folder, New Project. Closes on click outside, Esc, or selection. The popover replaces the four-icon row that used to live at the top of the files panel.
- **Settings** — calls `openSettingsWindow(state)`. Settings remains reachable from the command palette too.

Both buttons hide their `.panel-footer-label` text by default (`max-width: 0; opacity: 0`) and reveal it on hover via a `max-width` transition.

**Recent Files panel (`sidebar/recent-files-panel.js`).** When `settings.showRecentFiles` is on, `createRecentFilesPanel(state)` mounts between the body-stack and the footer. It renders up to 50 rows from the existing `settings.recentFileIds` MRU (the same list that orders the `⌘O` picker), each resolved against the live file tree so renames / deletes drop out. Rows route clicks through `state.openFile` and reveal **flag** (→ `state.toggleFlagged`) + **remove** (drops the id from `recentFileIds`) icons on hover — laid out with `visibility: hidden` and a fixed row `min-height` so the reveal doesn't reflow. The panel re-renders on `settings-changed` / `files-changed` / `file-opened` / `notebook-open`. Its top border is a drag handle that writes `settings.recentFilesPanelHeight` (default 150, clamped to `[60, 70vh]`). `sidebar.js` mounts / unmounts the panel on every `settings-changed` so the General-tab checkbox and the **Show / Hide Recent Files** palette commands take effect immediately. Persisted via the new Rust `AppSettings` fields `show_recent_files`, `recent_files_panel_height`, and `recent_file_ids`.

The Settings > Editor **Make space for panes** / **Shift column to** controls were removed — make-space is automatic (see the `visiblePaneCount` / `visiblePaneCentroid` runtime fields). The **Find across files** command and its `shortcutFindAll` binding were also removed; the Find panel already spans the whole desk.

**Cursor.** The open panel body uses `cursor: crosshair` so hovering anywhere surfaces a consistent navigation affordance distinct from the editor cursor. Inputs / textareas inside the panel revert to `cursor: text`. The grip's two children opt out of crosshair (pointer on the toggle, col-resize on the resize strip).

**Tooltips.** Pane header buttons, notebook UI buttons, and file-row affordances share a single tooltip gate in `src/tooltips.js`: `applyTooltip(el, label)` stashes the label on `data-tooltip` and only writes the live `title` attribute when `showTooltips` is enabled; `setTooltipsEnabled(enabled)` flips a body class and walks every `[data-tooltip]` to add / strip its title. Wired from `main.js` once at startup and on every `settings-changed`.

**Resize drag.** `attachGripResize` captures the pointer on the resize strip, takes the panel's current width as the start, and updates `--panel-width` through `applyPanelWidth(px)`. Two anti-jank tricks: (1) on pointerdown the panel gets a `.resizing` class that zeroes its `transition: width 0.2s` (otherwise the panel interpolates 200 ms behind the cursor on every move); (2) `pointermove` is rAF-coalesced so `state.runtime.columnResizeHandler` fires at most once per frame instead of stacking up reflows. The persisted value (`sidebarPanelWidth`) is written once on pointerup. Width is clamped against `[220px, viewport-width / 2]` on every apply, including a re-clamp on window resize so the panel never overflows after a shrink.

**Session-state persistence.** Two per-window settings — `sidebarOpenPanel` and `sidebarPinned` — ride through to the next session via `AppSettings`. Only the Files panel lives in the sidebar now, so `sidebarOpenPanel` is effectively `"files" | null`; legacy values (`"styles"`, `"versions"`) are coerced to `"files"` on replay since any truthy value reopens the panel. `createSidebar()` runs a replay block at the end of init — guarded by a `_suppressStatePersist` flag — that calls `openPanel()` when the saved value is truthy and re-applies the pin class on narrow viewports. Subsequent open / close / pin toggles route through a single `persistSidebarState()` helper.

**Styles → command palette.** The retired styles sidebar list now lives entirely in the command palette: one `Use Style: <name>` entry per saved style (plus `Use Style: Default`), generated dynamically by `buildUseStyleCommands(state)` in `command-palette-commands.js`. A separate `Edit Styles` entry opens the three-column Edit Styles modal (`sidebar/style-editor-modal.js`) — left rail lists every style with a hover-revealed duplicate action (each row renders in the style's own font with two `Aa` swatches previewing its light + dark colours), middle column is the existing settings form rendered via `openStyleModal(state, target, onDone, { host })`, right column is the live preview. Deleting a style now lives at the bottom of the settings form (below "Export style as JSON") rather than on the rail row: the form's **Delete style** button routes through `showDeleteConfirmModal` (native `window.confirm` doesn't reliably block in the WebView) and, on confirm, calls the `onDelete` option the shell passes — which drops the style and remounts on Default. The editor sets an internal `deleted` flag first so its debounced flush can't re-add the just-removed style. Selecting a row in the rail flushes any pending edit, then remounts the editor for the new target. The light / dark / auto appearance toggle moved into the rail's footer.

**Versions → modal.** The Versions panel is encapsulated by `sidebar/versions-modal.js`, which renders a centered backdrop with the snapshot list on the left and a positioned preview host on the right. `createVersionsPanel(host, state, close, { previewHost })` is reused from the original sidebar panel — the `previewHost` option scopes the `.version-preview-overlay` inside the modal (now `position: absolute; inset: 0`) rather than the viewport. The legacy `show-versions-panel` event still works; the sidebar listener dynamically imports `versions-modal.js` and opens it.

**Export → command palette.** The sidebar Export button is gone. `export-current-file` is still emitted (by the palette's Export entry); the sidebar listener routes it to `stack-export.js` when a stack is open, `notebook-export-modal.js` when a notebook is open, or `doc-export-modal.js` otherwise.

Sibling helpers next to the main file: **`add-popup.js`** (footer Add button popover), **`sidebar-export.js`** (markdown export path), **`doc-export-modal.js`** (format / style / layout / citation picker for doc and project exports), **`ratchet-dropdown.js`** (centered duration grid surfaced by the command palette), and **`panel-resizer.js`** (exports `applyPanelWidth(px)` and `attachGripResize(state, resizeEl, panelOverlay)`).

### Files Panel (`sidebar/files-panel.js`, `files-panel-shared.js`, `files-panel-local-sync.js`, `files-panel-row-menu.js`, `files-panel-actions.js`, `files-panel-multi-select.js`)

Nested tree view with four node types:

- **Documents** — Markdown files. Click to open in the editor.
- **Notebooks** — Canvas-based visual notes. Click to open in the notebook view. See [README-NOTEBOOK.md](README-NOTEBOOK.md).
- **Folders** — Containers for organizing. Drag-and-drop reordering.
- **Projects** — Ordered containers whose document children concatenate into one editor buffer with dashed separators between them. Notebook children are accepted too but render as a supplementary block in the sidebar — sorted below all docs and painted at 50 % opacity — and stay self-contained (clicked → opens the canvas) instead of feeding the joined buffer. `tree-helpers.js::normalizeProjectChildren` runs after every drag-drop and on initial render so the docs-first / notebooks-after order is canonical; it skips the pinned `__inbox__` node, which is typed `project` for legacy reasons but behaves like a folder. The per-row vertical-line connector and the notebook-fade are pure CSS hanging off `data-type` / `data-id` attributes set in `sortable-list/rendering.js`. Consecutive doc rows inside a real project carry a 1 px × 4 px vertical line over the gap between icons (`.sl-item[data-type="project"]:not([data-id="__inbox__"]) > .sl-list > .sl-item[data-type="document"] + .sl-item[data-type="document"]::before`); notebook rows inside that same scope drop their `tree-item-row` to `opacity: 0.5`.

The four "create" actions (New Doc / Notebook / Folder / Project) used to live in a top-of-panel row; they now live in the **Add (+)** popover surfaced by the footer's Add button — see `sidebar/add-popup.js`. The popover's button row is the only place that renders them in the sidebar. Every existing row shares a single hamburger **Menu** button that opens a labelled dropdown (rename, duplicate, delete, …). Active item shown bold and underlined. Rendered via the `SortableList` component.

**Row interactions.** A click anywhere on a folder row — including its icon and label — toggles the folder open or closed. This applies uniformly to every container node: regular folders, Projects, **Inbox**, **Images**, and **Trash**. The explicit expand arrow (if present) remains as a visual cue but is no longer the sole hit target. A second click on the already-selected row still enters rename mode via the existing double-click/enter keymap; the toggle only fires on a single click that is not part of a pending rename gesture.

**Hover menu overlay (`files-panel-row-menu.js`).** Each row's action slot is a single hover-revealed hamburger button — earlier versions packed a row of per-action icons (flag / rename / duplicate / delete / convert / use-as-note) into the same slot and ran out of room on narrower sidebars. Clicking the hamburger opens a `position: fixed` `.tree-row-menu` dropdown anchored beneath the button; the entry list is rebuilt on demand by `getMenuEntries(nodeId, …)` so the same logic that decides "is this row eligible for `Use as note`?" doesn't need to know whether it's rendering inline or in the popup. Container rows (folders / projects / Inbox) lead with **New Doc** and **New Notebook** entries — the New-Notebook path routes through `showPromptModal` (see `files-panel-shared.js`) so the user names the file at creation time. Click outside / `Esc` / window blur close the menu — listeners attach one rAF after open so the click that opened the menu doesn't immediately close it. `dispatchRowAction` is a single function in `files-panel.js`; both the hamburger's open intent and the menu items' chosen actions route through it, so there's no parallel dispatch table. The handler bodies themselves (rename / duplicate / convert / convert-project-to-doc / convert-doc-to-project / delete / empty-trash / reveal-in-finder) live in **`files-panel-actions.js`** so `files-panel.js` stays under the 700-line repo cap. The action-slot's container has symmetric `padding: 0` so the hamburger icon visually sits with equal whitespace on every side rather than being pushed against the row's right edge. The overlay itself is still an absolutely-positioned layer above the row label so its width doesn't bite into the filename column. While a row is in rename mode the overlay is hidden (`display: none`) so the input can span the full row width.

**Row color tinting.** Each row menu (and the multi-select view, see below) closes with a 10-swatch palette: one **clear** swatch on the left plus nine faded tints exported from `files-panel-row-menu.js` as `ROW_COLORS` (red through pink, each as `{ key, rgba, swatch }`). Picking a swatch dispatches `set-color` through `dispatchRowAction`, which writes `node.bgColor = <key>` (or deletes it for clear) and persists via `saveFileTree`. Rendering happens in `sortable-list/rendering.js` — it imports `rowColorRgba(key)` and sets the matched RGBA on the `li` as an inline `--item-bg` custom property; `.sl-item > .sl-item-content { background: var(--item-bg, transparent) }` paints it. Because CSS custom properties cascade through the DOM, a coloured parent automatically tints every descendant `.sl-item-content` whose own ancestry doesn't define `--item-bg`; a child that picks its own colour overrides the parent without any extra bookkeeping. The palette UI itself is built once by the exported `createColorPalette(currentKey, onPick)` helper so the row menu and the multi-select view share an identical implementation. Skipped for the Trash root row and for anything inside Trash (those menus only carry destructive actions). For persistence, `bg_color: Option<String>` is declared on the Rust `TreeNode` struct in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs` — without that field, serde would silently drop the unknown property on load/save round-trip. `state-convert.js` and `state-tree.js#duplicateTreeNode` carry `bgColor` through doc↔project conversions and duplicates so the visual tag follows the node identity (mirrors how `flagged` is carried).

**Drag-and-drop visuals.** The drag ghost anchors to the cursor's upper-left rather than to the click-relative offset, so the cursor never obscures the drop indicator. The drop-target outline (`.sl-drop-target-item`) wraps the entire `.sl-item` (row + nested children) rather than just the row label, so the user sees the bounds of the container they're dropping into; the inner `.sl-drop-target-list` class is now layout-neutral (the previous bumped-border + padding-left version shifted every existing child several pixels right while a drag was held over the parent).

**Multi-select (`files-panel-multi-select.js`).** Three selection modes drive `state.selectedDocIds: string[]` (doc, notebook, and PDF fileIds): **shift-click** range-expands from the last anchor through the clicked row (walking `collectVisibleDocs(state, …)`, which collects docs, notebooks, and PDFs while respecting collapsed containers so an unseen folder doesn't leak into the range); **cmd / ctrl-click** toggles a single row (an empty selection seeds with the active fileId first so the first cmd-click on a sibling lands a two-row batch); and **drag-select** arms a bounding-box session whenever pointer-down lands on a non-interactive panel area (the body itself, the 20 px left padding, gaps between sections, below the file list — anywhere `isInteractiveTarget(target)` returns false). A 4 px move threshold suppresses zero-area drags. `SortableList.config.onClick` now forwards the pointer-up event so the files-panel handler can branch on `event.shiftKey` / `event.metaKey` without a separate global listener. The sidebar's `multi-select-changed` listener toggles `.multi-selected` directly on existing rows via `querySelectorAll(".sl-item[data-file-id]")` rather than calling `refreshFilesPanel(state)` — a rebuild during an active drag-select would replace every `.sl-item` element and strand the drag's cached row references on detached DOM, freezing the capture mid-gesture. Doc, notebook, and PDF rows stamp `data-file-id` in `renderItem` so the lightweight repaint can find them.

**Duplicate + Folder ↔ Project toggle.** The duplicate action (surfaced via the hover menu) shows a confirmation modal (a generic `showConfirmModal` extracted to `files-panel-shared.js`, mirroring the delete dialog's style) and the new node lands beside the original with a `-Copy` suffix instead of the previous `" copy"` filename. The same menu surfaces **Convert to project** / **Convert to folder** on real folder / project rows; the handler calls `state.convertContainerType(nodeId, target)` — folders and projects are structurally identical so the swap is a one-field tree edit. Project → Folder loses ordering and the joined preview view, so that direction prompts via `showConfirmModal` first; Folder → Project is a silent flip. A deeper conversion pair also appears: **Convert to Doc** on projects and **Convert to Project** on standalone documents. These transform the node's *content*, not just its type — Project → Doc merges child documents into a single tabbed document (via `state.convertProjectToDoc`), while Doc → Project splits a tabbed document at its `---Tab---` markers into individual child docs (via `state.convertDocToProject`). Both prompt via `showConfirmModal`. The command palette mirrors these as **Convert this Project to Doc** (visible when a project is open) and **Convert this Doc to Project** (visible when a standalone doc is open). Handlers live in `files-panel-actions.js`; the core logic in `state/state-convert.js`. Standalone-doc rows also surface **Split Headings to Files** (alongside Convert to Project, gated on `isDoc && !inProject`); the `split-at-headings` row action lazy-imports `split-at-headings-modal.js#openSplitAtHeadingsModal(state, nodeId)`, and the same modal is reachable doc-only from the command palette — core logic in `state/state-split-combine.js`.

**Images folder.** The pinned Images node renders with a photo-frame icon that has a single diagonal slash through it (replacing the previous "X"-like mark). Like Trash, it defaults to collapsed and is only expanded when the user explicitly clicks to open it; the expanded/collapsed state is persisted per-user alongside other sidebar state.

**Google Doc link badge.** Documents that carry an entry in `settings.googleDocLinks` get a small two-arrow icon slotted between the type icon and the name via `googleLinkBadgeHtml(item, state)` in `files-panel-shared.js`. `sidebar.js`'s `settings-changed` handler tracks a serialised snapshot of `googleDocLinks` and re-renders the files panel when it changes, so the badge appears the moment **Link Document to Google Doc** completes and disappears the moment Unlink fires. The icon's `<title>` reports the linked GDoc's name on hover.

**Flagged bubbling (longview).** The outline view's **Flagged** section walks nested folders so that a folder marked "flagged" lifts all of its descendants into the Flagged list. `longview-parser.js` recurses through children rather than stopping at the first non-document; the resulting entries preserve their indentation depth relative to the flagged ancestor so the hierarchy is still legible inside the Flagged group.

**Flagged section (files panel).** The sidebar's virtual **Flagged** folder is rendered outside the SortableList by `renderFlaggedSection` / `renderFlaggedNode` in `files-panel.js`, from `collectFlaggedItems(visibleTopLevel(state))` (`tree-helpers.js`) — scoped to the *visible* desk(s), so flagged items are desk-specific in single-desk mode (all-desks mode shows every desk's flags, since every desk is on screen). `collectFlaggedItems` clones flagged nodes with children preserved (folders bubble their descendants, sanitised of image/special subtrees); `renderFlaggedNode` mirrors the main tree's row look, including the `--item-bg` tint from `bgColor` (via `rowColorRgba`) so a highlighted row keeps its colour in its flagged copy, and only directly-flagged rows get the unflag button.

**Local Sync rendering** lives in `files-panel-local-sync.js`. The mounted-folder subtrees render outside the SortableList because their content comes from disk (lazy-expanded via `local_sync_read_dir`). `positionLocalSync` (now in `files-panel-local-sync.js`, called through a thin wrapper in `files-panel.js` that passes the tree + panel root elements) slots the section in just *above* the Trash row in both views, so Trash stays the last thing in the panel, below any mounts. In the single-desk view that means it sits beneath the flagged + files trees but ahead of Trash; in the all-desks view it relocates the whole section *inside* the active desk's row, just above that desk's Trash, flush with and continuing the desk's connector line. It re-runs after every tree render (`setData`, collapse toggle, drag-drop, and the drag-end snap-back / external-drop paths via the new `onDragEnd` hook) since those rebuild and detach the host row. Shared icons / escapers / hover-handlers live in `files-panel-shared.js` so both files can use them without a circular import. The root row for each mount carries a hamburger menu whose only entry is **Unlink Folder** — it invokes `removeLocalSyncFolder(id)` (Rust persists the change inside `local_sync_remove`, so the JS side just mirrors the result into `state.settings.localSyncFolders` and calls `invalidateLocalSyncCache()` + `refreshFilesPanel`). Visible mounts are filtered by `state.settings.activeDeskId`: a mount whose `deskId` matches the active desk renders, and legacy mounts with `deskId === null` stay visible across every desk for backwards compatibility. The renderer keeps a structure fingerprint (`folder list + expanded set + activeDeskId`) and a render token so a) concurrent refresh calls (`settings-changed`, `local-sync-changed`, `file-opened`) can't double-append a mount, b) refreshes triggered by `file-opened` skip the async directory rebuild and just restamp the `.active` class. The watcher path in `main.js` calls `invalidateLocalSyncCache()` before refresh so on-disk changes still force a rebuild.

**Desktop / File mode + pane indicators** (`sidebar/files-panel-pane-indicators.js`). A doc or notebook with at least one floating pane is in "desktop" mode; the same file with no panes (or hidden panes) is in "file" mode. The files panel paints a per-row strip of 5 × 7 px squares (1 px outline, 2 px radius) under the filename — one per pane the file owns — by appending a `<span class="tree-pane-indicators">` inside a flex-column wrapper around the normal `tree-item-row`. The strip's left edge is indented past the 12 px type icon + 6 px gap so it lines up with the first character of the filename. It's built by `paneIndicatorsFor(item, state)` which calls `getPanesForContext(contextIdForFile(item.fileId, item.type))` on the live pane Map. `attachPaneIndicatorTooltip(listContainer)` adds delegated `mousemove`/`mouseleave` handlers that surface a small fixed-position tooltip with the pane's `fileName` while the user `cmd`-hovers a cell (event-level `metaKey` check — no global modifier listener). The strip rerenders on `panes-changed` / `panes-hidden-changed`, both emitted from `pane-manager.js#notifyLayoutChange` / `state-panes.js`. **Hide / Show panes** (command palette) toggle `settings.panesHiddenByContext[ctx]`: a truthy entry hides every pane participating in that context (owner-match or pinned-into) and drops the indicator strip to 30 % opacity so the row still signals "this is a desktop, just tucked away." **Clear panes** drops every pane in the active context via `clearPanesForContext(ctx)`. **Copy panes to Document** / **Switch panes to Document** open a picker (active desk scope, current file excluded), then run `copyPanesBetweenContexts(sourceCtx, targetCtx)` to re-instantiate each pane under the target's `ownerContext` (preserving size, position, attach, pin, collapsed). The Switch variant additionally clears the source context, force-clears the target's hidden flag, and opens the target so the panes ride along. The hidden-context map persists per-device in `settings.panesHiddenByContext`.

**Notebook minimap** (`notebook/minimap.js`) is a floating widget anchored bottom-right of `#notebook-container` and toggled via the command palette (**Show minimap** / **Hide minimap**, gated to notebook context). State lives on `settings.minimapVisible`; the toggle goes through `state.toggleMinimap()` which emits `minimap-visibility-changed`. `main.js` mounts the widget on `notebook-open` (or at boot when restoring the last notebook) if the setting is on, and unmounts it on `notebook-unmount`.
