The FST dictionary is held in a static `Arc` inside harper, so back-to-back lints of edited buffers don't repay the dictionary build cost.

### Spellcheck (`editor/plugins/spellcheck.js`)

Lightweight doc-only spelling underline that runs alongside Proofread but with a very different cost profile. Surfaced via the command palette as **Spellcheck**, reads `state.spellcheckMode`; flipping it goes through `state-modes.js::toggleSpellcheck` (short-circuits when a notebook is open) which emits `mode-changed` and `state.updateSettings({ spellcheckMode })` so the toggle **persists across sessions**. Rehydrated from `settings.spellcheckMode` in `state.init()` next to `typewriterMode` / `dryMode`.

The mode is persisted because the underlying engine is fast enough not to need warm-up choreography: the [`spellbook`](https://github.com/helix-editor/spellbook) crate (pure-Rust Hunspell, what Helix and Zed's `codebook` use) loads the bundled en_US dictionary in roughly 10 ms inside a process-wide `OnceLock`, so there's no harper-style multi-second cold start and no need for a "Spellchecking…" modal. The dictionary files (`en_US.aff`, `en_US.dic`, MIT/MPL-licensed from `wooorm/dictionaries`) live in `src-tauri/dictionaries/` and are embedded at compile time via `include_str!`.

**Plugin.** `createSpellcheckPlugin(state)` calls the `check_spelling` Tauri command on every doc change, debounced to 600 ms (tighter than Proofread's 1.5 s since each lint is sub-millisecond after the first). Results cache per `EditorView` in a `WeakMap` with the same `runId` discard-stale-runs pattern as the grammar plugin. Misspellings paint as `.hush-spellcheck-error` with a `text-decoration: underline wavy #dc2626` (a brighter wavy red in dark mode) plus `cursor: pointer` to advertise the click affordance — the underline itself is the click target.

**Click-to-fix popover.** A second extension, `spellcheckClickHandler` (`EditorView.domEventHandlers({ mousedown })`), walks up from `ev.target` looking for `.hush-spellcheck-error`. On a hit it resolves the issue at `posAtCoords(ev.clientX, ev.clientY)`, then mounts a `<body>`-level popover (`.hush-spellcheck-popover`) at the click point with a small "Loading…" placeholder. The actual suggestions come from a second Tauri command, `spelling_suggestions(word)`, which is called lazily per-click rather than precomputed for every misspelling in the doc — `spellbook::Dictionary::suggest` is cheap but not free, and most underlines are never clicked. Clicking a suggestion dispatches `view.dispatch({ changes: { from, to, insert: replacement } })` to rewrite the word and parks the caret at the end of the replacement. Outside-click (capture-phase `mousedown` document listener) and Esc both close. The popover re-clamps its position once mounted so it never slides off the viewport's right or bottom edge.

**Wiring.** Mounted only on the main editor (`editor/editor.js`) — `createBaseExtensions` for floating panes intentionally skips it, matching the doc-only scope of Proofread. The "Turn off Spellcheck" entry appears at the top of the command palette while the toggle is active alongside the other `Turn off …` rows.

**Backend.** `commands::spellcheck::check_spelling(text)` is `async` + `spawn_blocking` (same shape as `check_grammar`) so the WebView main thread stays responsive even during the dictionary's first-call load. It walks the buffer with a small Unicode-aware tokenizer — runs of `char::is_alphabetic` characters, with inline apostrophes (both `'` and `'`) kept inside the word so "don't" tokenizes as one — skipping digits and standalone punctuation that the dictionary doesn't index. For each token it calls `dict().check(&word)`; misses turn into `SpellingIssue { from, to, word }` with the span converted from Unicode-scalar to UTF-16 code-unit offsets through the same `char_to_utf16_range` helper grammar.rs uses, so CodeMirror can apply the range directly. `commands::spellcheck::spelling_suggestions(word)` runs `dict().suggest(&word, &mut Vec<String>)`, truncates to 8, and returns. The dictionary itself sits behind a `static DICT: OnceLock<Dictionary>` so back-to-back calls — including the first one after startup — share the parsed `Dictionary` value.

### Typewriter Mode (`editor/plugins/typewriter.js`)

Locks cursor to a fixed screen position (default 60% from top). Draggable boundary line for repositioning. Extra padding so first/last lines can reach the boundary. Also handles ratchet scroll (pins last line to 50% center).

### Per-Editor Mode Contexts (`state/mode-context.js`)

Focus mode, typewriter mode, and D.R.Y. mode are scoped per editing surface rather than being global flags. Each floating pane and stack column creates its own **mode context** via `createModeContext(appState)` — a prototype-inherited proxy (`Object.create(appState)`) with own-property `focusMode`, `typewriterMode`, and `dryMode` booleans (all start `false`). The proxy is passed as `appState` to `createBaseExtensions` and `createDryHighlightPlugin`, so every plugin that reads `stateRef.focusMode` etc. reads the local value while settings, event emitter, and file tree all delegate through the prototype chain to the real AppState.

**`toggle(modeName, view, container)`** flips the local flag, applies typewriter DOM side-effects (padding, boundary line) when the mode is `typewriterMode`, and nudges the CM view via `view.dispatch({ effects: [] })` so decoration-based plugins (focus mode, DRY) rebuild.

**`getActiveModeContext(appState)`** resolves the active editing context: checks the active stack column first, then the active floating pane, returning `{ mc, view, container }` or `null` for the main editor. The keyboard shortcut handlers in `commands.js` route all three mode toggles through this function — when a context is found, `mc.toggle()` is called; otherwise the shortcut falls through to the global `state.toggleFocus()` / `state.toggleTypewriter()` / `state.toggleDry()` (main editor path, unchanged).

The main editor does NOT use a mode context — it reads the global flags on `AppState` directly. This preserves backward compatibility and avoids changing the main editor's typewriter code path (which uses a body-level boundary line, not pane-local padding).

### Project View (`editor/plugins/project-view.js`)

CodeMirror plugin for project mode. `createProjectViewField` (StateField) replaces `---hush-separator---` lines with non-editable dashed widgets. `createSeparatorFilter` (transactionFilter) blocks edits touching separator lines.

**CSS spacing gotcha — use padding/height, not margin.** CodeMirror's height map measures each block from `getBoundingClientRect`, which excludes margin. Any vertical `margin` on a CM block (or on a block widget like `.project-separator`, or on a line carrying a `Decoration.line` class like `.cm-tab-marker-line`) gets stored in the heightmap as a `spaceAbove` pseudo-block of type `WidgetBefore`. `moveVertically`'s `posAtCoords` walks upward through `WidgetBefore` blocks **without** running the text-area check, then jumps `yOffset` past the entire line — so ArrowUp overshoots the immediately-previous line. ArrowDown escapes the same path because the `spaceAbove` sits above its line, not below. The separator's surrounding gap is rendered via `height: 50px` + a centred `::before` (no margin), and the tab marker's vertical breathing room is `padding`, not `margin`.

### File Drop (`editor/file-drop.js`)

Four context-aware drop targets. When the **files** panel is open, a non-blocking "Import file" overlay appears inside `#panel-overlay` and the panel itself listens for drops; the row under the cursor lights up via `.sl-item.drop-target` and resolves to a folder / project / desk parent (non-container rows fall back to their parent, empty space falls back to the active desk's Inbox). `.md` / `.txt` files become docs via `state.newFile(parentId, { initialContent, initialName })`; `.hushnote` files are unpacked through `unpackNotebook` (a single zip envelope with `data.json` + an `images/` folder, also produced by the Export modal) and land via `state.createNotebook(name, parentId, { initialContent })`; `.hushstack` files round-trip the JSON envelope verbatim via `state.createStack`. File extraction on drop goes through `collectDroppedFiles` which unions `dataTransfer.files` and `dataTransfer.items[].getAsFile()` and dedupes by `name|size|lastModified` — iPadOS WKWebView often hands external-app drops through the `items` collection while leaving `files` empty, so reading only one source silently lost iPad imports. Drop outcomes (success / failure / unrecognised payload) surface through the shared `editor/import-toast.js` banner so iPad users see what happened without a console. In doc mode, drops on the editor append text content for `.md`/`.txt` files; image drops (PNG/JPG/GIF/WebP/SVG/etc.) are routed through `state.createImageFromFile()` which saves the binary via `save_image` and inserts a standard `![alt](filename.png)` reference at the cursor coordinate (see "Doc Images" below). In notebook mode, the canvas handles drops natively (images become image shapes, text files become text shapes); holding Cmd/Ctrl while dropping plain text wraps it in a markdown blockquote and creates the resulting TextShape at 14 px instead of the default 18. Doc panes get their own drop wiring via `attachPaneTextDrop` (in `pane/pane-editor.js`) — the main editor's drop net only covers `#editor-container` so without this drops on a floating doc pane fell through. The two payload-classification helpers (`hasAcceptableDragPayload`, `readDragText`) are shared exports of `file-drop.js`. Tauri's built-in drag-drop is disabled so DOM events reach the webview.

### iPad File Associations (`src-tauri/Info.ios.plist`, `editor/external-open.js`)

`.hushnote` and `.hushstack` are declared via `UTExportedTypeDeclarations` so iPadOS recognises both system-wide (Files.app icon, "Open With" sheet, share sheet); matching `CFBundleDocumentTypes` entries register Hush as `LSHandlerRank=Owner` for each. Markdown / `public.plain-text` ride along as `Alternate`. When the user taps a file in Files.app the OS hands the app a `file://` URL through `tauri-plugin-deep-link` — `main.js` calls `getCurrent()` once on startup to catch cold launches and registers `onOpenUrl` for subsequent opens, both routing through `importExternalFile(state, url)` in `editor/external-open.js`. That helper reads the bytes via `plugin-fs` (`fs:allow-read-file` is in the capabilities allowlist), unpacks `.hushnote` zips through `unpackNotebook`, hands `.hushstack` JSON straight to `createStack`, and lands `.md`/`.txt` via `newFile`. Every branch surfaces through the same `import-toast` banner. The pre-init "app not ready yet" case is detected by `state.getInboxId()` returning null so the user can retry once the file tree has loaded.

### Stack Export (`sidebar/stack-export.js`)

Pressing Export with a stack open bypasses the doc/notebook modals because the column layout, scroll positions, view state, and spine colours only round-trip through the native `.hushstack` envelope. `exportCurrentStack(state)` pulls the active `StackComponent` from `stack/stack-bridge.js`, calls `serialize()`, re-encodes through `encodeStackContent`, and writes the bytes via the OS save dialog (`@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog`) on desktop or `navigator.share` on iPad. Sidebar `export-current-file` short-circuits to this helper when `state.currentStackFileId` is set so the doc Markdown/PDF picker never shows for stacks.

### Google Docs Integration (`editor/google-docs/` + `google-docs/`)

The Google Docs feature is split across two folders by responsibility. **`editor/google-docs/`** holds the Phase 1 paste/copy converters — pure DOM/string functions that don't touch the network and depend only on CodeMirror. **`google-docs/`** (top-level) holds the Phase 2 stack: OAuth, the Drive/Docs API client, the Drive picker modal, the source-of-truth modal, the link bar, the link store, and the link-command flows. Phase 2 reuses Phase 1's converters on both sides of the API boundary — push runs `markdownToHtml`, pull runs `htmlToMarkdown` — so the same syntax handling (`==highlight==` → `<mark>`, `%%comments%%` strip, list aria-level depth) lands in both clipboard and API paths.

#### Phase 1 — paste/copy (`editor/google-docs/`)

Clipboard-only formatting bridge, no auth or API. Four files:

- **`html-to-markdown.js`** — pure converter. Walks the DOM produced by `DOMParser` and emits Hush-flavoured markdown. Handles `<h1>`–`<h6>`, `<p>`, `<ul>`/`<ol>`/`<li>`, `<blockquote>`, `<a>`, `<br>`, `<hr>`, `<code>`/`<pre>`, plus the inline emphasis tags (`<b>`, `<strong>`, `<i>`, `<em>`, `<s>`, `<del>`, `<mark>`). Google Docs's quirks are special-cased on the way in: the `<b id="docs-internal-guid-…">` envelope is unwrapped before block walking (otherwise every block would render as bold), inline-styled `<span style="font-weight:700">` / `font-style:italic` / `text-decoration:line-through` are detected via small style regexes alongside the standard tags, `<span style="background-color:…">` (non-white / non-transparent) becomes `==highlight==`, and `https://www.google.com/url?q=…` redirect links are peeled back to the real destination via `URL.searchParams`. Returns `null` when the input doesn't contain any of `RICH_TAG_RE`'s tokens, so plain-text pastes fall through to CodeMirror's default path untouched. A once-indented paragraph (`margin-left`/`padding-left` ≥ 18pt, half a Google indent level) is read back as a `> ` block quote — the inverse of the push side's indented-paragraph emission, since Google Docs has no block-quote style of its own. **Lists handle three different DOM shapes Google Docs actually produces**: (1) standard nested `<ul>`/`<ol>` inside an `<li>`; (2) consecutive sibling `<ul>`/`<ol>` blocks at the same DOM level with `aria-level` on each `<li>` indicating visual depth; and (3) the invalid-HTML pattern where `<ul>`/`<ol>` sits as a *direct child* of another `<ul>`/`<ol>` (no intervening `<li>`) — the HTML5 parser preserves this verbatim, so without explicit handling every nested item silently dropped. `blockListGroup` walks every consecutive sibling list, `collectListItems` recurses through nested lists in *both* the inside-`<li>` and direct-child positions, and depth is read from `aria-level` (1-indexed) when present, otherwise from ancestor-list count. Depths are renormalised to a contiguous 0..N range so visually-jumped levels collapse cleanly, and per-depth ordered-list counters pop when depth decreases so a later sibling group restarts numbering. A `data-hush-consumed` flag keeps already-processed sibling lists from re-entering the group at the top level.
- **`markdown-to-html.js`** — pure converter for the copy commands. Two-pass: `parseBlocks` walks lines into block objects (`heading`, `paragraph`, `blockquote`, `list`, `code`, `hr`), then `renderBlock` + `renderInline` emit HTML. Lists nest by leading-whitespace depth via `nestList`, which keeps a stack of open sublists and pops back when the next entry's indent regresses. Inline order matters: code spans first (so emphasis runs don't reach into them), then links (anchored on a non-`!` lookbehind so image refs aren't matched), then `==highlight==` → `<mark style="background-color:#fff475">`, then `~~strike~~`, bold, italic. `%%comments%%` (single- and multi-line) are stripped by a leading regex pass before `parseBlocks` runs, as is the imported-comment scaffolding (`{>…<id}` anchors unwrapped to their text, `[>id]:` notes dropped) via `editor/comment-syntax.js#stripCommentSyntax` — comment sync is pull-only, so push keeps the prose and never disturbs Google's own comment anchors. **Block-level spacing is tuned for the Google Docs importer**: every paragraph/heading carries a `margin:0 0 10pt 0` space-after so paragraphs don't collapse flush together on push (the old `margin:0` produced no inter-paragraph gap), and since Google Docs has no block-quote *style*, a `> ` quote is emitted as a once-indented paragraph (`margin-left:36pt`) — the convention the pull side reads back.
- **`paste-extension.js`** — `createGoogleDocsPasteExtension()` returns an `EditorView.domEventHandlers` paste listener. Reads `clipboardData.getData("text/html")`, hands it to `htmlToMarkdown`, and on a non-null result calls `event.preventDefault()` and dispatches a single `view.dispatch({ changes, selection })` to insert the markdown at the cursor. Mounted on the main editor (`createEditor`'s extension list) and on every floating pane (`createBaseExtensions`) — registered *after* `createImagePasteExtension` in both lists so a clipboard payload with image bytes still wins for the image-paste path.
- **`copy-command.js`** — exports `copyAsGoogleDoc(view)` and `copyAsHtml(view)`. Both pull the active selection (or the whole document if `sel.empty`) and run it through `markdownToHtml`. The Google Doc variant writes a `ClipboardItem` with both `text/html` (rich for Google Docs / Word / Notion) and `text/plain` (the original markdown for terminals, code editors, etc.); the HTML variant calls `navigator.clipboard.writeText` with the HTML *source* so users can paste the markup itself into a code editor or blog draft. Both share `execCommand("copy")` fallback paths (a hidden `contenteditable` div for rich, a hidden `textarea` for plain) for older WKWebViews that ship without `ClipboardItem` / `writeText`. A small toast (`.gdocs-copy-toast`, reusing the `.style-locked-toast` styling) confirms the copy. The two command-palette entries (**Copy as Google Doc**, **Copy as HTML**, both `ctx: "doc"`) dynamic-import the module so users who never run the command pay nothing at startup.

#### Phase 2 — per-document link with explicit push/pull (`google-docs/`)

User-driven only — there's no sync engine, no polling, no debounce, no conflict-resolution state. The user explicitly drives every push and pull via the link bar, and both Hush and Google Docs's own version histories are the safety net. Eight files plus a Rust commands file (`commands/google_docs.rs`) covering OAuth + the per-document link map.

- **`auth.js`** — OAuth PKCE flow + token cache. Google's OAuth-for-Desktop policy doesn't accept custom URI schemes for generic desktop apps (only iOS / Android / UWP / macOS-bundled apps); the supported pattern is a **loopback HTTP redirect** that the app listens on directly. `startOAuthFlow()` calls the Rust `start_google_oauth_listener` command, which binds `127.0.0.1:0` (OS-assigned ephemeral port), returns the port number, and spawns a thread that accepts a single connection — parses `code` / `error` out of the request line's query string, writes a tiny success/error HTML response, and emits the existing `oauth-callback` event (with `provider: "google"`). The user registers `http://127.0.0.1` (no port) as the authorised redirect URI on their OAuth client; Google wildcards the port on loopback URIs so a fresh ephemeral port every launch is fine. **Credentials live in `AppSettings`** rather than build-time env vars — the user enters a Client ID (and optional Client Secret) in Settings > Sync > Google Sync, and the values round-trip through `googleClientId` / `googleClientSecret` on the settings struct. `getClientId()` is async and lazy-loads the cached value from settings on first use; `setClientId(v)` lets the Settings tab invalidate the cache after an edit. Scopes: `auth/drive` (full Drive R/W) plus `auth/userinfo.email` (the Settings tab "Connected as" line). The narrow `drive.file` scope was tempting but only grants per-file access for files the app created or that the user explicitly picked via Google Picker — since Hush uses our own picker (a small Drive `files.list` modal), `drive.file` would lock us out of existing user-created docs. Tokens are cached in module locals, lazy-loaded from `AppSettings.googleAccessToken` on first need, and proactively refreshed when the cached `expiresAt` is within 30 s. `gFetch(url, opts)` is the authenticated wrapper used by every API call. The Rust token-exchange / refresh commands (`exchange_google_token`, `refresh_google_token`) read both client id and (optional) client secret straight from `AppSettings`; the secret is included in the form-encoded body only when non-empty, so Native / UWP clients with no secret work alongside Desktop-app clients that require one. **Cross-window cache priming.** The Settings window and the main editor each get their own copy of this module — `completeOAuthFlow()` populates `_accessToken` in Settings, but the main editor's locals start empty until something reads from disk. `hasTokensAsync()` awaits `ensureTokens()` (which pulls fresh values via `invoke("get_settings")`) before reporting, and `link-command.js::requireConnection()` awaits it, so the link / push / pull gates don't trip the first time they're hit from the main editor after a fresh OAuth completion in Settings.
- **`api.js`** — thin Drive + Docs API wrappers. Phase 2 uses Drive (not Docs API directly) for the **content** path because Drive's media-upload converter handles HTML → Google Docs structural conversion server-side: push runs `PATCH /upload/drive/v3/files/{id}?uploadType=media` with `Content-Type: text/html`, pull runs `GET /drive/v3/files/{id}/export?mimeType=text/html`. Tab support adds a thin Docs API layer alongside Drive: `getDocumentWithTabs(docId)` issues `documents.get?includeTabsContent=true` (no field mask — Google's mask parser flags some sub-fields as comment-related and rejects the call with a 400 unless `includeComments=true` is also set); `listTabs(docId)` re-runs the same fetch and projects each tab's metadata into a flat `[{ id, title, index, nestingLevel }]` list for the push path; `createTab(docId, index, title, parentTabId?)` issues an `addDocumentTab` batchUpdate request with `tabProperties.{title,index,parentTabId}` (the actual Docs API surface — `createTab` was the old JS wrapper name, not the wire type); `renameTab(docId, tabId, title)` issues an `updateDocumentTabProperties` request with the target `tabId` set inside `tabProperties` alongside the new value and `fields: "title"`; `deleteTab(docId, tabId)` issues a `deleteTab` request. `replaceTabPlainText(docId, tabId, text)` fetches the doc with tab content, walks to the target tab's body, deletes its content range (`deleteContentRange` with `range.tabId`), and inserts the new text at `index: 1` of that `tabId` (`insertText` with `location.tabId`). `exportAsHtml(docId)` is unfiltered — Drive's export doesn't actually support per-tab filtering (the documented `tabIds` parameter is only on `files.get`), which is why the multi-tab pull path bypasses Drive entirely and walks `documentTab` structures via `docs-walker.js`. `createDocumentFromHtml(title, html)` for Branch A of the link flow ("Create new") uses a multipart upload that bundles JSON metadata (`{ name, mimeType: application/vnd.google-apps.document }`) with the HTML body so Drive does the conversion in a single call. `listDocuments` filters Drive to `mimeType='application/vnd.google-apps.document'` for the picker. `viewUrl(docId)` synthesises the `https://docs.google.com/document/d/{id}/edit` URL for the link bar's title chip.
- **`tabs-sync.js`** — orchestrates the tab-aware push and pull on top of `api.js`. **Push** parses the markdown into ordered `{ path, content }` sections via `editor/tabs.js#parseTabs`, then media-uploads the first section's HTML via Drive. Drive's media upload **resets a tabbed Google Doc to a single default tab** (the previously-documented "only replaces the root tab" behaviour doesn't hold in practice), so the push rebuilds the entire tab tree from scratch via the Docs API after the upload lands: rename the auto-created root tab to the first section's leaf, then walk the remaining sections in source order and `addDocumentTab` each one with the right `parentTabId` resolved from a path → tabId map populated as ancestors are created. Per-parent index counters keep siblings in source order. Each new tab gets its content via `replaceTabPlainText` (plain text — structured insertion into a specific `tabId` isn't a Drive media-upload primitive, and emitting `insertText` + `updateTextStyle` + `updateParagraphStyle` batchUpdate requests for every formatted run would balloon the surface area). **Pull** issues one `documents.get?includeTabsContent=true` and walks the response: `tabs[]` is flattened depth-first into `[{ path, documentTab }]`, each `documentTab` is converted to markdown via `docs-walker.js`, and the per-tab markdown is rejoined with the canonical `---a---/---b---` marker form via `editor/tabs.js#joinTabs`. Single-tab docs short-circuit to a plain Drive HTML export so they don't accumulate marker noise.
- **`incremental-push.js` + `gdoc-diff.js`** — non-destructive push. The legacy `tabs-sync.js` push media-uploads fresh HTML and rebuilds the tab tree, which **replaces every body** — destroying every comment anchor and resetting all formatting. `tryIncrementalPush` (called first by `pushToGoogleDoc`) instead diffs the *live* Google Doc against the markdown at the **paragraph level** and applies only the changed paragraphs via Docs API `batchUpdate`, so untouched text keeps its comments and styling. **Tabs are covered**: a `batchUpdate` request can carry a `tabId`, so `pairSectionsToTabs` matches each Hush section (`---Name---` markers via `parseTabs`) to the Google tab with the same path (`flattenGoogleTabs` walks the tab tree depth-first into `{ path, tabId, documentTab }`), each tab's body is diffed independently, and the per-tab requests are concatenated into one call. `gdoc-diff.js` is the pure engine: `projectToParagraphs` strips markdown (and `{>…<id}` / `%%…%%` scaffolding) to the plain-text paragraphs Google holds; `readGoogleParagraphs` reads a tab's paragraphs with their character indices; `computeParagraphOps` LCS-aligns the two by whitespace-normalised text (unchanged paragraphs → no-ops, gaps pair into in-place modifications with leftovers as insert/delete — empty Google spacing paragraphs are never touched); `buildBatchRequests` emits minimal requests, using a common-prefix/suffix char diff *inside* a modified paragraph so a comment anchored mid-paragraph (outside the edited run) survives, ordered high-index→low so earlier edits don't shift later indices. It's deliberately conservative — it bails (caller falls back to the whole-document rebuild, so we're never worse than before) on a *structural* tab change (a tab added, removed, renamed, reordered out of a matching path set), footnotes/tables/images, or an aggregate diff large enough (>60 % of a non-trivial doc's paragraphs) to suggest the projection drifted. `computeParagraphOps` takes an unused `baseParas` arg: the seam for a future 3-way merge (the durable base snapshot it'd need should live in a sidecar, **not** the `GoogleDocLink` map). The throwaway node harnesses used to validate the index math and tab pairing live nowhere in-tree (no JS test runner is wired up).
- **`docs-walker.js`** — converts a `documentTab` (Docs API structured form) to markdown. Coverage is intentionally narrow but enough for the common cases: headings (H1–H6 plus `TITLE`/`SUBTITLE`), paragraphs with bold/italic/strikethrough/link runs, bulleted and numbered lists nested via `bullet.nestingLevel`, and tables flattened with ` | ` separators. A non-heading, non-list paragraph with `paragraphStyle.indentStart.magnitude ≥ 18pt` is emitted as a `> ` block quote (mirror of the indented-paragraph push). Inline objects (images, equations, drawings) are dropped — they'd need binary upload to round-trip and aren't a tabs-feature concern.
- **`comments-sync.js`** (top-level `google-docs/`) — pull-only Google comment integration. On pull (`importFromGoogleDoc` / `pullFromGoogleDoc`), `fetchAndWeaveComments` lists the doc's comments via the Drive Comments API (`api.js#listComments` — `content`, `author`, `quotedFileContent`, `resolved`, `replies`) and `weaveComments` threads them into the markdown: each open comment's `quotedFileContent.value` is located in the pulled text and wrapped as a `{>…<id}` anchor with a `[>id]:` note appended (author-prefixed, replies flattened with `↳`). **Picking the right occurrence:** the Drive API only gives the quoted *text*, not a position, so a comment on a word that recurs earlier would land on the wrong instance. To fix this the pull keeps Google's inline comment markers as position sentinels (`html-to-markdown.js` emits `COMMENT_MARKER_SENTINEL` in `commentMarkers` mode instead of deleting the `#cmntN` superscripts; `extractCommentMarkers` recovers their offsets), and `weaveComments` anchors each comment to its marker: the strongest signal is an *exact* match — a marker whose immediately-preceding text equals the quote is unambiguously that comment's, so a comment on a single "." lands on the marked period rather than the first one in the document. Failing an exact match it falls back to the occurrence nearest a marker, then (no markers — the Docs-API multi-tab path) the first free occurrence. Anchors are applied back-to-front so earlier offsets stay valid. When the quoted text can't be matched at all (the doc changed since the comment was made), the note is appended unanchored with the quote echoed inline, so nothing is silently dropped. Each note carries the Google comment id as hidden `%%cmnt id=…%%` metadata. Comment *content* is pull-only — Google's own anchor stays authoritative, so pushing the document body can't lose a comment's selection range — but **resolving** is written back: `resolveMarkedComments` (called from `pushToGoogleDoc`, before the scaffolding is stripped) posts an `action:"resolve"` reply via `api.js#resolveComment` for every comment the user flagged resolved locally, returning `{ resolved, failed, noId }` counts that `pushToGoogleDoc` surfaces to the sync log (`noId` flags comments imported before id-capture, which a re-pull fixes). The editor visualises the anchors via `editor/plugins/comment-anchors.js`: the commented range is tinted while every delimiter (`{>`, `<id}`) and the whole `[>id]:` definition block are hidden, so a reader scrolling through sees only highlighted text. Hovering the range pops a CodeMirror `hoverTooltip` with the note and a Resolve/Unresolve button (no inline icon — the tint plus hover is the whole affordance); resolving writes the `resolved` flag back into the definition. After a successful push, `stripResolvedComments` removes every locally-resolved comment from the editor (unwrap anchor → drop `[>id]:` line) so it disappears immediately rather than lingering until the next pull. A **comments panel** (`ui/comments-panel.js`, pinned to the left edge of the outline and sharing its show/hide + dock offsets, scrolling independently) lists the document's comments in full (no excerpting, light separators); clicking one scrolls the editor to the anchor via `EditorView.scrollIntoView`. When the outline is inset, `editor/modes.js#applyColumnLayout` adds the panel's width to `rightInsetOffset` so it pushes the editor column in rather than overlaying it (the panel calls `columnResizeHandler` on every visibility change). `link-command.js#dropDuplicateLeadingTitle` collapses *every* consecutive copy of the leading title (not just one, and skipping a leading `---Tab---` marker) so the doc title can't accumulate across pull/push cycles. Comments are **omitted entirely from PDF export** — `preprocess::process_comments` strips them to plain prose (markdown export keeps the full syntax). On import, `html-to-markdown.js#stripGoogleCommentArtifacts` drops Google's *own* exported comment scaffolding — the inline `#cmntN` superscript markers and the entire foot section from the first `#cmnt_ref` back-reference onward (comment threads, replies, and "X reacted with ⭐ …" reaction lines) — so it never leaks into the body and gets pushed back as literal text.
- **`icon.js`** — exports `GOOGLE_DOCS_ICON_SVG`, a single inline SVG string shared by every tab-related surface (editor pill in `editor/plugins/tab-marker.js`, outline header in `longview/longview-tabs.js`, files sidebar row in `sidebar/files-panel-tabs.js`). Strokes are remapped to `currentColor` so each surface's theme drives the chrome alongside the rest of the row.
- **`picker.js`** — `openGoogleDocPicker({ allowCreate, defaultTitle })` is a small modal (`gdoc-picker-*` classes; CSS in `styles/google-docs.css`). Search input is debounced ~250 ms, `lastRunId` short-circuits stale results so a slow query that lands after a newer query doesn't overwrite the displayed list. With `allowCreate: true` a "Create new" row at the bottom returns `{ create: true, title }` so the link flow can branch on it. Resolves to `null` on Esc / backdrop click / close button.
- **`source-of-truth-modal.js`** — the one-time direction picker that appears when the user links a Hush doc to an *existing* Google Doc. Two big buttons (Use Hush as source / Use Google Doc as source). Each option carries a one-line detail that names the destructive action explicitly ("Replaces the Google Doc with the Hush content"). Resolves to `"hush"`, `"gdoc"`, or `null` (cancelled — link is not established). The "Create new" branch skips this modal entirely since the GDoc doesn't exist yet.
- **`link-bar.js`** — the rounded pill pinned to the top of the editor column whenever the active doc has a link in `settings.googleDocLinks`. `initLinkBar(state)` is called once from `main.js`; it subscribes to `file-opened`, `settings-changed`, and `layout-changed` and re-renders / re-syncs on each. The bar hides while a notebook or project is open (neither maps to a single GDoc). **Mounted at body level** with `position: fixed; z-index: 90` rather than inside `#editor-container`, because the macOS Tauri `#drag-region` element (`position: fixed; z-index: 80; -webkit-app-region: drag`) would otherwise sit on top of the bar and steal every click for window-dragging. Lifting the bar above z-index 80 at the body stacking level is the only way to outrank it; a child of `#editor-container` (a `z-index: 0` stacking context) can never escape its parent's rank at the body level no matter how high its local z-index. `-webkit-app-region: no-drag` stays on the bar as belt-and-suspenders. `syncLayout()` pins the pill's outer `left` / `right` to the *inside* edges of the text column — `scroller.rect.left + scroller.paddingLeft` and `(window.innerWidth - scroller.rect.right) + scroller.paddingRight` — so the pill's background scopes to the column instead of spanning the full editor viewport (a previous full-span bar painted over Gutter pane headers). Title chip on the left opens the GDoc in the system browser via `@tauri-apps/plugin-opener`; three plain-text buttons on the right (Pull / Push / Unlink) fire immediately on click — no `window.confirm` prompts since both sides have version history and the inline status line is the synchronous feedback channel. **`setStatus(text, mode, autoClearMs)`** drives a `<span class="gdoc-link-bar-status">` between the chip and the actions: `Sending…` / `Receiving…` / `Unlinking…` for in-flight (italic, 0.75 opacity), `Sent ✓` / `Received ✓` in green for success (auto-clears after 2 s), `Failed: …` in red for errors (auto-clears after 4–5 s).
- **`link-store.js`** — JS wrapper over four Rust commands (`set_google_doc_link`, `get_google_doc_link`, `clear_google_doc_link`, `list_google_doc_links`). The durable copy of each link lives in its file's desk (`.hush/gdoc-links.json` — see "Portable desk meta" under Local Desks) so links ride desk handoffs; the `google_doc_links: HashMap<String, GoogleDocLink>` field on `AppSettings` is the merged read cache the commands maintain. `getLink(state, fileId)` is sync (reads through the cached `state.settings.googleDocLinks`) so the link bar can decide whether to render in a single tick. `setLink` / `clearLink` / `appendLog` round-trip through Rust, then refresh `state.settings` from disk via `get_settings`.
- **`link-command.js`** — the five user-facing flows: `importFromGoogleDoc` (Phase 2a — picker + tab-aware pull + `state.newFile({ openImmediately: false })` + `setLink` + `state.openFile` so the link is stored *before* the editor switches and the link bar paints in one pass on `file-opened`), `linkCurrentDocument` (Phase 2b — picker + source-of-truth modal + initial push or pull + `setLink`), `createGoogleDocFromCurrent` (one-shot inverse of import — pushes the active Hush doc into a brand-new GDoc via `createDocumentFromHtml` and stores the link, no picker / source-of-truth detour), `unlinkCurrentDocument` (Phase 2b — clear the link, leave both docs alone), and the link-bar's `pushToGoogleDoc(state, link)` / `pullFromGoogleDoc(state, link)`. Push + pull both delegate to `tabs-sync.js#pushMarkdownWithTabs` / `pullMarkdownWithTabs` so `---Tab name---` markers (and nested `---a---/---b---` paths) round-trip to real Google Doc tabs via the Docs API. Each surfaces clean errors via the picker / source-of-truth modal `null` returns; `requireConnection()` throws a friendly "Connect Google Docs in Settings > Sync > Google Sync first" if tokens are missing. The `inlineGoogleExportStyles(html)` helper handles a Drive *export* HTML quirk: instead of inline-styled spans like the clipboard HTML uses, Drive's export embeds CSS classes in a `<style>` block at the top of the document (`.c1 { font-weight: 700 }` etc.). Before running Phase 1's `htmlToMarkdown`, we walk the rules, build a class → declarations map for the four properties we care about (`font-weight`, `font-style`, `text-decoration`, `background-color`), and inline them onto each element's `style=""` attribute. Phase 1's existing inline-style detection then handles them unchanged.

#### Tabs UI (editor pill, outline, files sidebar)

The `---Tab name---` syntax (and nested `---Parent---/---Child---`) shows up across three surfaces:

- **Editor pill** (`editor/plugins/tab-marker.js`) — CodeMirror ViewPlugin. Each marker line gets a `.cm-tab-marker-line` line decoration plus a replace-decoration widget that swaps the raw text for a small rounded pill carrying the Google Docs glyph + the full path label (`Parent / Child / …`). The pill is hidden only when a caret sits **strictly past** `line.from` — that way a freshly-opened file with a marker on line 1 keeps the pill rendered (the editor places the initial caret at offset 0, which would otherwise un-pill the marker on every open). Non-empty selections that overlap the line always reveal the raw text so the user can replace the run. `parseTabMarkerPath(line)` from `editor/tabs.js` returns the segment list; the widget joins with " / " via `pathToLabel(path)`.
- **Outline header** (`longview/longview-tabs.js`) — `buildTabContainers(content, tabs, onJumpTo)` walks parsed tab markers in source order and appends a flat `.longview-tab` container per marker to the outline root (no nesting, no fold chrome, no pill background). Each header is a dashed-top-border row carrying the same Google Docs glyph and the full path label; clicking the header runs `scrollToOffset(state, marker.startOffset)` which dispatches a `selection: { anchor }` plus `EditorView.scrollIntoView` state effect (the explicit state effect rather than `scrollIntoView: true` because `coordsAtPos` returns null for offsets outside the rendered viewport, which silently no-op'd the previous manual-scroll path). Fragments are routed into each container's body by source-offset — the `routeFor(offset)` function returns the deepest container whose `startOffset ≤ offset`, and paragraph rows are routed per-line so a text fragment that straddles a marker (between a heading and a flag) splits across the right tab bodies.
- **Files sidebar** (`sidebar/files-panel-tabs.js`) — `augmentTreeWithTabs(state, tree)` walks the file tree, calls `getTabsForDoc(state, fileId)` on every document node, and builds a nested tab-marker subtree using the source-order markers' depths (top-level tabs at depth 0, nested at depth 1+). Synthetic `type: "tab-marker"` nodes carry `fileId` + `tabOffset` + `tabPath` + `tabDepth`; SortableList's standard nesting visuals (indent + left-border rule) render the hierarchy for free. The click handler is wired **inline in `renderTabMarkerRow`** rather than through SortableList's `onClick` because SortableList's pointer flow bails on items where `canDrag` returns false (no `pendingDrag`, so no synthetic click event fires) — a direct row-level listener routes around that. The row carries the Google Docs glyph at 50% opacity (lifts to 85% on hover) plus the leaf name. `stripTabMarkersFromTree` runs inside `onChange` so persisted file trees never inherit a synthetic node. `initFilesPanelTabSync(state)` is idempotent and called from `createFilesPanel`; it attaches one `doc-content-changed` listener that debounces at 600 ms and re-augments the panel so newly-typed markers show up live.

The Google Docs SVG glyph itself lives in `google-docs/icon.js` (one inline string with `currentColor` strokes) and is imported by all three surfaces so the visual stays consistent across the app.

**Filename rule for linked and tabbed docs.** `state/state-naming.js#maybeRenameFromFirstLine` and `maybeRenameFileFromContent` both short-circuit when `state.settings.googleDocLinks[fileId]` is set — the Google Doc title is the source of truth for the filename, and the first-line auto-rename would otherwise overwrite it with the first content line (or the leading tab marker). A second guard skips auto-rename for tabbed documents (any doc containing `---Tab---` markers) that already carry an explicit name (not "Untitled") — this preserves names set by Project → Doc conversion or manual rename. Freshly created tabbed docs (still named "Untitled") continue to auto-rename from the first content line. `deriveName` also walks past `---Tab name---` markers and blank lines so even non-linked docs whose first content line follows a marker pick up a sensible filename.

- **`link-bar.js`** — the rounded pill for linked docs. Pinned at `top: calc(env(safe-area-inset-top, 0px) + 6px)` so the pill slides clear of the iOS status bar / dynamic island on iPad / iPhone with a small visual gap so it reads as a deliberate pill (resolves to 6 px on desktop, which is also where we want it). Without the safe-area anchor the Push / Pull / Unlink buttons sat under the system overlay where touches don't reach — `viewport-fit=cover` in `index.html` lets the WebView extend under the notch, and `position: fixed; top: 0` plus a 25-30 px bar height meant the buttons literally lived in the strip iOS reserves for its own gestures. The buttons also carry `touch-action: manipulation` and `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` so the first tap routes straight through on iOS.

#### Settings > Sync sub-tabs

The Sync settings tab is split into three sub-tabs (**Google Sync** / **iCloud** / **Log**). Local Sync folder management lives on each Local Sync row's hamburger dropdown (mounts come from the sidebar's Add (+) menu, "Local Folder"); the **iCloud** sub-tab is a debug panel for the iOS folder-bookmark plugin (`settings/settings-tabs-icloud.js`, `render`/`bindICloudSubTab`); the **Log** sub-tab shows the persistent `settings.syncLog` (error rows tagged with the `[!]` prefix paint red) with a Clear button. The active sub-tab is module-local in `settings/settings-tabs-sync.js` (a `_activeSubTab` variable plus `getSyncSubTab` / `setSyncSubTab` exports — the whitelist accepts `google` / `icloud` / `log`). The outer `renderSyncTab(settings)` paints the sub-tab nav at the top of the panel and delegates the body to whichever sub-renderer is active. Bindings live in `settings/settings-sync-tab.js`: a single `bindSyncTab` wires the sub-tab nav clicks (which call `setSyncSubTab(id)` then `render()`), a sibling `bindGoogleSubTab` wires Google's credential-form / connect / disconnect / test-connection / clear-log buttons, and `bindICloudSubTab` (lazy-imported) wires the iCloud debug controls.

The Google sub-tab has three render states gated on `_editingGoogleCreds` (module-local) plus `settings.googleClientId` and `settings.googleAccessToken`:

1. **Credential form** — shown when no client id is saved *or* the user clicked "Edit credentials". Two inputs (Client ID, Client Secret) with help text linking to Google Cloud Console and naming the exact redirect URI to register. The secret field shows `••••••••` as a placeholder when a value is stored; saving with that masked value preserves the existing secret rather than blanking it. Save persists both fields via the generic `saveSetting` invoke, then calls `auth.setClientId(id)` to invalidate the in-memory cache. Cancel only appears in edit mode (not on first-time setup).
2. **Saved, not connected** — surfaces the masked client id (first 12 + last 12 chars), a **Connect Google account** button, and an **Edit credentials** affordance.
3. **Connected** — full status block: connection status, connected account email, per-document link count, masked client id, plus Test Connection and Edit credentials. Below: the activity log (capped at 50 entries via `append_google_sync_log` in Rust) and a Disconnect button that calls `revoke_google_tokens` — best-effort POST to `oauth2.googleapis.com/revoke`, then clears tokens + per-document links locally (but **leaves the OAuth credentials intact** so the user can re-connect without re-entering them) so the disconnect is immediate even if Google's revocation endpoint is unreachable.

#### OAuth callback dispatch

Google OAuth rides the Rust loopback HTTP listener: `commands::google_docs::start_google_oauth_listener` spawns the listener, parses the OAuth redirect inline, and emits `oauth-callback` with `provider: "google"`. `handleOAuthCode(state, invoke, code)` in `style-application.js` completes the flow via `google-docs/auth.js`.

### Doc Images (`editor/plugins/image-decorator.js`, `editor/image-preview.js`, `state/state-images.js`)

**Storage model.** Image binaries live at `{data_dir}/files/images/{filename}` under the user's original filename; the Rust `ImageManager` auto-suffixes on collision (`brown-cow.png`, `brown-cow (2).png`, ...). A tree node with `type: "image"` and `fileId === filename` records each one. The Images folder is a pinned special node (`__images__`) in the tail block (Images | PDFs | Archive | Trash) and only accepts image children; `files-panel.js`' `canDrop` and the generic `canDropIntoParent` check in `sortable-list/drag-drop.js` reject any attempt to drag an image out.

**Reference syntax.** Standard markdown — `![alt](filename.png)` — with two extensions: an optional caption after a pipe (`![alt | caption](filename.png)`) renders italic below the image, and URLs containing spaces or parens are wrapped in double quotes (`![alt]("brown-cow (2).png")`) so the parser can unambiguously recover the filename. `IMAGE_MD_RE` in `state-images.js` is the shared regex (bare or quoted URL, capture groups 1=alt, 2=quoted URL, 3=bare URL) consumed by the decorator, the export path, the rename rewriter, and the delete purger so every caller agrees on syntax.

**Inline rendering.** `createImageDecoratorPlugin` replaces a resolved ref with an inline `<img>` wrapper whenever no cursor overlaps it; cursor-inside reveals the raw markdown for editing. CSS enforces `max-width: 100%` (editor column-bounded) and `max-height: 50vh`, and narrower images are centered. Data URLs are lazy-loaded via the Rust `load_image` command and cached by filename. The plugin re-runs `buildDecorations` when `state` fires `files-changed` by dispatching an annotated transaction — this is how a pane opened before the image existed still picks it up when the tree updates.

**Interactions.** Hovering an `image` row in the Files panel shows the full image in a floating tooltip (`attachImageHoverTooltip` in `editor/image-preview.js`; the tooltip hides when its source row leaves the DOM via a coarse (200 ms) `setTimeout` connectivity-watch poll — coarse on purpose, since it only needs to notice the row being removed and a 60 fps rAF for that was pure idle drain). Clicking either the sidebar row or the editor-rendered image opens `openImagePreviewModal`, a centered lightbox dismissed on Escape or backdrop click.

**Rename + delete.** Renaming an image calls `rename_image` in Rust (renames on disk, auto-suffixes on collision, preserves the original extension if the user drops it) and then `rewriteImageRefs` walks every document in the tree, updating both bare and quoted URL forms — including the currently-open editor buffer. Deleting an image (soft or permanent) first runs `removeImageRefs` to purge every matching markdown ref from every doc; solo-line refs consume their line so no blank line is left behind. `findImageNode` also skips the Trash subtree so a manually-typed ref to a trashed image won't resolve.

**Drag routing.** One unified pipeline handles all image transfers:
- *Desktop → doc / notebook*: `editor/file-drop.js` (docs) and the notebook's own drop handler detect image files and save via `save_image`.
- *Sidebar → doc / notebook*: the sortable list's new `forceDragOutside(item)` config hook lets image rows escape the panel without a modifier. `files-panel.js`' `onDragOutside` routes them to `dropSidebarImageAt(filename, x, y)` in `pane/text-drag.js`, which `elementsFromPoint`-scans for a CodeMirror editor or registered notebook canvas and inserts markdown or adds an `ImageShape` accordingly.
- *Doc ↔ pane*: `attachImageDrag` hands the markdown ref to `startTextDrag`; the receiving editor re-decorates it on drop.
- *Doc → notebook via Cmd-drag*: when `startTextDrag`'s drop target is a notebook canvas and the payload is a single local image ref, it resolves the data URL and adds an `ImageShape` instead of a text shape.
- *Notebook → doc*: `attachNotebookImageShapeDrag` initiates a drag with a `{ dataUrl, name, width, height }` image payload; the drop handler invokes `save_image` (reusing the auto-suffix logic), creates an image tree node, and inserts `![alt](filename.png)` at the drop coordinate.
- *Notebook → notebook* with an image payload clones the shape at the drop point. The drag ghost shows a thumbnail for image payloads.

**Export.** `collectImageRefs` walks the current document for local image refs; `export_with_images` (Rust) writes `text.md` + `images/<filename>` into a user-chosen folder, and `rewriteImageRefsForExport` rewrites each ref to the relative `images/` path (quoting URLs when they need it).

### Doc / Project PDF Export (`sidebar/doc-export-modal.js` + `src-tauri/src/typst_export/`)

PDF export for docs and projects runs through Typst, embedded as a Rust crate (`typst 0.14`) inside the Tauri backend. The same code path serves desktop and iOS — no `typst` CLI, no subprocess, no network access at render time. Everything the compiler needs (markdown body, bibliography YAML, image bytes) is fed in through an in-memory `World` implementation; no file actually lands on disk during compilation.

**Frontend modal** (`sidebar/doc-export-modal.js` + `sidebar/doc-export-render.js`). The sidebar's Export button (and the `export-current-file` event) opens this modal whenever a doc or project is the active surface; notebooks still take the existing notebook-export modal. The modal is two columns — controls on the left, a live preview on the right. The modal file owns the UI wiring; the stateless rendering / delivery / format helpers live in `doc-export-render.js` (kept separate to stay under the 700-line cap). For PDF the preview *is* the real Typst render (debounced ~350 ms on any option change) painted into the pane via `pdf.js` (`paintPdfPreview` → `getPdfjs` from `pdf/pdf-viewer.js`); the rendered bytes are cached keyed on the render-relevant options so pressing Export reuses them instead of rendering twice. The non-PDF formats (Markdown / RTF / Google Doc) are all text-shaped — the preview shows the markdown they start from, with citation processing already applied so the preview tracks the Zotero toggles.

Four formats ride a `data-group="format"` segmented control: **Markdown** defers to the established `exportCurrentFile` path (now accepting a `{ content }` override so the citation-processed body flows through the same folder-with-images / iOS-share plumbing); **RTF** runs `markdownToRtf` (`markdown-to-rtf.js`) and saves a `.rtf` via `deliverText`; **Google Doc** (only offered when `hasTokensAsync()` resolves true at modal open) runs `markdownToHtml` → `createDocumentFromHtml` → `openUrl`, a one-shot export that doesn't link the file. **Citation processing** for the three text formats is shared in `export-citations.js` (`applyCitations`) — the JS mirror of `bibliography::inline_citation` / the bibliography emitter: `renderCitations` rewrites `[@key]` to an author-date label, `includeBibliography` appends a sorted `## References` section. The two toggles only appear when Zotero is configured *and* the doc cites something.

The PDF panel pulls its style list from the `list_doc_styles` Tauri command so adding a style is a Rust-only change. A **Header size** slider (60–180 %, `headerScale` 0.6–1.8) threads through `render_doc_pdf` to a `#let header-scale` binding the style preambles multiply their per-level heading sizes by (see `styles.rs`). The **document's first line** — Hush's filename by convention — is stripped from the PDF body by `stripLeadingTitleLine` (frontend, PDF-only) when it normalises-equal to the export name, so the name isn't duplicated on the page. Layout toggles (`Strip comments`, `Strip flags`, `Include tabs`, `Number headings`, `Page numbers`) ride a generic `data-choice` wiring so adding another toggle is HTML-only; the PDF's **Include bibliography** toggle only appears when Zotero is configured *and* the doc contains at least one `[@key]` reference (cheap regex sniff). References are loaded whenever the doc cites anything (not just when the bibliography is on) so inline citations can be formatted either way (see `markdown.rs`). Render hands the bytes to the platform-appropriate sink — `navigator.share` on iOS, `save` dialog + `write_binary_file` on desktop.

**Rust pipeline** (`src-tauri/src/typst_export/`, command surface in `commands/pdf_export.rs`). Six small modules:

- **`preprocess.rs`** — Pre-conversion hygiene passes on the raw markdown. `strip_comments` drops `%% inline %%`, multi-line `%% ... %%`, and `---%`-to-separator/EOF regions (mirrors the editor's `createMultiLineCommentPlugin` and `createCommentAfterPlugin` scopes; honours `%%%` as the same "not a comment" escape). `strip_flags` drops `==FLAG==`, `==FLAG: body==`, and bare `==highlight==` runs. Footnotes (`[^id]` reference + `[^id]:` definition) collapse to a `FOOTNOTE_OPEN…FOOTNOTE_CLOSE` control-char run carrying the definition body, which survives pulldown + the markup-escape pass for `notes.rs` to expand. Imported Google-Docs comments (`{>text<id}` anchor + `[>id]:` definition) are always *stripped* to their commented prose here — comments never appear in the PDF. A shared definition collector handles both note shapes. Hand-written scanners, no regex crate.
- **`markdown.rs`** — Walks the cleaned markdown with `pulldown-cmark` and emits Typst. The two Hush citation shapes (`[@key]` and `[@key](zotero://...)`) are folded into a single sentinel pre-pass so pulldown's split-text events don't lose the bracket framing. Sentinel expansion at the end of the walk is driven by a `CitationMode`: **Resolve** (bibliography on) substitutes `#cite(<key>)` for known keys; **Inline** (bibliography off but references present) substitutes a pre-formatted author-date label built by `bibliography::inline_citation`, emitted as a `#text("…")` expression; **Strip** (no references) leaves the bare key. Either resolved mode renders a bold red `[@key]` marker for keys not in the library so a missing citekey doesn't sink the export. Emphasis, strong, and citations all use Typst's function form (`#emph[…]`, `#strong[…]`, `#cite(<…>)`, `#text("…")`) instead of the whitespace-sensitive markup form (`_…_`, `*…*`, `@key`) so intra-word patterns like `*shape*attention` and cites flush against emphasis don't trigger Typst's "expected comma" / "unclosed delimiter" rules.
- **`notes.rs`** — Final pass: expands the footnote sentinels planted by `preprocess.rs` into real Typst `#footnote[…]` (numbered, collected at the page foot — replacing the old broken `#footnote[see id]` stub that dropped the definition text). Citations/links inside a footnote body have already been expanded to balanced Typst by this point, so the wrapping brackets close cleanly. (Imported comments aren't rendered — they're stripped to prose in `preprocess.rs`.)
- **`bibliography.rs`** — Builds a Hayagriva YAML from the loaded Zotero references. The `type:` mapping is constrained to Hayagriva's closed vocabulary (anything Zotero-specific that doesn't have a slot collapses to `misc`), so the YAML always parses. The bibliography is registered in the World as `/refs.yml` and referenced from the wrapped doc via `#bibliography("/refs.yml", style: …)`, where the style id comes from `csl.rs` (built-in CSL name or a virtual `/style.csl`). Also exports `inline_citation(&ZoteroRef)` — the author-date `(Surname Year)` / `(A and B …)` / `(A et al. …)` label used by `markdown.rs`'s Inline mode when the bibliography is off.
- **`styles.rs`** — `Style` registry plus the `wrap()` composer. A style is a Typst preamble string plus an id and name; `wrap()` emits a `#let header-scale = <value>` binding (clamped 0.5–3.0) **before** the preamble, prepends the preamble, optionally appends `#set page(numbering: "1")` and `#set heading(numbering: "1.1")`, maps the modal's line-spacing toggle to a `#set par(leading: …)`, then the converted body, then the bibliography directive when references are in play — forcing `#set page(columns: 1)` before it so the reference list reads full-width even under a multi-column style. The `header-scale` binding is the **Header size** slider: each preamble sizes its per-level headings as `header-scale * <em>`, so a single multiplier scales every level together (preserving their relationship) while shifting their size relative to the body. `WrapOptions` carries `header_scale` alongside `line_spacing` / `page_numbers` / `number_headings`. Two styles ship: *Formal* (Libertinus Serif, US-letter, white background) and *Article (2 Column)* (two-column, sans-serif Karla); the registry table at the bottom of the file is the one place to add another.
- **`world.rs`** — Minimal in-memory `typst::World`. Holds the main source (`/main.typ`), optionally `/refs.yml` and `/style.csl`, and one virtual file per supplied image. Fonts come from `typst-assets` (`fonts` feature enabled — Libertinus Serif, New Computer Modern, DejaVu Sans Mono) plus Hush's bundled **Karla** sans (`src-tauri/fonts/karla/`, compact variable upright + italic, registered via `extra_font_blobs`) since `typst-assets` ships no proportional sans — so the export doesn't depend on any host font being installed. No package resolution (`@preview/...`) — styles must stay self-contained because the iOS build can't reach the network.

**Project handling.** Projects flow through the same code path. The sidebar replaces `\n---hush-separator---\n` with `\n\n` before handing the joined buffer to the modal, and the markdown converter has a belt-and-braces line filter that drops any separators that slip through. From the renderer's perspective a project is just one continuous markdown document.

**Image handling.** The modal calls `collectImageRefs` (shared with the markdown export path) and passes the filenames to the `render_doc_pdf` command; the Rust side loads bytes through the existing `ImageManager` and stuffs each one into the World keyed at `/<filename>`. The markdown converter rewrites `![alt](images/foo.png)` to `#image("/foo.png", …)` — Typst reads via `World::file`, so nothing touches the actual filesystem during compilation. Missing image refs surface as a Typst diagnostic rather than a panic.

**Error surfacing.** On compile or PDF-emit failure, `render_pdf` dumps the generated `.typ` source to a temp file and includes the path in the error message returned to JS. The dump survives container restarts long enough to grep, and the file:line:col span carried by each diagnostic lines up with that source.

### Floating Panes (`pane/`)

Draggable reference windows that float above the editor or notebook canvas. Created by Cmd-dragging a file from the sidebar files panel past the panel boundary into the editing area. The subsystem is split across nine modules; **`pane-state.js`** is the shared state hub (the `panes` Map + accessors for the lazy notebook bridge, the active pane id, etc.) so siblings can read/mutate without circular imports against `pane-manager.js`.

**`pane-manager.js`** — Lifecycle entry point: create, close, focus, theme/style sync, ratchet lock. Imports the worker modules below; owns the public API (`initPaneManager`, `createPane`, `closePane`, `focusPane`, etc.). The title-bar DOM (icons, buttons, collapse/attach/pin toggles) lives in **`pane-toolbar.js`**, and the positioning helpers (`getInitialPanePosition`, `fitActivePaneToGap`, `centerPaneInViewport`) live in **`pane-layout.js`** — both extracted to keep `pane-manager.js` under the line limit. `pane-toolbar.js` receives `{ closePane, focusPane, createPane, getCurrentContext, onContextChange }` from `pane-manager.js` at build time so it doesn't need a back-import.

**`pane-editor.js`** — Factory that calls `createBaseExtensions()` from `editor/editor.js` so pane editors share the identical plugin, shortcut, and theme setup as the main editor. Accepts an optional `opts.modeContext` — a mode proxy from `createModeContext()` — which is passed to `createBaseExtensions` and `createDryHighlightPlugin` so all plugins read per-editor mode flags instead of the global AppState. When a mode context is present, the global `mode-changed` event only nudges the CM view (for theme/settings propagation); typewriter and mode flags are managed by the context's `toggle()`. Exposes `setEditable(bool)` via an `EditorView.editable` compartment — inactive panes are locked non-editable to prevent input leaks. Also exports `applyPaneTypewriterFromContext(view, modeRef, container)` for mode-context toggling of typewriter DOM side-effects.

**`pane-content.js`** — Load + save + bidirectional sync with the main editor / notebook canvas. Includes `loadDocumentPane`, `loadNotebookPane`, `savePaneContent`, `autosaveAllPanes`, and the four `syncDoc*` / `syncNotebook*` functions. The sync flag is held in pane-state and protected by try/finally guards so a thrown handler can't permanently jam the channel.

**`pane-attach-sync.js`** — Anchoring loop. `startCanvasSync` drives a per-frame `requestAnimationFrame` that converts the pane's canvas world coords to screen coords through the active camera (also applying `transform: scale(zoom)` so the pane shrinks/grows with the surrounding shapes); it skips the pane's DOM writes when the computed position + zoom are unchanged, so a static canvas doesn't force a layout recalc every frame (and `startStackPinSync` does the same, which also keeps its per-frame `getBoundingClientRect` reads cheap by leaving layout clean). `startScrollSync` listens to `scrollDOM.scroll` for doc-mode panes. `stopAttachSync` cleans up both flavours.

**`pane-drag.js`** — `setupPaneDrag` (titlebar) + `setupPaneResize` (edge / corner handles). Notebook-attached panes translate screen deltas into world coords via the camera zoom. **Option/Alt-dragging the title bar clones the pane**: the drag reads `getCurrentContext` to spawn a duplicate via `createPane(..., { allowDuplicate: true, skipFocus: true })`, then — because `createPane` runs its DOM build + `panes.set` synchronously before its first `await` — captures the fresh pane out of the `panes` Map (key-set diff) *in the same tick*. It copies the original's size + `fontSize` onto the clone (`applyPaneFontSize`), starts it exactly over the original, and makes the clone (not the original) the `dragTarget` the rest of the gesture moves/docks/focuses. The original's z is bumped *before* the clone's (`zForPane` is a monotonic counter) so the clone rides above it.

**PDF panes & suspend/resume (`pdf/pdf-viewer-suspend.js`).** Off-context PDF panes are suspended to a JPEG snapshot and their pdf.js doc is torn down; the raw bytes are kept in `cachedPdfData` and `resume()` rebuilds from them. pdf.js's `getDocument` **takes ownership of (neuters) the TypedArray it's handed**, so resume passes a throwaway `new Uint8Array(cachedPdfData)` copy rather than the master — otherwise the first resume detaches the cache and every later resume renders an empty frame ("survives one reopen, never more"). The restore-time render (`loadPdfPane`) also `.catch()`es a bad-binary throw so one corrupt PDF can't abort the whole pane-restore loop.

**`pane-size-popover.js`** — Per-pane font-size override via a CSS custom property on the pane root that CodeMirror's `hushTheme` reads. The "A" affordance in the titlebar opens a popover with `−` / `+` (Cmd-click applies to all open panes).

**`pane-persistence.js`** — Serialise the open pane set into `AppSettings.persistedPanes` on a 300 ms debounce; restore on app start. `restorePanes` takes `buildPaneDOM` + `onContextChange` callbacks as deps so it doesn't have to import pane-manager.js (which would be circular). Beyond the obvious geometry / anchoring / pinned fields, the persisted record carries `editorScrollTop` (the per-pane CodeMirror scroll offset) so reopening a pane lands the reader where they left off — `pane-content.js` wires a 200 ms-debounced scroll listener via the editor's `onScroll` API and applies the saved offset on next mount one rAF after `setContent` has populated the buffer.

**Pane object:** `{ id, fileId, fileName, fileType, collapsed, attached, pinned, dirty, editor, notebook, el, width, height, x, y, ownerContext, localSync, zotero, editorScrollTop, pdfZoomLevel, pdfScrollLeft, notebookCamera }`. `fileType` is one of `"document"`, `"notebook"`, `"pdf"`, `"stack"`, or `"zotero-highlights"`. The trailing view-state fields round-trip through `.hush/panes.json`: `editorScrollTop` (doc + PDF vertical scroll), `pdfZoomLevel` / `pdfScrollLeft` (PDF fit-or-fixed zoom mode + horizontal scroll), and `notebookCamera` (per-pane pan + zoom, kept separate from the notebook file's own camera). The `zotero-highlights` type is a fileless pane (no underlying tree node, no `editor` / `notebook` instance) used by the Zotero highlight browser; the `zotero` field carries `{ itemKey, attKey, title, authors, year }` once the user has chosen an attachment. See "Highlight browser pane" under Zotero Integration for the load / persist branches.

**Attach vs Pin:**

- **Attach** — Anchors the pane to content. In notebooks, converts screen position to canvas world coordinates and syncs every frame via `requestAnimationFrame`. In docs, records `scrollRelY` (pane Y + scrollTop) and updates on the editor's scroll event. Dragging a canvas-attached pane converts screen deltas to canvas deltas (dividing by zoom).
- **Pin** — Marks the pane as global (`.pinned` class — blue **border** with a thin matching shadow ring; header coloring stays normal). Pinned panes stay visible across all document switches. Unpinning triggers `onContextChange()` so the pane returns to its original context. Attach and pin are mutually exclusive — toggling one while the other is active shows a confirmation dialog.

**Duplicate** — Creates a new pane for the same file with `ownerContext` set to the current document (not the source's context). The duplicate check in `createPane` scopes by context, so the same file can have panes in different documents.

**Content sync:** Document panes fire `syncDocFromPane` on every `docChanged`, pushing content to the main editor if the same file is open (with `_syncPulling` flag to suppress `markDirty`). The reverse direction uses a `doc-content-changed` event from `main.js`. Notebook panes sync shapes via `loadShapes()` with a `_syncing` guard reset via double `queueMicrotask` to account for `DrawingState`'s batched change events.

**Input isolation:** The notebook's window `keydown` and document `paste` handlers skip processing when `document.activeElement` is inside a `.floating-pane`. Inactive pane content gets `pointer-events: none` via CSS, and the editor is set to non-editable. A window-level capture-phase `pointerdown` listener deactivates panes when clicking outside.

**Z-index layering:** `#pane-container` is `z-index: var(--z-pane)` (90 — above editor content at 0–80, below sidebars). The notebook container has no z-index to avoid creating a stacking context, allowing the shelf panel (`var(--z-shelf)` — 150) to render above panes. The full scale is documented in `base.css`; see "CSS Structure" below.

**Gutter pane (`project/gutter.js`).** Promotes a notebook pane into a docked, doc-aligned sidebar (right by default; the header's `<->` toggle flips it left/right via `toggleGutterSide`, persisted as `gutterSide`). **Geometry is owned by the dock module** (`pane-dock.js`): `useActivePaneAsGutter` calls `dockPane(pane, side)`, so the pane is viewport-sized and fixed (full height, flush against the docked edge) and the doc text column carves space for it via the standard `--pane-dock-{left,right}-width` machinery; `stopActivePaneAsGutter` calls `undockPane`, and `restoreGutterLayout` re-docks. The scroll alignment is carried by the notebook's camera on top of that — `syncCameraFromScroll` sets `state.camera = { x: previous.x, y: _gutterOffset - scrollTop, zoom: 1 }` on every scroll tick, where `_gutterOffset = (scrollerRect.top + paddingTop) - (pane.y + TITLEBAR_HEIGHT)` aligns world-y inside the canvas with doc-content-y. With `camera.zoom = 1`, world-y in the canvas equals doc-content-y; the stroke engine's coverage logic (`vpTop = -cam.y / cam.zoom`) sees the correct world rect, so re-anchor never drifts. (`pane-dock.js` no longer tears gutter mode down when docking — gutter *is* a dock now; persistence skips its generic dock-restore since `restoreGutterLayout` owns it.) Earlier attempts at a doc-tall pane DOM that moved with scroll re-triggered the engine's re-anchor on every scroll-induced canvas resize and accumulated floating-point drift over long sessions.

**Pairing is per-doc (`project/gutter-commands.js`).** A gutter belongs to a single **document**, not the project. The pairing rides a **naming convention** — the gutter notebook is created as `<docName>-gutter` inside the doc's project — so `reapplyGutterMarkers` (tree-helpers) re-derives it from names on every render by matching a notebook `<docName>-gutter` to a sibling doc `<docName>`, stamping `gutter` + `gutterForDoc`. That makes the pairing survive the `get_file_tree` round trip, restart, and the `.hushproject` envelope with no dedicated metadata field. `assignedGutterNotebook(state)` resolves the current doc's gutter via the in-memory `state.gutterAssignments` map (keyed by `docFileId`) first, falling back to the naming convention and seeding the map; `markGutterForDoc(docFileId, notebookFileId)` (state-project) records both. The gutter pane binds to the `doc:<fileId>` context, so opening that doc shows doc + gutter while the project's combined buffer (`pj:`) shows none. **Add Gutter** packages a standalone doc into a project (`convertDocToProject`), opens the resulting child doc, then creates + docks `<docName>-gutter`; **Add notebook as gutter** seeds it from a chosen notebook's content. Once a doc has a gutter the palette swaps those for **Open Gutter** (`promoteNotebookPaneAsGutter`) / **Close Gutter** (`closePane`, pairing kept). Predicates `gutterAddHidden` / `gutterOpenHidden` / `gutterCloseHidden` key off `assignedGutterNotebook` and `liveGutterPane` (a gutter pane in the active `doc:` context). The sidebar renders the gutter notebook directly beneath its doc as a standalone row (its own dropdown intact): `getIcon` returns `typeIcons.gutter` (the notebook dot-grid with its left/right dot columns swapped for vertical rules), the row label is forced to "Gutter" (in both the main tree's `renderItem` and the flagged-section render), `normalizeProjectChildren` nests it right after its paired doc (by `gutterForDoc`), and a `data-gutter` attribute drives the CSS that dims the row to 0.5, shrinks the filename, and indents the row 16px so the project's doc-connector column stays clear on its left. Because the gutter sits between two joined docs, the `document + document` I-beam connector can't span it — the gutter row paints its own vertical stem at the same x (`left:30px`) reaching up into the gap above (always a doc) and, via `:has(+ .sl-item[data-type="document"])`, down into the gap below only when a doc follows, so the joined-doc line reads continuously alongside the indented gutter. Pane indicators move to the gutter row when a doc has one. A gutter pane never shows a square of its own; instead the **gutter row is where the doc's *other* panes are painted, inline after the "Gutter" label**. `paneIndicatorsFor` (files-panel-pane-indicators) special-cases a gutter notebook item: it pulls the paired doc's panes (`getPanesForContext("doc:"+gutterForDoc)`), drops the gutter (`!p.gutter`), and returns a `.tree-pane-indicators-inline` strip that `renderItem` appends *inside* the `.tree-item-name` span (directly after the text, not shoved right by the name's `flex:1`). The doc's own below-row strip is suppressed whenever it has a paired gutter (`hasPairedGutter` scans the doc's project siblings for a `gutter`-stamped notebook with `gutterForDoc === fileId`), so its panes show only on the gutter row; a doc *without* a gutter renders its strip below the row as before. Both paths filter `!p.gutter`, so a gutter pane is never drawn anywhere. `useActivePaneAsGutter` / `stopActivePaneAsGutter` emit `panes-changed` after flipping `pane.gutter` (the flag changes *after* createPane's own emit, so without this the doc keeps a stale square). The `gutter` flag is surfaced on the pane summary alongside `pinned` to make the split possible; the panel re-renders on `panes-changed`, so squares track the live pane set.

**Per-scroll cost.** The scroll handler still calls `scrollerRect = scroller.getBoundingClientRect()` every event — that read is cheap and, crucially, *flushes CodeMirror's pending measure pass* so `lineBlockAt(line.from).top` reads inside `scanDocHeaders` see the freshly measured lines that just scrolled into view. The expensive part — the cascade walk inside `getComputedStyle` for `paddingTop` — is cached on `pane._gutterPadTop` and invalidated only on window resize and `doc-content-changed` (typewriter / sticky-header plugins can shift the scroller's padding on doc edits). An earlier optimization that cached the rect too dropped shadow headers entirely: removing the per-scroll bounding-rect read meant CM's height-map was stale by the time the rAF callback ran the scan, so newly-revealed headers got y=0 and rendered off-screen.

**Scroll-handler rAF throttle.** The scroll listener installed by `startGutterSync` coalesces to one sync per animation frame and bails on no-op `scrollTop` deltas (`if (st === lastSyncedScrollTop) return;`). Without that, every scroll event drives `syncCameraFromScroll` → `state.notify("camera")` → the drawing layer's `setCamera` → potentially `fullRebake()` (O(N strokes)). On a notebook with many strokes the rebakes pile up inside one frame and show as jumpy / laggy scroll, especially under iOS momentum which fires many scroll events per frame. The cap lets the browser's scroll compositing carry the visual gap of the in-between frames — the canvas catches up on the next frame at worst.

**Restore-path fixes.** `loadNotebookPane`'s post-load `requestAnimationFrame` (the one that centres the canvas on the same point the main notebook view would show) early-returns when `pane.gutter` is set. Previously it overwrote the gutter's careful `state.camera` after both fresh and restored gutter setup, and on restore (no scroll event to undo it) the per-header bounds check in `drawShadowHeaders` (`y < -40 || y > canvasH + 4`) culled every header off-screen — symptom: "shadow headers stopped appearing after I closed and reopened the app." `restoreGutterLayout` also fires three timed retries at 100 / 500 / 1500 ms that re-invalidate the pad-top cache, recompute the gutter offset, re-sync the camera, and re-scan — the editor's scrollDOM rect can still be settling when the synchronous setup runs, so the initial offset is computed against a half-laid-out scroller. Without the retries the camera stayed stuck at the wrong y until the user scrolled.

**Save-side gutter-camera shield.** `savePaneContent` swaps `pane.notebook.state.camera` for `pane._gutterPrev.camera` when serialising a gutter pane's `.hushnote` file. The live `state.camera.y` while in gutter mode is `_gutterOffset - scrollTop` — a scroll-tied value that's meaningless as a "saved viewport." Persisting it would put a scroll-driven y into the file, and the next time the notebook is opened via the main canvas (where `notebook-bridge.js` does restore `snapshot.camera`) the canvas would seed at that bogus position. The `_gutterPrev` snapshot — taken at gutter-enter — round-trips the pane's actual pre-gutter camera instead.

**`applyAnchors` skips `DrawShape`.** Stroke shapes (`type: "draw"`) store their geometry in `points[]` and have no `position` field. The anchor walk early-returns with `if (!s.position) continue;` so the first stroke in the array can't throw `TypeError: undefined is not an object (evaluating 's.position.y')` and abort `scanAndSync`. Before the guard, any notebook brought back from disk with at least one stroke (the "previously gutter + closed + has content" repro) would crash the scan before `publishShadowHeaders` ran, leaving `state.shadowHeaders = []` and no faded headings — exactly the symptom users reported.

**Geometry / resize.** Now that the gutter is a docked pane (left or right per `gutterSide`), the dock module owns its box — width is the standard dock width (resize from the inboard edge), height is full, and window / sidebar / chrome resizes re-flex it through `pane-dock.js`'s reflow listeners. `gutter.js`'s own resize hook re-runs `applyDockGeometry(pane)` and then re-derives the camera offset so the scroll alignment stays correct after the geometry settles.

Input redirect is gated by `state.gutterScrollDOM` (set to the live cm-scroller while gutter is active, nulled on exit / context-switch / teardown). `handleWheel`, the pointer-pan branch in `handlePointerMove`, the two-finger touchmove handler in `input-handler.ts`, and the drawing engine's `onTouchPanMove` / `onTouchPinchMove` callbacks in `notes-canvas.ts` all check the flag and: write `scrollDOM.scrollTop` for vertical movement (with the start-of-gesture scrollTop captured at pointerdown / touchstart), update `camera.x` for horizontal movement, and re-set `camera.y = -scrollDOM.scrollTop` atomically so there's no inconsistent frame between the scroll write and the scroll listener's camera update. Pinch is disabled — wheel events with `ctrlKey=true` (Mac trackpad pinch synthesis) are dropped. `focusShape` (shelf row click) computes `target = scrollerRect.top + cy - viewportH/2` and snaps `scrollTop` to it (instant, not smooth — `behavior: "smooth"` fights any user gesture that arrives mid-animation).

**Shadow headers.** `scanDocHeaders()` walks `view.state.doc` line-by-line, regex-matches `^(#{1,6})\s+(\S.*)$`, and reads each line's y via `view.lineBlockAt(line.from).top`.
