The list is pushed onto `state.shadowHeaders` and rendered by `drawShadowHeaders` (in `renderer-selection.ts`) **outside** the camera transform — `drawShadowHeaders` takes `camera.y` as an argument and computes `canvasY = cameraY + h.y` so the text scrolls vertically with the doc while staying fixed horizontally at `SHADOW_HEADER_X` (35 px = 15 px inboard of the gutter rule). A 1 px horizontal rule sits 4 px above each header (starting at the same inset) so the section break reads at a glance.

**Gutter orientation rule.** The gutter pane's *edge* is now styled like any other docked pane (no red border, no `::before` overlay). The orientation cue is instead a 2 px red vertical line drawn on the canvas by `renderer.ts` when `state.gutter` (set from `gutterScrollDOM` in the render snapshot): screen-x = `20 * camera.zoom + camera.x`, full canvas height. Because it's anchored to world-x = 20 it **pans horizontally with the canvas** (and spans the full height regardless of scroll), so at the default pan it reads ~20 px in from the left edge. The shadow headers sit 15 px inboard of it but stay fixed in screen space for now (they don't follow horizontal pan — that coupling is expected to change later).

**Anchor-based shape reflow.** Each shape stores `{ idx, offset, lastY }` against the header it sits under in `pane._shapeAnchors`. On every `scanAndSync` (the rAF-debounced wrapper), shape positions are derived as `currentHeaders[idx].y + offset` — anchors are *re-attached* on the fly when a shape has no record yet, when the shape's current y differs from `lastY` (a manual drag), or when the header count changes (the entire anchor map is dropped). Critically, shape positions are always computed from the **current** header y, not from accumulated deltas — that's what made the earlier delta-based approach drift as CodeMirror's `lineBlockAt(...).top` values refined under scrolling.

**When to rescan.** `scheduleSync(pane)` is called on scroll, on `doc-content-changed`, on window resize, and on gutter-enter / restore. The rAF debounce coalesces scroll storms to one scan per frame. Rescanning on scroll (not just on doc edits) is what keeps the header positions accurate as CM measures previously-off-screen lines for the first time.

**Toolbar integration.** There is no longer a per-pane "Use as Gutter" header button — gutter is owned by the `.hushproject` flow and entered through the command palette (**Add Gutter** / **Add notebook as gutter**), both of which pair the gutter notebook to its doc inside a project. After every enter / exit / restore, `syncGutterButton(pane)` still flips `fp-btn-disabled` on the pin button (pin and gutter are mutually exclusive); the call no-ops gracefully when no gutter button is present. A gutter pane's header is otherwise pared down via the `.floating-pane.gutter` CSS: the **pin** and **attach** buttons are `display: none` (a gutter can't be either), title-bar **dragging** is short-circuited in `setupPaneDrag` (`if (pane.gutter) return;` in the pointer-down handler — geometry is doc-driven), and a **`<->` side toggle** (`fp-btn-gutter-side`, built for every notebook pane but only shown under `.floating-pane.gutter`) calls `toggleGutterSide(pane)` in `gutter.js` to flip the dock between the left and right edge. The flip re-docks via `dockPane(pane, side)` (which preserves `dockUserSize`, so width carries across), swaps the `gutter-left` / `gutter-right` classes, and re-derives the camera offset — the alignment math depends only on the pane's y (both edges are full-height docks at the same y), so the scroll lockstep survives the side change.

**Z and stacking.** `zForPane(pane)` returns a fixed low `GUTTER_Z = 100` for gutter panes so they sit beneath every other floating pane regardless of focus order; free-floating panes still bump the global z counter (starts at 1000+). Persistence rides on `persistedPanes`: `gutter`, `gutterSide`, and `gutterPrev` (the snapshot used to restore the pane's pre-gutter geometry) are serialised alongside the existing fields. `restoreGutterLayout` reads `gutterSide` (defaulting to `"right"`) so a gutter the user flipped to the left re-docks on the left after a restart, applies the matching `gutter-left` / `gutter-right` class + `dockPane(pane, side)`, and re-points `gutterScrollDOM` at the live scroller after a context switch / restore so the redirect can't outlive its target.

**Locked styles.** When the user runs the command-palette **Lock style to document** entry on a doc or notebook, its tree node stores a `lockedStyleId` (the active style at that moment, or `"__default__"` when no style was active). A pane whose `fileId` resolves to a file with a locked style applies that style scoped to the pane element (theme compartment reconfigure for the CodeMirror instance; theme resolve + `HUSH_TO_NOTEBOOK_THEME` lookup for notebook panes) rather than the session-active style. Pane creation and `file-opened` updates both consult the locked style; `style-changed` events only affect panes whose file is unlocked. The scoping lives on `.floating-pane[data-locked-style]` via CSS custom-property overrides so the main editor's style is untouched.

**Drag-from-sidebar integration:** `sortable-list/drag-drop.js` has an `onDragOutside(item, x, y)` callback. In `finishDrag`, the callback fires instead of the normal reorder drop when the pointer is right of the panel overlay AND either Cmd/Ctrl is held (real `metaKey`/`ctrlKey` OR the virtual `window.__hushCmdHeld` flag from Touch mode's ⌘ pill) OR `forceDragOutside(item)` returns true. `files-panel.js` uses the modifier path for documents and notebooks (creating a floating pane via `createPane()`) and `forceDragOutside` for image rows so they escape the panel without a modifier — `onDragOutside` then calls `dropSidebarImageAt` in `pane/text-drag.js` to insert markdown into the editor or add an `ImageShape` to the notebook under the pointer. `canDropIntoParent` also runs `canDrop` for sibling reorders and root-level drops (not just "drop into" targets), which is how the images-stay-in-Images rule is enforced. A **null target means the root list**: in single-desk mode that's the active desk's own children, so `canDrop` allows folders / projects / docs / notebooks / stacks to land there as Inbox siblings (`return !isAllDesksMode(state)`); in all-desks mode the root is the list of desks, so only desk rows may drop there.

### Sticky Notes (`sticky/sticky-notes.js`)

Square temporary reminders floating above every other surface in a dedicated `#sticky-container` (appended to `document.body`) at `--z-sticky: 250` — above panes (90), the notebook shelf (150), and the sidebar (200); below overlays, popovers, and modals. Stickies are **not files**: they live entirely in `AppSettings.stickyNotes` (mirrored as `sticky_notes: Vec<serde_json::Value>` in the Rust struct, opaque to Rust like `persisted_panes`), never appear in the file tree, and closing one deletes it permanently.

**Note record:** `{ id, kind, target, x, y, width, height, collapsed, fontSize, text, createdAt }`. `kind` is one of `"file"` / `"project"` / `"desk"` / `"global"`; `target` is the pane-style context id (`doc:` / `nb:` / `pdf:` / `st:` + fileId) for file notes, a project tree-node id for project notes, a desk node id for desk notes, and null for global. Persistence runs through `state.updateSettings({ stickyNotes })` on a 500 ms debounce (drag end, resize end, collapse, font change, text input).

**Palette entries** (`command-palette-commands.js`, gated by the exported `canAddFileSticky` / `canAddProjectSticky` predicates): **Add File Sticky** (a doc / notebook / PDF / stack is open), **Add Project Sticky** (a project — or a file whose ancestors include one — is open; the target resolves via `nearestAncestorProjectId`), **Add Desk Sticky** (targets `state.getActiveDesk()`), **Add Global Sticky** (always). New notes cascade from the viewport centre and focus their textarea immediately.

**Visibility** re-evaluates on `file-opened`, `notebook-open`/`-unmount`, `pdf-open`/`-unmount`, `stack-open`/`-unmount`, `active-desk-changed`, and `files-changed`: global notes always show; desk notes show while their desk is active; project notes show when `currentProjectId` matches or the open file's `findAncestorIds` path contains the target; file notes show when the current context id matches. `files-changed` also refreshes the context-button tooltips (they track renames) and prunes notes whose target no longer exists anywhere in the tree — a file in Trash still exists, so its sticky merely hides until restore or permanent delete; the restore path applies the same pruning so orphans don't survive a restart.

**Interaction** mirrors panes where it makes sense: header drag (PointerCapture), double-click header to collapse to the 35 px title bar (width kept; expand restores the resized height), pointerdown raises the note to the top of the sticky z-band via a module z-counter, and eight `sn-resize` handles (same geometry as `fp-resize`) resize between 120 px and the 300 × 300 default/max. The body is a plain `<textarea>` in `--ui-font-family` with fixed black text on the scope colour (file/project `#ffe4ec` pink, desk `#fff6c4` yellow, global `#dcebfa` blue) — deliberately independent of the active theme. A keydown handler on the note element catches `⌘+` / `⌘−` (steps `fontSize` by 2 between 10–48 px) before the window-level shortcut fallback.

**Header** carries no text label — the scope colour already signals the attachment. Instead the left slot is a `.sticky-note-context` button showing the current scope's glyph (`CTX_ICON`: document / desk / globe; project borrows the document glyph); `labelFor(note)` rides as its `title` tooltip. Clicking it opens a `.sticky-context-menu` (appended to `#sticky-container`, since the note is `overflow:hidden`, and positioned to the button with an on-screen nudge) offering **Document** / **Desk** / **App**; `contextAvailable` disables Document / Desk when there's no `currentFileContext` / active desk, `contextKeyMatches` marks the current one, and picking one runs `changeContext` — swap the `sticky-<kind>` class, repoint `kind` + `target`, refresh the glyph + visibility, persist. The middle slot is a `.sticky-note-excerpt` flex spacer (keeps the `×` flush right); its text — `excerptFor(text)`, the first ≤42 chars on one line — is `visibility:hidden` until the note is `.collapsed`, giving a peek at the content of a collapsed sticky without crowding the buttons.

### Zotero Integration (`zotero.js`, `zotero-snapshot.js`, `zotero-annotations.js`, `zotero/highlight-pane.js`)

Citation management plus a highlight browser pane. Connects to the Zotero Web API with a user key, downloads references (and their attachments) with progress tracking, caches the result locally. The shared fuzzy search (`fuzzySearch` in `zotero.js`) is matched against title / shortTitle / authors / year / item key. Both the top-level items and the attachment pages are fetched through `zoteroFetch`, which retries transient failures (429 / 5xx) with exponential backoff instead of the old silent `break` — a single failed attachment page used to drop every attachment after it, so items further down a large library lost their PDFs and the link menu couldn't offer *Download to Hush*. Each cached attachment records `{ key, title, filename, contentType, isPdf }`, with `isPdf` computed by `isPdfAttachment` (content type, else a `.pdf` filename/title) so attachments with a missing or unusual content type still register as PDFs.

Two surfaces consume the cache:

- **Insert reference modal** (`openZoteroModal` in `zotero.js`, palette label "Zotero: Insert reference", default `⌘⇧I`) — modal that picks an item or attachment and inserts a `[Title](zotero://...)` link, with an optional page anchor and optional PDF snapshot.
- **Highlight browser pane** (`openZoteroHighlightPane` in `zotero/highlight-pane.js`, palette label "Zotero: Create highlight browser") — a new pane fileType, see "Highlight browser pane" below.

**Insertion contexts.** A single helper, `resolveInsertContext(view, notebookTextHandle, state)`, decides where the citation lands:

1. **Active notebook text-shape edit** — modal captures the inline textarea handle on open (see "Notebook text shapes" below), routes citation through `TextEditor.insertAtSelection()`.
2. **Notebook canvas with no active edit** — `state.currentNotebookFileId` takes priority over the always-mounted-but-hidden CodeMirror view, so inserts here drop a new `TextShape` at the viewport centre rather than silently landing in the hidden doc.
3. **Doc** — `view.dispatch({ changes })` at the cursor.

**Notebook text shapes.** Opening the search modal blurs the inline textarea overlay (`notebook/ui/text-editor.ts`), which would normally commit the shape and tear down the editor before the citation could be inserted. `text-editor.ts` exports `suspendCommitOnBlur()` / `resumeCommitOnBlur()` / `insertAtSelection(text)` plus a `getActiveNotebookTextEditor()` accessor (also mirrored on `window.__activeNotebookTextEditor` for synchronous lookup from focus-stealing UI like the command palette). The modal suspends the commit on open, inserts the citation through the textarea handle on confirm, and resumes blur-commit on close.

**PDF snapshots.** When a PDF attachment is selected, the detail panel reveals an **Insert snapshot** checkbox + page selector. On confirm, `renderPdfPage()` in `zotero-snapshot.js` lazy-loads `pdfjs-dist`, rasterizes the chosen page to a `<canvas>` at the configured render height, and emits a WebP data URL via `canvas.toDataURL("image/webp", quality)`. The result is inserted alongside the citation:

- **Notebook (edit or canvas)** — the data URL is embedded directly on a new `ImageShape` placed flush to the right of the text. It does *not* go through `createImageFromDataUrl` — the bytes already round-trip inside the notebook's JSON envelope, and surfacing every snapshot in the global Images folder felt like clutter. A Cmd-drag from the canvas into a doc still promotes the shape via `text-drag.js`.
- **Doc** — `state.createImageFromDataUrl(dataUrl, filename)` saves the binary to `Images/`, returns the (possibly auto-suffixed) final filename, and the modal inserts `![alt](filename.webp)` after the citation. Filenames containing whitespace/parens (collisions emit `name 2.webp`) are wrapped in double quotes per the convention enforced by `IMAGE_MD_RE`, otherwise the doc image decoder silently ignores the markdown.

**PDF download.** Zotero's `/users/{id}/items/{key}/file` endpoint returns a 302 to a presigned S3 URL whose CORS policy rejects the webview's `null` origin, so a webview-side fetch fails after the redirect. The `download_zotero_pdf` Tauri command (`commands/zotero.rs`) does the request server-side via `reqwest` (with redirect following enabled), persists the bytes to `{data_dir}/zotero_pdfs/{itemKey}.pdf` keyed on a sanitised attachment id, and returns them. Subsequent renders of other pages from the same paper read from the cache without re-fetching.

**Settings.** The Zotero tab carries credentials (User ID + API key) plus a **PDF Snapshots** group: render height (default 1500 px), display height on canvas (default 300 px), and WebP quality (default 90, 1–100). All three round-trip through `AppSettings` (`zotero_snapshot_render_height`, `zotero_snapshot_display_height`, `zotero_snapshot_quality`).

**Highlight browser pane.** A third pane fileType — `"zotero-highlights"` — surfaces a paper's PDF annotations next to the editor. The pane is fileless: it has no underlying tree node, so `pane-content.js` skips the doc/notebook load path and calls `mountZoteroHighlightPane(pane, appState)` instead, `pane-manager.js::buildPaneDOM` skips the font-size button and the title-link's open-in-main-view handler, and `savePaneContent` is a no-op for the type. The chosen attachment is persisted on `pane.zotero = { itemKey, attKey, title, authors, year }` and serialised by `pane-persistence.js` so a restart lands directly back in annotations mode.

The pane has three internal modes that swap the body:

1. **Search** — fuzzy search over the local reference cache (`loadReferences` + `fuzzySearch` re-exported from `zotero.js`).
2. **Pick attachment** — only entered when the chosen item has 2+ PDFs. With exactly one PDF the pane jumps straight to mode 3; with zero, the search step shows an inline message.
3. **Annotations** — header with a Zotero deep-link title (clickable, opens the PDF at page 1), authors/year, a back arrow, and a refresh button. The back arrow (`zh-back-arrow-btn`) sits to the left of `↻`, shares its pill style, and on click clears `pane.zotero`, restores the default title, persists, then renders mode 1 again so a different paper can be picked. Below the header is an annotation search input, then a two-column body: a 30 px column of 15 px color swatches (with an empty-circle "All" filter at top), and the filtered annotation list. Empty-text annotations (typically ink / image annotations) are filtered out — those don't surface usefully here and would otherwise dilute the color buckets.

Each annotation row's `p. N` label is itself a Zotero deep link. Tauri webviews don't navigate plain anchors with custom schemes, so an `attachExternalLinkHandler` helper intercepts the click and routes through `@tauri-apps/plugin-opener::openUrl` (with a `window.open` fallback for browser dev). Drag-out reuses `pane/text-drag.js::startTextDrag` — `pointerdown` on a row formats the annotation as a markdown blockquote with comment + a `zotero://open-pdf?page=N` citation suffix and hands it to the existing pipeline. A re-entrancy guard on `_fetching` prevents overlapping network calls during rapid refreshes / reloads.

**Annotation cache.** `src/zotero-annotations.js` exposes `getAnnotations(attKey, userId, apiKey, { forceRefresh })` and `groupByColor(annotations)`. The fetch goes through the `fetch_zotero_annotations` Tauri command (server-side, paginated), which hits `/users/{id}/items/{attKey}/children?itemType=annotation` — note the `/children` endpoint, not `/items?parentItem=…`; the latter doesn't actually scope by parent and returns the entire library. Results are cached at `{data_dir}/zotero_annotations/{attKey}.json` via `save_zotero_annotations`, and `load_zotero_annotations` returns `Option<String>` so a missing cache is distinct from an empty array. The pane reads cache-first and only re-fetches when the user hits `↻`.

### PDF Shelf, Covers & Project Aliases (`pdf/pdf-shelf.js`, `pdf/pdf-covers.js`, `state/state-pdf-aliases.js`)

**Shelf.** `openPdfShelf(state, containerId)` takes over the editor area with a card grid of the PDFs in a PDFs folder — either the desk special (`__pdfs__:<deskId>`) or a project's own `pdfFolder` folder. Same takeover pattern as the multi-select view: one host div in `#app`, a `pdf-shelf-active` body class that `visibility: hidden`s the editor / notebook / PDF / stack / empty-state containers so closing is a class flip. Both takeover views track the inset sidebar in pure CSS — `#panel-overlay.panel-inset:not(.hidden) ~ .pdf-shelf-view { left: var(--panel-width) }` (grip width when hidden), so grip resizes reflow live without a JS observer. Opened from the PDFs folder row's dedicated shelf button (`tree-action-shelf` in `renderRowMenuButton`, icon from `temp/temp-icons/shelf-icon.svg`, dispatching `view-shelf` → lazy-import); closed by Esc, the header button, any file-open event, or a 2+ sidebar multi-select (the listing view wins). Cards read title / year / authors from the pdf-sync registry (falling back to the node name), no-op while a download is pending, and route clicks through `state.openPdf` — whose `pdf-open` event is also what closes the shelf. A header **sort dropdown** orders cards by title / author (ascending) or added / opened date (descending; `openedAt` is stamped on the registry entry by `touchPdfOpened` in `openPdf`, `addedAt` prefers a project alias's own stamp). Cards carry a **shelf-local multi-select** (cmd-click toggle, shift-click range over the sorted order — deliberately *not* `state.selectedDocIds`, which would summon the multi-select view over the shelf); 2+ selected surfaces **Create Stack from Selected** in the header, and Esc clears the selection before it closes the shelf. `createStackFromSelected` files the new stack under the owning project (`findParentOfNode` of the `pdfFolder`) when the shelf is a project shelf, else passes `null` for the desk Inbox default. **Search / results view.** `render()` builds a stable header (scope label + search input + sort + actions) once; only the `.pdf-shelf-body` below it re-renders per keystroke (`renderBody()` bumps `_renderToken`), so the input keeps focus without the old live-DOM filtering trick. An empty query renders the thumbnail grid (`renderGrid`); a non-empty query renders the **results view** (`renderSearchResults`): one `.pdf-shelf-result` row per matching PDF — a compact `.pdf-shelf-card` (thumbnail + metadata, same cover hydration path) on the left, a `.pdf-shelf-result-matches` list on the right built from `collectMatches(card, q)` (Title / Author / Year, then bookmark names with their page, then annotation text + comments with `pageLabel`), each match `highlightHtml`-wrapped so the term shows in a `<mark class="pdf-shelf-hl">`. A bookmark-match row click routes through `openPdfAtBookmark`, an annotation-match row with a page through `requestPdfJump` + `openPdf`, and the card itself opens at the top. Annotation text loads lazily via `getCachedAnnotations` (cache-only reader in `zotero-annotations.js` — local disk only, never the API), now indexed per fileId as `{ text, comment, pageLabel }` objects (original case) so matches can be shown, not just counted; a late index resolve re-runs `renderBody`. **Flagging**: a hover flag toggle (upper-left thumbnail corner, the Recent-Files flag glyph) flips the shared `flagged` flag on the *tree node* — the desk node or the project alias, so flags are per-context — so a shelf flag also surfaces in the sidebar's Flagged section. `enforceFlaggedPdfOrder` (state-pdf-aliases.js, formerly `enforcePinnedPdfOrder`) stable-partitions every PDFs folder's children flagged-first and *migrates* the legacy `pinned` marker onto `flagged`, running at toggle time, from `toggleFlagged` (state-tree.js) for pdf nodes, and inside `normalizePdfProjectAliases`; the shelf's `sortCards` floats flagged cards above whatever sort is active. Cover hydration walks the cards sequentially: cached covers land instantly, missing ones are rendered from the local binary on the spot (the backfill path for PDFs saved before covers shipped), and each fileId is checked once per session against its cached annotations via `refreshPdfCoverIfStale` so an annotated cover repaints in place; a render token abandons a stale mid-walk pass.

**Bookmarks (`pdf/pdf-bookmarks.js`).** Named + colored deep links stored on the registry entry (`items[fileId].bookmarks: [{ id, name, color, page, addedAt }]` — CRUD in pdf-sync.js, `pdf-bookmarks-changed` emitted on every mutation; aliases share the set since they share the fileId). Surfaces: **page hover** — `attachPageHoverButtons` (called from the viewer's page build; it also absorbed the Zotero pop-out button so one mousemove drives both corner zones) reveals a bookmark button in the page's upper-right 100 px zone; **folded view** — `attachFoldBookmarkButton` parks the same button beside each fold's expand toggle; both open the create popover (name input, 8 ROW_COLORS-matched swatches, Enter saves). Buttons on **bookmarked pages stay visible without hover** (`has-bookmark` class, ribbon filled with the bookmark's color): every page/fold button registers in a module-level set repainted by a single `pdf-bookmarks-changed` listener (pruned by `isConnected`, so viewer reloads and suspend/resume cycles can't leak), and the same listener rebuilds an **open list popup in place** — a bookmark added from a page button in a pane lands in an already-open menu immediately. The **toolbar** gets a Bookmarks button prepended as the first control (hidden when the viewer has no `fileId` — `createPdfViewer` now takes `opts.fileId`, passed by all three mounts: bridge, pane, stack column); it opens the **list popup** (`openBookmarkListPopup`): row click navigates via `goToPage`, hover-revealed edit / delete rows manage the set, and **⌘-pointerdown starts a text-drag** with the `[name](hush-pdf://<fileId>/<bookmarkId>)` markdown so the link drops into any doc or notebook through the standard pipeline. The shelf paints a thumbnail badge for bookmarked PDFs opening the same popup. **Link resolution**: `link-decorator.js#openUrl` short-circuits the `hush-pdf://` scheme to `openPdfBookmarkUrl` for every CodeMirror surface; notebook text shapes route through the `window.__hushOpenPdfBookmark` hook (registered by `initPdfBookmarks` in main.js — the canvas module stays free of app imports, mirroring wikilinks). Links carry the page as a `?p=` fallback so a deleted bookmark (or a not-yet-loaded registry) still lands on the right page; a live bookmark's current page wins. Navigation jumps in place (smooth) when the PDF is already the main surface; otherwise the jump is registered with the bridge (`requestPdfJump(fileId, page)`) and performed *inside* `mountPdf`, replacing the saved-scroll restore for that mount — no event/timing race — using `goToPage(n, { instant: true })` (direct `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop`, since a smooth `scrollIntoView` started while the container is still unhiding gets cancelled), re-asserted once ~180 ms later after the first page renders land.

**Covers.** `pdf-covers.js` rasterizes page 1 via pdfjs (same lazy import + render pipeline as `zotero-snapshot.js`) at 600 px height and stores the encoded bytes through `save_pdf_cover` / `load_pdf_cover` (Rust `PdfManager`, `{fileId}.cover` beside the binary in `files/pdfs/`; `delete_pdf` removes it with the binary). The extension is codec-agnostic on purpose — WebKit's canvas emits PNG where Chromium emits WebP from the same `toDataURL("image/webp")` call, so the loader sniffs magic bytes when building the object URL. Covers are per-device cache, never synced; both import paths (`importPdf`, `pdf-sync.js` background download) fire `ensurePdfCover` with the just-downloaded bytes and emit `pdf-cover-ready` so an open shelf swaps the placeholder in place. **Annotation marks are baked into the cover**: `renderCoverFromPdfBytes(bytes, annots)` paints page-1 highlight rects (translucent fill) and ink paths (stroked) onto the canvas via `viewport.convertToViewportPoint` — the same math the thumbnail overlay uses (`parseAnnotationPosition` shared from `pdf-viewer-annotations.js`), the annotations pulled from the local cache by `loadCoverAnnotations`. A per-device localStorage signature (`hush_pdf_cover_annot_sigs`, keyed by fileId, the sorted `key:color` of the page-1 marks) records what was baked; `refreshPdfCoverIfStale(fileId)` re-renders (revoking the old object URL) when the cached annotations no longer match — cheap when current (one cache read, no pdf parse). The initial import cover predates the annotation prefetch, so `importPdf`, `pdf-sync.js`'s background download, and `pdf-bridge.js`'s annotation load all call `refreshPdfCoverIfStale` after the prefetch and emit `pdf-cover-ready` on change; the shelf also probes once per fileId per session while hydrating.

**Project aliases.** A PDF "added to a project" is an alias node: `type: "pdf"`, the *same* `fileId` as the desk original (so binary, registry metadata, annotations, and panes all resolve identically), plus `pdfAlias: true` and an `addedAt` stamp, filed in a project-local folder marked `pdfFolder: true` (named "PDFs", pdf icon, sunk to the supplementary block by `normalizeProjectChildren`). All three markers are declared on the Rust `TreeNode` (serde would silently drop them otherwise). The sidebar's `canDrop` lets a dragged PDF land on a real project or a `pdfFolder`, and `normalizePdfProjectAliases` (called from the SortableList `onChange` next to `enforceSpecialPositions`) converts the move after the fact: the original returns to its desk's `__pdfs__` special, an alias lands in the project's PDFs folder (deduped by fileId), an alias dropped elsewhere in its project is re-slotted, an alias dragged out of any project is removed, orphaned aliases (original gone) are pruned, and emptied `pdfFolder`s are dropped. Deletion is asymmetric by design: deleting an alias or a project's PDFs folder just removes references (no Trash trip — special-cased at the top of `deleteTreeNode`, menu label "Remove from Project"), while trashing / purging a desk PDF calls `removeAliasesForFileIds` so every project reference dies with it. `collectTypedFileIds` skips aliases so a purged project can't delete the desk's actual binary or registry entry. The multi-select **Create Project** action aliases selected PDFs via `addPdfAliasToProject`; project → folder / doc conversions drop the alias folder (references only make sense on a project); `.hushproject` export ignores aliases (binaries are per-device caches). Pickers that walk pdf nodes (wikilink index, stack picker, `collectFileLeaves`) skip `pdfAlias` nodes so an aliased PDF isn't listed twice.

### Desktops (`desktop/`)

**View (`desktop-view.js`).** `openDesktop(state, containerId)` — containerId is a project node id (the container walk still handles desk ids so a desk-wide Desktop can come back later, but every entry point is project-scoped now) — takes over the editor area with a full `NotesCanvas` of file thumbnails. Unlike the PDF shelf, opening a Desktop calls `state.clearActiveFile()` so the underlying surface is genuinely torn down — the Desktop *is* the open thing, not an overlay (no doc margins to drag, no outline, and `#pane-container` joins the `desktop-active` `visibility: hidden` list so floating / pinned panes don't leak through). Two things belonging to the replaced file leak past `clearActiveFile` and are shut off explicitly: the **Outline View** (`#right-panel-overlay` and its hover-revealed `.right-panel-trigger` both hang off `#app` at a z-index above the Desktop, so they join the `desktop-active` hidden list), and **docked-pane edge reservations** — `publishDockCssVars` skips only `display:none` panes, so a doc's docked gutter kept reserving `--pane-dock-right-width` and shoved the Desktop's own shelf a gutter-width inboard; it now zeroes every dock while `desktop-active` is set, and openDesktop / closeDesktop republish after flipping the class. There's no Close button — and no header either: `renderChrome` mounts nothing but the canvas host, and the two view actions it used to carry (**Reset Desktop View**, **Refresh Desktop Thumbnails**) are passed into `buildDesktopOptionsSection` as an `actions` object so they render inside the bg-settings flyout. The canvas runs edge to edge like a notebook's, which means `body.desktop-active` also has to `display: none` the 30 px `#drag-region` strip (notebook / PDF / stack modes drop it the same way) — it's z-index 80, above `.desktop-view`, so leaving it up would swallow pointer events along the whole top edge. You leave by opening any file (a thumbnail or a sidebar row fires `file-opened` etc., which closes the Desktop) or Esc. One host in `#app`, a `desktop-active` body class hiding the editor surfaces, the same `panel-inset` CSS sibling rules for the sidebar. The two takeover views are mutually exclusive (`openDesktop` closes the shelf; the `view-shelf` dispatch closes the Desktop). The canvas mounts exactly like a stack column's notebook (fresh `NotesCanvas` + `computeNotebookSettings(state, null)`, with `DESKTOP_BG_DEFAULT` — a 25 px grid at 20% opacity — applied unless the per-desktop background override says otherwise), so every notebook feature — tools, layers, undo, align, the drawing engine — is live, except *authoring* a flowchart: `DrawingState.flowchartEnabled` (a per-canvas switch folded into the layer's `isFlowable` callback and the ⌘→ `startEditingFlowchartChild` entry) is flipped off for Desktops, so drop-to-connect targets, edge creation, and ⌘-merge stay inert, and saved flowEdges aren't restored. The layer itself stays live — it's what paints the derived document-order connections (see below). Closing paths: Esc (document-level capture that defers to the canvas's own Esc semantics — text edit and reorder mode pass through, a live selection clears first), any single-file open event (`file-opened` / `notebook-open` / `pdf-open` / `stack-open`), a 2+ sidebar multi-select, or the container vanishing from the tree. The last two are the ones that can close a Desktop into *nothing* — `openDesktop` already ran `clearActiveFile`, so the empty-state pane is sitting right underneath — and both are guarded accordingly. The open events only close when `somethingIsOpen(state)` (any of the `current*` pointers, or `currentLocalSync`) is actually set, so an open that bailed part-way (missing file, failed `load_file`) leaves the Desktop up instead of dropping the user on "No file selected". And the container check moved *out* of the `files-changed` handler and into the debounced `reconcileLive`, where it only closes if `state.fileTree` is non-empty — a mid-reload `files-changed` (multi-window broadcast, background PDF sync) used to be able to close the Desktop on a transient tree miss. A `settings-changed` listener re-derives the notebook settings and re-applies them when the theme signature actually changed, so a style switch repaints the open Desktop's chrome (thumbnails stay until the next open / Refresh — their cache signature includes the theme). Opened from the Desktop button (same `tree-action-shelf` styling + grid glyph as the shelf button, `view-desktop` action) on project rows, and from the palette (`Open Project Desktop`, plus `Refresh Desktop Thumbnails` gated on the `desktop-active` body class so the palette never imports the notebook graph). The canvas toolbar starts parked at the bottom edge and minimized (`setDrawingToolbarPosition("bottom")` + `setDrawingToolbarMinimized(true)` at mount).

**File thumbnails are ImageShapes with a `fileRef`.** `{ key, kind, fileId, nodeId, name }` on the shape (declared on `ImageShape` in types.ts) — `key` is the reconcile identity (fileId; node id for projects). Drag / group / scale / lasso all come free from the engine; what `fileRef` withholds is destruction, resizing, and image-processing: `DrawingState.deleteSelected` drops fileRef ids from the deleting set (mixed selections still delete the rest); `hitTestResizeHandles` skips fileRef shapes and `drawSelectionHighlight` paints their dashed selection border without handle squares, so a thumbnail keeps its natural size; the selection toolbar hides Delete when the whole selection is fileRefs plus the inline image-rename input, Crop, Rasterize, and Recognize-handwriting for any fileRef selection; and `renderer.ts::drawImageShape` grows an optional `theme` param to paint the fileRef chrome (a hairline border — filenames are no longer canvas-painted at all: labels are hover-only DOM in desktop-hover.js, a centred caption under the hovered thumbnail gated by the Thumbnail-labels option). **Tints**: a node's sidebar highlight (`node.bgColor`, a ROW_COLORS key) rides the entry → `refOf` → `fileRef.tint`, and `drawFileRefChrome` paints it as a translucent wash plus a 2 px coloured frame from a `FILEREF_TINTS` literal mirrored in renderer.ts (kept literal so the renderer imports nothing from the sidebar). Tinting is chrome, not baked, so it needs no cache invalidation — the reconcile just re-caption s a shape whose tint changed. **Raise order**: `raisedLast(shapes, selectedIds)` in desktop-content.js sorts thumbnails that temporarily float above their neighbours — selected ones, and docs showing an outline column — to the end; renderer.ts runs the equivalent as two stable paint passes per layer (paint order only, so the shape array keeps its real z and undo stays clean), while desktop-hover and the outline hit test both route their `findShapeAtPoint` through the helper so hover captions, buttons, and clicks all resolve to the same shape the user sees on top. `arrangeShapesAsGrid` reserves a 28 px label allowance under fileRef shapes so grid rows leave room for the hover captions, and Rasterize is withheld Desktop-wide via a `DrawingState.desktopMode` marker. One window-level guard in `input-handler.ts`: while `desktop-active` is set, only the Desktop's own canvas (or a pane's) may act on window keydowns — the hidden main-notebook canvas underneath would otherwise mirror deletes and undos invisibly. Hover controls (`desktop-hover.js`) are a DOM overlay anchored at the hovered shape's top-right via `canvasToScreen`, select-tool only. They surface on hover **or selection**: `hide()` falls back to the lone selected thumbnail instead of clearing, and the change listener watches `selectedIds` too, so the controls follow a selection made from anywhere (canvas click, shelf row, "View in Desktop") and stay put with the pointer elsewhere. Opening has no button — a `dblclick` on the canvas resolves the thumbnail under the pointer and routes by kind through `state.openFile / openNotebook / openPdf / openStack / openProject`; touch gets a separate tap-pair detector (iOS reserves double-tap for zoom and doesn't reliably deliver `dblclick` on canvas) with a 600 ms guard so a synthesized `dblclick` can't open twice. The tap-pair detector is **multi-touch aware**, which it has to be: a two-finger pan or pinch ends with two `pointerup`s milliseconds apart, and a pinch-to-zoom-out finishes with the fingers close together — well inside the detector's 400 ms / 24 px window — so panning or zooming an iPad Desktop would fire a phantom "open" and yank the Desktop away mid-gesture. A `liveTouches` set tracks concurrent contacts; the moment a second one lands the gesture is flagged `multiTouchGesture` and every release in it is ignored, and the flag only clears when a *fresh* contact lands on an empty screen (not on release), so a stray `pointerup` arriving after iOS cancelled the pinch still counts as multi-touch. `pointercancel` is wired to the same bookkeeping — iOS cancels the pointer stream when `input-handler.ts` `preventDefault()`s the two-finger `touchstart`, and without it the cancelled contacts would sit in the set forever and lock the detector out. `desktop-outline.js`'s click handler drops its pending press on `pointercancel` for the same reason (otherwise a stale pointerdown pairs with a later pointerup and reads as a heading click mid-pan). The remaining icon opens the file as a floating pane via `createPane` — for a nested project, its Desktop as a `desktop` pane. Docs and nested projects grow an **Outline** icon wired to the outline controller's `toggle` (its `.active` paint reads `opts.hasOutlineRows(key)`; clicking hides the overlay so the next hover re-anchors against the resized thumbnail).

**Thumbnails (`desktop-thumbs.js`, drawing primitives in `desktop-thumb-draw.js`).** All render at 2× through the existing `renderForExport` pipeline, and **always in light mode** regardless of the app's appearance: `makeThemeCtx` in desktop-view.js builds the thumbnail context from `DrawingState.themeForVariant("light")` with `appearance: "light"` and no canvas-background override, so page grounds are white and ink dark even while the Desktop canvas itself follows the app theme. A `THUMB_STYLE_VERSION` constant folds into every cache signature so geometry/styling changes (and the light-mode switch) regenerate cached renders on the next open. Docs: the markdown as a single auto-colour TextShape (8 px type) on a printed page — 320 × 420, 40 px margins, solid white ground (thumbnails are always light), `docPageTheme` guarding ink contrast, frontmatter and `%%comments%%` stripped, render text capped at 4 KB — sitting on a paper pile that encodes length: one faintly-bordered page-ground sheet per 500 words (capped at 20), offset 6 px down-right each with a hair of shadow (2 px would render sub-pixel at the Desktop's fit-all zoom); the pile makes the bounding box non-rectangular, so doc records are `frameless` (fileRef.frameless) and `drawFileRefChrome` skips its hairline border in favour of the baked ones. Notebooks: decode the envelope, fit content bounds + 10 px margins to a 400 px long edge (vector re-render, so upscaling small content stays crisp) inside a 20 px page-ground matte (always white, content clipped to the inner box), image shapes decoded into a per-render cache (dark variant when the appearance is dark), strokes approximated as polylines via `drawApproximateStrokes` (now exported from notebook-snapshot-preview.js). PDFs: the shelf cover (`loadPdfCoverUrl` / `ensurePdfCover`) displayed at 400 px tall — never duplicated into the Desktop cache since covers are already a per-device disk cache; a pending download shows a card and `pdf-cover-ready` swaps it in place. Projects: a composite of the project's own Desktop, sized by the *notebook* rules (fit to `optLongEdge` inside the same scaled `NB_MATTE` page-ground matte) since both are a whole canvas shrunk down rather than a printed page, recursion capped at depth 2. It renders through the same `renderForExport` + `drawApproximateStrokes` pair a notebook thumbnail uses, plus its own `FlowchartLayer` seeded from `docOrderEdges` for the document-order arrows (drawn directly rather than through `renderForExport`'s flowchart hook, which resets the arrow colour and so can't carry the Desktop's translucent heavy stroke) and a baked pass for the notes pinned to that Desktop. The shape list is rebuilt from the child Desktop's envelope: one synthetic ImageShape per file at its saved rect (its thumbnail decoded into the render's `imageCache` under a matching id, its `fileRef` carried so `drawFileRefChrome` paints the tint / hairline), plus every *non*-file shape the envelope holds — text shapes, drag areas, strokes — untouched. Bounds come from `computeNotebookBounds` over the lot, extended by the pinned notes' world rects, so a stroke or a note outside the file grid still lands in frame rather than hanging off the edge. Child captions are painted here rather than left to hover DOM, since a composite has no hover. Stacks (`.hushstack` files): a row of 80 px slices, one per constituent in stack order, each clipped to the left edge of that file's thumbnail scaled to the long-edge height (the record also returns a `slices` band list — `{ x, w, name }` in thumbnail CSS px — which rides on the fileRef so desktop-hover can name the slice under the pointer on the caption's second line; names travel with their decoded image so a slice that failed to load can't shift the captions out of step) — constituents render through the same `ensureDesktopThumb` pipeline (so they share the per-file cache); falls back to a glyph card for an empty stack, and the row recomposes when the stack file itself changes (constituent content edits catch up on Refresh). Doc / notebook / project thumbs cache in IndexedDB keyed by `file.modified` + a theme signature (`themeSigOf`); `ensureDesktopThumb` is the cache-aware entry (`force` regenerates), and the open-time hydration pass *is* the "thumbnails update when the Desktop is opened" behaviour. `computeDesktopGrid` is the shared initial layout: by-filetype sections (doc, notebook, pdf, project, stack), 4 columns, 150 px gaps — used for first opens, for new files gridding in below existing content, and for never-opened project composites so the preview matches what the Desktop will actually open as.

**Outlines (`desktop-outline.js`).** A doc thumbnail — and a nested project's composite — can carry a clickable **outline column** to the **left** of the page. Two halves live in this module. *Render helpers* — `layoutDocOutline(headings, scale, colX)` lays the column out in the thumbnail's own scaled CSS space (= the shape's local space, so a stored row maps straight through `world − shape.position`), returning per-heading hit-boxes (`{ text, level, startOffset, x, w, y, h, textX, indent }`) plus a `contentH` and the `dividerX` that separates column from page; `drawDocOutline` paints a divider and the indented headings (H1 600-weight / brighter, deeper levels dimmer, an italic "No headings" placeholder when empty). desktop-thumbs calls both from `renderDocThumb` when `entry.outline` is set: headings come from `parseHeadings` on the *original* content (so `startOffset` indexes the real document), the image widens to `blockW + colW` and grows to `max(blockH, contentH)` so a long outline makes the thumbnail taller, and the record returns `outlineRows`. Because the column takes the left band, every page-and-pile coordinate shifts right by `colW` (`ox` in `renderDocThumb`), and `regenDoc` slides the shape's `position.x` left by exactly the width it gained — so opening an outline grows the thumbnail leftward and the page itself doesn't jump sideways under the pointer. `fileRef.outlineX` (which used to pull the hover buttons back over the page when the column hung off its right edge) is gone: the shape's right edge *is* the page's right edge now, so the buttons anchor at `bounds.maxX` unconditionally and `applyThumbRefFields` just clears any stale value. The staleness signature gains an `o0`/`o1` flag so toggling the outline re-renders. The column paints its own opaque page-ground panel before the divider and headings (it overhangs the page and floats over neighbours, so it can't be transparent). A **project's** rows are its child *files* rather than headings: `projectOutlineRows` (desktop-thumbs.js) emits one row per `collectDesktopFiles` entry, level 1 for the flow kinds (docs + sub-projects) and level 2 — dimmer — for notebooks / PDFs / stacks, each carrying a fileRef-shaped `target`. `layoutDocOutline` passes `target` through onto the row, which is what the click handler routes on: a row with a target opens that whole file, a row without one scrolls the doc the column belongs to. So a project's outline reads like one document's table of contents and jumps by file. *Controller* — `createDesktopOutline(deps)` owns the per-thumbnail row geometry (keyed by fileRef key), a canvas pointer handler that tells a click from a drag by travel and maps a hit through `hitAt` (raised-last order → world→local → row box) to either `deps.openRef(target)` or `openDocAtOffset` (open the file, poll ≤900 ms for the editor, dispatch a selection + `EditorView.scrollIntoView`), a pointermove that publishes the hovered row to `DrawingState.desktopOutlineHover` (a repaint-only notify key, like `flowHoveredEdgeId`) so `renderer.ts` underlines it, and `toggle` / `setAll` which flip a thumbnail's `fileRef.outline` (gated to `OUTLINE_KINDS` = doc + project), re-`ensureDesktopThumb`, and re-apply. `hitAt` keys off `fileRef.key` rather than `fileRef.fileId`, since a project thumbnail has no file id of its own. desktop-view instantiates it once with lazy getters over its module state; `keepRefFields` (desktop-content.js) preserves `fileRef.outline` across reconcile/hydrate, and mountCanvas seeds `entry.outline` from the saved shapes so a Desktop reopens with the same outlines up. Because a thumbnail swap reuses the shape id, `applyThumbToShape` refreshes the canvas image cache (`getImageCache().get(id).src = dataUrl`) — NotesCanvas's `_syncImageCache` only auto-reloads on an appearance-variant change, so without this a content swap (outline toggle, PDF cover arriving, Refresh) would keep painting the stale image.

**Thumbnail sticky badges.** A file's own file-level stickies render as mini notes along the bottom of its Desktop thumbnail — and a nested project's composite carries each child file's notes on its own miniature: `renderProjectThumb` returns a `children` list (`{ kind, fileId, x, y, w, h }` in the finished thumbnail's own CSS px) plus the `childScale` it shrank them by, `applyThumbRefFields` parks both on the fileRef as `projectChildren` / `projectScale`, and the renderer draws each child's badges against its sub-rect with `scale` set to the composite's zoom.

The notes pinned to a *project Desktop's canvas* go the other way — those are **baked** into the composite, because they're content of the Desktop being pictured rather than an annotation over a file, and floating them live meant they sat outside the frame the bounds had been sized for. `renderProjectThumb` reads them through a `window.__hushDesktopStickiesFor(projectId)` bridge (`sticky-desktop.js` — the existing `desktopStickyRows` only answers for the *open* Desktop, and a composite needs any project, open or not), folds their world rects into the bounds, and paints them with the shapes. Baking means staleness, so `entrySig` gains a compact `pinnedNotesSig` (position + size + text length per note) — an edited note lands on the next Desktop open, the same cadence file edits get through `modified`. Both the live badges and the baked notes share `drawStickyBox`, exported from renderer.ts, which drops the body text below a 4 px font so a heavily-shrunk note keeps the pink square as a "there's a note here" marker rather than painting noise. Deliberately *not* baked into the thumbnail image — a note's text changes constantly, and folding it into the raster would mean a regenerate (and a cache-signature term) per keystroke. Instead `renderer.ts::drawThumbStickies` paints them live each frame, right after `drawImageShape` inside the same raised-last paint pass, so they stay glued to their thumbnail and inherit its z. Geometry is world-space (130 px notes, first offset −12 px so it overhangs the left edge, row bottom 10 px above the thumbnail's), which means they scale with the camera exactly like the thumbnails; a row wider than the thumbnail compresses its step into an overlapping fan rather than overflowing. Data arrives through `RenderState.fileStickies`, a per-frame accessor notes-canvas wires to `window.__hushFileStickies` (same shape as the `flagColors` / `touchMode` hush bridges); sticky-notes.js implements it by mapping the fileRef kind to the `doc:` / `nb:` / `pdf:` / `st:` context prefix `currentFileContext` builds and filtering the note map. Because the badges aren't shape state, nothing in the normal notify path invalidates them, so note add / edit / close call `repaintDesktop()` (sticky-desktop.js), which fires the repaint-only `"interaction"` key — repaint without marking the Desktop dirty for a save.

**Shelf integration.** The shape shelf doubles as the Desktop's file list: `ShelfNode` grows a `fileKind`, image rows with a fileRef render the matching filetype glyph (`file-doc` / `file-notebook` / `file-pdf` / `file-stack` / `file-project` in `ui/icons.ts`, 16-unit viewBoxes mirroring the sidebar's `typeIcons`), and the row's `excerpt` — which the shelf's search + snippet drill-down already read — is filled with the file's deep content through the `window.__hushDesktopSearchText` hook. desktop-view builds that index after hydration (`buildSearchIndex`): doc bodies, notebook text via `extractSnapshotText`, PDF title/authors/bookmark names plus cached Zotero annotation text + comments, project child names; entries land as they resolve, the map refreshes with reconcile and clears on teardown.

**Per-Desktop options (`desktop-options.js`).** The bg-settings flyout grows a Desktop section through `DrawingState.extraBgSettingsSection` (a host-injected builder the popup appends on every render): Doc text size (3–12), Thumbnail long edge (200–800, 100 px steps), Thumbnail labels, Include Gutters, Show Document Connections. It also hosts the Desktop's two **view actions** — Reset Desktop View and Refresh Desktop Thumbnails — passed in as an `actions` object now that the header is gone; the refresh button owns its own busy label (the old one lived in the header markup, which no longer exists). Values live in `envelope.options`; the size options fold into the thumbnail cache signature (`themeCtx.docFontSize` / `themeCtx.longEdge` — everything scales relative to the 400 px baseline), so a slider change just re-ensures each thumbnail and re-applies natural dims (debounced 350 ms). Labels flip `DrawingState.hideFileLabels` (threaded through RenderState into `drawImageShape`); Include Gutters re-runs the reconcile — `collectDesktopFiles` skips `node.gutter` notebooks unless asked, while a pre-scan flags their paired docs `hasGutter` either way (that flag drives the hover row's third icon, **Open with Gutter Visible**: `state.openFile` then `gutter-commands.openGutter`, since the pairing survives closes). Show Document Connections re-applies the derived edge set (see below). The section also carries **Outlines: Open all / Close all** buttons — `setAllOutlines(bool)` routes to the outline controller's `setAll`, which regenerates every doc + nested-project thumbnail with the column on/off (bailing if the open token changes mid-loop).

**Stacks (`desktop-stacks.js`).** Dropping fileRef thumbnails onto one another piles them: members share a `fileRef.stackId` and a `groupId === stackId`, so the engine's group mechanics drag the pile as one; positions cascade at 50 px offsets (right + down, later = higher z). Every fileRef image gets `drawImageShape`'s subtle drop shadow (the shadow flag is just `!!shape.fileRef` now — an earlier `stackShadowIds` pass gave it only to non-bottom pile members), so thumbnails read as cards on a surface and a pile still stacks visibly because each member casts its own. Desktop-hover lists the pile's titles left-aligned beneath its union bounds while hovering — clicking a title runs `moveToTopOfStack` (re-cascade with the clicked member last / on top, one undo entry; the write is exempted from the overlay's hide-on-change via an own-writes counter). The shape shelf groups a pile like a drag-area: a collapsible "N stacked" parent row (layers glyph) with the members indented at depth 1. The gesture layer is three listeners on the canvas element registered after the engine's own (so pointer-up sees committed drag positions): a plain drop merges piles (`mergeIntoStack`), ⌘-drag releases the grabbed member (`releaseFromStack` — `handlePointerDown` in state.ts skips the group promotion for `cmd + fileRef.stackId` so the drag moves the lone shape). `normalizeDesktopStacks` heals half-states on every rebuild: lone members dissolve, an Ungrouped member drops its stackId. Sibling per-canvas switches gate notebook behaviours that don't fit a Desktop: `altDuplicateEnabled` (option-drag clones), `flowchartEnabled` (edge *authoring*), and `flowEdgesLocked` / `flowArrowAlpha` / `flowArrowWidth` / `flowArrowHeadSize` / `flowDragDescendants` for the derived document-order chain.

**Document connections (`desktop-connections.js`).** The Desktop chains its doc thumbnails together in project order using the notebook's own `FlowchartLayer`, so the routing, bezier geometry, and arrowheads are the ones a hand-drawn chart uses. What differs is ownership: the edges are **derived** from `collectDesktopFiles`' walk order (the same order the files sidebar renders), so a sidebar drag → `saveFileTree` → `files-changed` → `reconcileLive` re-chains them with no extra plumbing. `FLOW_KINDS` is `doc` + `project` — a nested project is a chapter in the reading order, not an attachment, so it chains alongside the docs while notebooks / PDFs / stacks stay off it. `docOrderEdges(entries, shapes)` maps each flow entry's `fileRef.key` to its live shape id and emits one edge per adjacent pair with a deterministic `docorder:<fromKey>><toKey>` id; `applyDocConnections` diffs against the current set and, when it differs, `deserialize`s the new one and repaints through the `"interaction"` notify key — repaint-only, so derived edges never mark the Desktop dirty for a save. `reconcileLive` calls it *before* its `if (!changed) return` early-out, since a reorder changes the entry order without touching a single shape.

Four per-canvas switches make them read (and behave) as annotation rather than content. `DrawingState.flowArrowAlpha` (0.4 on Desktops) is applied in `renderer.ts` as a `ctx.globalAlpha` around `flowchart.draw` — the layer's own `save`/`restore` preserves it. `flowArrowWidth` / `flowArrowHeadSize` / `flowArrowLineCap` (30 / 60 / `butt`, against the layer's 1.5 / 11 / `round`) go through a new `FlowchartLayer.setArrowMetrics`, called per frame next to `setArrowColor`. The head deliberately isn't scaled by the same 20× the stroke is, because `arrowHeadSize` doubles as how far the line backs off its target and a proportional 220 px head would consume the whole 150 px `DESKTOP_GRID_GAP`; the butt cap matters because a round one overhangs the arrowhead's base by half the stroke width, and since the head is `fill`ed after the line is `stroke`d that overlap paints twice — at 30 % opacity the doubled sliver reads as a dark notch across the tip. `flowEdgesLocked` marks the set as derived: `renderer.ts` skips the per-edge delete dot / X entirely, `handlePointerDown` skips the edge-midpoint hit-test, `handlePointerMove` skips `findEdgeNear`, and `_checkpoint` / `_applyCheckpoint` leave `flowEdges` out of the undo history (undo would otherwise resurrect a stale chain the host is about to re-derive). `flowDragDescendants` (true everywhere else, false here) gates the two places a drag hauls a node's flowchart subtree along — the per-frame move set in `handlePointerMove`, and `_collectMovingShapeIds`: a hand-drawn chart's subtree is a spatial unit the user arranged, but a Desktop's chain is derived from file order, so each thumbnail drags alone and the arrows just re-route. Piles are unaffected — they move together through `groupId`, not through the chart. The Desktop envelope persists `flowEdges: []` for the same reason — the chain is always rebuilt from the live file order on open.

**Opening a nested project.** A project *is* a Desktop, so double-clicking its thumbnail drills the takeover into it (`openFileRef`'s project branch calls back into `openDesktop`) rather than opening the project's joined document buffer. Its hover pane button therefore opens the **Desktop in a pane** — `desktop-pane.js`, registered as a `desktop` pane type alongside the Zotero highlight pane's custom-type hook in `pane-content.js#loadPaneContent`, with `pane.fileId` holding the project's tree-node id. The pane mounts a fresh `NotesCanvas` straight into `pane._content` (the way a notebook pane does) and reuses the takeover's pieces wholesale: `collectDesktopFiles` → `ensureDesktopThumb` → `buildShapes`, `initDesktopConnections` + `applyDocConnections`, the same stored envelope. It's deliberately the *reading* half — no options flyout, no outline columns, no stacking — with pan / zoom and the same double-click / multi-touch-guarded double-tap open gesture. **Hydration is two-pass and never blocks the mount**: generating a thumbnail is an IPC read plus a 2× canvas render and a WebP encode, so the original serial `ensureDesktopThumb` loop could run into the seconds on a fat project — and because `createPane` *centres* a pane on the point it's handed, a pane sitting empty through that parks itself exactly on top of the hover button that opened it, so follow-up clicks land on the pane and the whole thing reads as "the button did nothing." Pass one is `peekThumb` (`entrySig` + one `loadThumbRecord`, no render) across every entry in parallel, and the canvas goes up immediately — misses paint `drawImageShape`'s placeholder card at their saved size. Pass two generates the misses one at a time behind it, `applyPaneThumb`-ing each into place (refreshing the decoded image in the canvas's cache, same as the takeover's `applyThumbToShape`) with a small `.desktop-pane-loading` corner chip while it runs. `openFileRefSecondary` also offsets its anchor down-and-right of the click so the pane never covers the control that opened it. Layout edits write back (merged over the stored envelope so the takeover's **camera** survives — a pane's viewport is not the Desktop's), and the canvas is parked on `pane.notebook` so the pane system's generic destroy and theme sync pick it up for free; every notebook-*specific* save / mirror path is gated on `fileType === "notebook"`, which this isn't. The one thing that needs a hand is the background: the theme-sync pass re-applies notebook settings to every `pane.notebook`, so `pane-theme-sync.js` calls back into `applyDesktopPaneBackground` afterwards to restore the project's own grid. `savePaneContent` skips the type, and `pane-toolbar.js` routes its title click to `openDesktop` and withholds the word-count chip and font-size button.

**Opening a thumbnail as a pane (`desktop-open.js`).** Panes belong to the file the Desktop replaced, so they have to be suppressed while it's up — which is why the hover row's pane button appeared to do nothing at first: `createPane` ran, the pane mounted, and it was invisible. A pane opened from a thumbnail now passes `{ desktopPane: true, ownerContext: "dt:<containerId>" }`, stamping a `.desktop-pane` class on the pane root that exempts it; `#pane-container`'s `--z-pane` (90) already outranks `.desktop-view` (50), so it floats over the canvas exactly as it floats over an editor.

The *shape* of that suppression turned out to matter. The obvious spelling — `visibility: hidden` on `#pane-container`, `visibility: visible` back on `.desktop-pane` — is correct CSS (visibility is inherited, so a descendant can override a hidden ancestor) and worked everywhere except WebKit's paint invalidation. A pane appended into the hidden container computed as `visibility: visible`, `opacity: 1`, correctly sized and composited at the right z-index within ~110 ms of the click — and simply never got painted. No dropped frame, no blocked main thread, no JS latency (an rAF gap probe stayed clean throughout and the mount logged complete in 110 ms); the element just wasn't drawn until an unrelated full-page invalidation — a canvas zoom, another pane opening — forced the region to repaint, which read as a ~20 s dead button. The rule now hides the panes **individually** (`#pane-container > .floating-pane:not(.desktop-pane)`), leaving the container visible, so nothing is ever visible-inside-hidden. `onContextChange` already `display: none`s panes outside the active context — and while a Desktop is up that context is its own `dt:<id>` — so what this rule actually covers is **pinned** panes, which stay displayed across every context. The `dt:` context is real, not cosmetic: `getCurrentContext()` returns it whenever `window.__hushDesktopOpenId` is set (a Desktop *replaces* the open file, so it outranks every `current*` pointer), and pane-manager subscribes `onContextChange` to `desktop-opened` / `desktop-closed`, so a Desktop pane hides when you open a file and comes back when you return. `desktopPane` round-trips through `panes.json` and the class is re-stamped on restore.

**Desktop-pinned stickies.** The palette gates (`canAddFileSticky` / `canAddProjectSticky`) and `noteVisible`'s project case all resolve context off the *open file*, which a Desktop deliberately has none of — `openDesktop` calls `clearActiveFile`. So each consults `desktopOpenId()` as well: without it "Add File Sticky" hid itself on exactly the surface that pins it, making the pinning path unreachable from the UI, and a project's own stickies stayed hidden on its Desktop. `addSticky(state, "file")` while a Desktop is open (window.__hushDesktopOpenId) automatically becomes kind `"desktop"`: target = the project id, position stored as canvas world coordinates (`wx`/`wy`) mapped through the live camera via `__hushDesktopWorldToScreen` / `__hushDesktopScreenToWorld`, re-anchored on every `desktop-camera-changed` document event (dispatched from the view's camera listener) — so the note rides pans and zooms like part of the background. Pinned in world units also means **pinned at world scale**: `__hushDesktopWorldToScreen` returns the camera `zoom` alongside the point, and `repositionDesktopNotes` applies it as a `transform: scale(z)` with `transform-origin: top left`, so a note keeps its size relative to the thumbnails it annotates. Top-left origin is what makes that cheap — it's the same point `left`/`top` set and the same point the drag handler converts back through the camera, so drag needs no zoom correction. **Resize does**: its handles apply raw pointer deltas to the note's unscaled width/height, so those deltas divide by `desktopZoom()` (and a `w`/`n` handle's origin shift multiplies back by it), and pointer-up re-derives `wx`/`wy` since those handles move the anchor. A pinned note also sits **beneath the sidebars**, which is a stacking-context trick rather than a z-index bump: `.sticky-desktop-layer` mounts *inside* `.desktop-view` instead of the body-level `#sticky-container`, so it inherits that takeover's context (z 50) — the files panel (z 100) outranks the whole Desktop, and the shape shelf (z 150) outranks the layer within it, while every other sticky keeps `#sticky-container`'s `--z-sticky` (250) above all chrome. The layer is `position: fixed` so notes stay in the viewport coordinates the camera bridge hands back (`.desktop-view` is untransformed, so fixed still resolves against the viewport and escapes its `overflow: hidden`), and sits at z 25 — above the thumbnail hover chrome, below the loading veil. `repositionDesktopNotes` adopts notes into the layer as it anchors them, since `closeDesktop` wipes the view's markup and detaches them back out. Pinned notes are also **selectable like canvas objects**: a delegated capture-phase `pointerdown` on the layer selects the note under the pointer (and clears the shape selection, so one model owns the selection at a time), a press on bare canvas clears it, and a marquee picks up every note it touches. The marquee is tracked as a pointer gesture on `canvasEl` rather than inferred from `selectionBox` notifies — those batch into a single change event alongside `selectedIds`, so by the time it fires the box is already null — and hit-tests each note's live `getBoundingClientRect`, which already folds in the camera pan and the note's zoom scaling, so a box can pick up notes and thumbnails together. Selection is visual only (`.sticky-note.selected`); deleting stays with the note's own `×`, since that is permanent and the canvas delete key is not a safe route to it. The behaviour lives in `sticky-desktop.js`, which takes the `notes` map as a parameter rather than closing over it — sticky-notes.js stays the single owner of note state. They also list in the shape shelf (a Desktop's right sidebar): sticky-notes.js publishes `__hushDesktopStickies` / `__hushRevealDesktopSticky`, shelf-label.ts wraps both like the existing `__hushDesktopSearchText` bridge, and `buildNodes` appends a `type: "sticky"` row per note — first line as the label, body in `excerpt` so shelf search reaches note text. Those rows aren't shapes, so `ShelfNode.onActivate` overrides the row's usual `focusShape` and reveal instead raises the note and centres the Desktop camera on its world point (via `getActiveNotebookState`, which is the Desktop canvas while one is open). Visible only while that Desktop is open (`desktop-opened` / `desktop-closed` state events refresh visibility); drags write back through the camera; prunes with the project like a project sticky. Header-wise it borrows the grid glyph and the pink body (`.sticky-desktop`).

**Other view chrome.** The header carries **Reset View** (fit-everything camera via `fitCameraFor`, also the first-open default), the palette adds **View in Desktop** (visible while a file inside a project is open; `openDesktop(..., { focusKey })` selects + centres that file's thumbnail via `focusShape`), and the `⌘⇧\` outline shortcut routes to the Desktop's shape shelf ahead of the stack / pdf / notebook branches in main.js.

**Return crumb (`desktop-return.js`).** A Desktop *replaces* what was open, so opening a file from one strands the user with no way back to the arrangement they were reading. The crumb is a fixed bar in the upper left — "Back to <Project> Desktop" plus a dismiss `×` — and it's deliberately a navigation marker, not a history stack: one hop back, never a trail. The mechanism is **arm-then-consume**. Every Desktop-initiated open calls `armDesktopReturn(state, containerId)` immediately before it opens anything (`openThumbnail` for the double-click and outline-row paths, the hover row's gutter action, and the Desktop pane's own open gesture, which arms the open takeover's container if there is one and its own project otherwise); the next surface-change event — `file-opened` / `notebook-open` / `pdf-open` / `stack-open` / `desktop-opened` — adopts the armed target and shows the bar. A surface change with **nothing** armed clears it, which is what stops the crumb following the user around after they navigate by some other route (sidebar, palette, wikilink). Clicking Back deliberately doesn't arm, so returning doesn't leave a crumb pointing at the Desktop you just left; a `files-changed` that deletes the target project drops it rather than offering a dead link. Back also **regenerates the thumbnail of what you're returning from** — `onSurfaceChange` records the surface it landed on (`landedKey`, keyed the way a Desktop keys its thumbnails: fileId for files, node id for a nested Desktop) and the click force-refreshes that one entry via `refreshDesktopEntry`. That only works if the buffer reached disk, which it previously didn't: `openDesktop` calls `clearActiveFile`, which drops the dirty flag *without writing*, so unsaved keystrokes never made it to the file the thumbnail renders from. `openDesktop` now flushes a dirty buffer before tearing the file down. Listeners install lazily on the first arm, so an install that never touches a Desktop never wires them.

**Broken-image paint aborts.** `drawImageShape` used to gate on `img.complete` alone, which is also true for a decode that *failed* (`naturalWidth === 0`) — and `drawImage` on a broken image throws `InvalidStateError`, aborting the rest of the paint pass and taking every later shape and sticky badge with it. That went unnoticed until Desktop *panes* started hydrating progressively: a pass-one miss leaves `dataUrl: ""`, `_syncImageCache` set `img.src = ""` (which resolves against the document URL and loads the page as an image), and the resulting broken element killed the frame from the first thumbnail onward — arrows drew, because they paint *before* the shape pass, and nothing else did. The gate is now `complete && naturalWidth > 0`, and `_syncImageCache` skips creating an element at all until there are bytes to decode.

**Persistence (`desktop-store.js`).** Per-device IndexedDB (`hush-desktop`), two stores: `envelopes` (containerId → `{ shapes, layers, flowEdges, camera, background }` — fileRef dataUrls stripped on save, rehydrated from the thumb cache on open) and `thumbs` (key → `{ dataUrl, sig, w, h }`). IndexedDB rather than a Rust store because it's pure per-device UI cache (like PDF covers, none of it rides sync) and dataURLs would blow localStorage. Saves debounce 800 ms off `notebook-change` / `notebook-camera-change`; close and container-switch serialize synchronously and fire the write. Reconcile on `files-changed` (debounced, and held until the open-time hydration lands): dead fileRef shapes drop, renames re-caption, new files grid in beneath `maxY + gap`; hydration keeps a shape's width and re-derives height from the fresh thumbnail's aspect so a grown notebook doesn't render squashed; duplicate fileRefs (e.g. a pasted copy) dedupe on the next open.

### Sync (`sync/`)

**Dropbox sync has been removed** (see LOCAL-DESKS-PLANNING.md — folder-based desks over the user's own file provider replace it; Local Folder mounts already cover direct folder access, including an iCloud Drive folder on iPad). What remains in `sync/` is the provider-agnostic core that Local Sync uses today and the desk-folder engine will reuse:

- **`apply-external.js`** — `applyExternalDocContent(state, { content, lockKey, skipWhenDirty })`, the single implementation of "apply externally-produced content to the open editor buffer." Pull-locks the dispatch (reentrant-safe), applies the change as a minimal common-prefix/suffix diff via `editor.applyExternalContent` so the cursor maps through the edit, clears `dirty` (an applied external change is not a user edit), and skips the dispatch on identical content. `skipWhenDirty: true` is the conservative policy — unsaved keystrokes are strictly newer than anything the disk can offer, so the buffer wins and the next autosave reasserts it. Callers: Local Sync's watcher reload + foreground refresh, and multi-window's sibling-broadcast receiver.
- **`echo-ring.js`** — bounded "recently seen" rings for own-write echo suppression (`createRing` / `createKeyedRing`), plus `sha256Hex`. Echo detection is by *identity* (a content hash), never by timestamp — iCloud's bird daemon replays events seconds late.
- **`pdf-sync.js`** — the PDF registry (metadata only; binaries are a per-device cache re-downloaded from Zotero in the background, with the batch progress ring and startup resume).
- **`sync-feedback.js`** — the corner "Synced" toast plus the persistent activity log (`settings.syncLog`, shown in Settings > Sync > Log; error rows carry the `[!]` prefix and paint red).
- **`notebook-sync.js`** — `.hushnote` zip pack/unpack, the sole wire format for notebook envelopes (export, import, Local Sync notebooks).
- **`local-sync.js`** — see Local Sync below.

The external-store mutation hooks on `AppState` (`syncFileToExternal`, `syncCreateNode`, `syncRenameNode`, `syncDeleteNode`, `syncCreateFile`, `syncProjectOrdering`, `reconcileSync`) are intentionally kept as no-ops: every mutation path in the app already reports through them, and the desk-folder write-through (Phase 1 of the Local Desks plan) re-attaches there rather than re-plumbing every call site.

### Local Sync (`sync/local-sync.js`, Rust `local_sync.rs` watchers)

Direct-filesystem folder mounting on desktop **and iPad**. The sidebar's footer **Add (+)** popover carries a **Local Folder** entry (`add-popup.js`) that opens the OS folder picker via `addLocalSyncFolder(activeDeskId)` in `sync/local-sync.js` and registers the result. Each mounted folder appears in the section beneath the main file tree, rendered with a plain outline-square icon (`typeIcons.localSync`) distinct from regular folders. The hamburger menu on each mounted-folder row (built in `files-panel-local-sync.js`) surfaces **View in Finder** / **View in Files** (label depends on platform; both call `opener.revealItemInDir(folder.path)`) and an **Unlink Folder** entry. Persistence runs through Rust: `local_sync_add` writes `settings.json` directly, so the JS side only mirrors the returned folder into `state.settings.localSyncFolders` and emits `local-sync-changed` — sending the full JS `state.settings` back through `save_settings` would risk clobbering fields Rust touched since startup (sync log entries, freshly-persisted desk meta, etc.). Each mount carries an optional `desk_id` so it appears only under the desk that was active when it was added; `local_sync_add` takes the desk id as an optional command parameter and stamps it on the `LocalSyncFolder` struct.

Local Sync folders are **outside the internal version-control system**: their contents are not copied into `{data_dir}/files/`, they do not receive `fileId`s in the SQLite snapshot DB, and `snapshots.rs` is never consulted for them. The tree node type for a Local Sync root is `local-sync`; children are resolved lazily from disk on expand. On desktop, reads and writes go through the Rust `local_sync_*` commands using the stored absolute path; on iOS they route through the icloud-folder plugin (see below).

**Import layer (`sidebar/files-panel-local-import.js`).** The single "bring an on-disk entry into the VC store" implementation, shared by drag-and-drop (move semantics — `moveLocalToVc` / `moveLocalDirToVc` in `files-panel-local-sync-dnd.js`) and the row menu's **Copy to Hush** (copy semantics — destination picked in a modal reusing the send-to-desk CSS, listing the active desk's Inbox + folders + projects as an indented tree). `importLocalFileToVc` routes by extension (doc / notebook / stack / image → `newFile` / `createNotebook` / `createStack` / `createImageFromFile`); `importLocalDirToVc` recurses a directory into a folder — or a nested **project** when the destination container is a project, since projects can't hold folders. Move semantics delete each imported file individually and then remove the directory only via `local_sync_delete_dir_if_clean` (Rust; iOS mirrors it in JS over the plugin's unfiltered `list_dir`), which refuses unless nothing but `.DS_Store` junk remains — the sidebar listing filters unsupported extensions and dotfiles (`.git`, PDFs, …), so a blind recursive delete after a folder move would destroy files the import never saw. If the moved entry (or a file inside a moved folder) was open, the editor re-points to the imported VC copy; a dirty buffer is flushed to disk first so the import reads the latest content.

**Local multi-select (`sidebar/files-panel-local-multi-select.js`).** Selection keyed by row id (`folderId:relPath`), file rows only — intentionally separate from `state.selectedDocIds`, which is fileId-based (Local Sync files have none) and feeds the fileId-shaped multi-select editor view. Shift-click ranges over the *visible* DOM order of `.local-sync-file` rows, cmd/ctrl-click toggles, Esc or a plain click clears, and the two selection systems clear each other at the click sites. Dragging a selected row resolves the full batch (`resolveLocalDragBatch` — dragged entry first, ghost reads "N items"): a VC drop imports every entry, a local-dir drop moves each same-mount entry on disk (cross-mount entries are skipped — `local_sync_move` is same-mount). The `.multi-selected` class is re-stamped after every section render so selection survives rebuilds.

**Drag auto-scroll (`sidebar/drag-autoscroll.js`).** Both drag pipelines (the SortableList's and the local rows' own pointer pipeline) feed `updateDragAutoScroll(clientY)` on every move; a rAF loop scrolls `.panel-overlay-body` while the pointer holds inside a 48 px edge zone (speed ramps to 14 px/frame at the very edge), so a drag that started deep in a long folder can travel back above the fold. `stopDragAutoScroll()` runs on every gesture end.

**iOS / iCloud (`tauri-plugin-icloud-folder`).** iOS apps are sandboxed, so an arbitrary iCloud Drive folder can only be reached through a security-scoped URL handed back by `UIDocumentPickerViewController` — and that access dies on relaunch unless a *security-scoped bookmark* is persisted and re-resolved. Stock Tauri does neither (no folder picker on iOS, no bookmark persistence), so Hush ships a small custom Swift plugin under `src-tauri/tauri-plugin-icloud-folder/` (mirroring `tauri-plugin-pencil`). It exposes `pick_folder` (folder picker → `bookmarkData()` → `{ path, bookmark }`), `resolve_bookmark` (re-acquire access on a later launch, with a `stale` flag), `stop_access`, and coordinated (`NSFileCoordinator` + `startDownloadingUbiquitousItem`) `list_dir` / `read_file` / `write_file` / `read_file_bytes` / `write_file_bytes` — binary payloads cross the bridge as base64. `LocalSyncFolder` gained an optional `bookmark: Option<String>`; `local_sync_add` stores it and, when present, skips the `std::fs` `is_dir` check, and the startup watcher re-arm skips bookmarked mounts (the `notify` watcher is macOS-only).
